description: "Dependency-ordered implementation tasks for Real Files and Tabs"

# Tasks: Real Files and Tabs

**Input**: `spec.md`, `plan.md`, `research.md`, `data-model.md`, `quickstart.md`, all four files in
`contracts/`, `.specify/memory/constitution.md`, current source/tests, and the primary-source fact checks below.
Screenshots of the Mockup for visual validation: `specs/003-real-files-and-tabs/surface`.

**Parent branch**: `feature/v1-implementation`. Every task uses one `feature/v1-implementation--003-tNNN-*`
branch, one Conventional Commit, its named evidence, and a squash merge back to the parent. Do not create a
second parent branch and do not commit on `master`.

**Scope rule**: Tests are mandatory because the specification makes scenarios and evidence mandatory. Within each
task, write the named test first, observe the relevant red failure, implement only the bounded outcome, then run
the named green evidence and the task-appropriate gate. Generated `frontend/wailsjs/` and `internal/db/store/`
files are generator-owned and are never hand-edited.

**Read-only boundary**: Do not modify `docs/delivery/`. Its binding mockup may be served as an immutable reference.
No task may weaken a test, locator, threshold, retry, mask, reference, architecture rule, hook, or quality config.

**Numeric boundary rule**: Every size threshold in this feature is a **binary mebibyte with an exact inclusive
boundary**. Wherever a task says a size, it means the exact byte count: **2 MiB = 2,097,152**,
**10 MiB = 10,485,760**, **50 MiB = 52,428,800**, classification read cap **52,428,801**. A fixture, assertion, or
evidence line written against decimal "10 MB" (10,000,000) is wrong and fails the task.

**Mock-bridge rule**: `npm --prefix frontend run verify:ui` resolves to `playwright test`, which runs against the
deterministic dev bridge mock. It proves control wiring, accessibility, layout, and projection contracts. It
**never** proves disk bytes, permissions, native dialogs, atomic replacement, or wall-clock timing. Any criterion
about those is measured on a fresh `just build` binary (constitution VII).

## Fact-checked implementation constraints

The current manifests confirm Go 1.25.7, Wails 2.12.0, `modernc.org/sqlite` 1.54.0, TypeScript 5.8.3,
React 19.1.1, Redux Toolkit 2.12.0, Monaco 0.52.2, Jest 30.0.5, and Playwright 1.61.1. The task design also
uses these primary-source checks:

- Go documents that `os.Rename` is not atomic on non-Unix platforms even within one directory, so T012 retains
  a build-tagged Windows replace-existing port instead of treating plain `os.Rename` as cross-platform proof:
  <https://pkg.go.dev/os#Rename>.
- Microsoft documents `ReplaceFileW` as a replace-existing operation with same-volume, access, attribute, ACL,
  and partial-failure conditions; T012 therefore requires target-host error and preservation tests rather than a
  documentation-only claim: <https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-replacefilew>.
- Apple documents that APFS can be case-sensitive; T005 must not globally lowercase macOS paths:
  <https://developer.apple.com/documentation/technologyoverviews/files-and-directories>.
- Wails 2.12 documents that `OnBeforeClose` runs for both native close and `runtime.Quit`, and `true` vetoes;
  T027 implements the required two-stage one-shot protocol:
  <https://wails.io/docs/v2.12.0/reference/options/>.
- Wails `EventsEmit` returns no browser-application acknowledgement; T013 treats projection convergence as an
  explicit revision barrier and proves event-loss recovery:
  <https://wails.io/docs/v2.12.0/reference/runtime/events/>.
- Monaco exposes model identity/URI, undo state, creation, and disposal APIs; T017 still proves the pinned 0.52.2
  behavior empirically rather than assuming disposal erases a cached session:
  <https://microsoft.github.io/monaco-editor/typedoc/interfaces/editor_editor_api.editor.ITextModel.html>.
- Playwright supports region screenshots, but its snapshot comparison permits a nonzero color threshold by
  default; T034–T035 use a pinned decoded-PNG comparator with explicit zero tolerance and retained triplets:
  <https://playwright.dev/docs/screenshots> and
  <https://playwright.dev/docs/api/class-snapshotassertions>.
- Wails 2.12's runtime package documents `ClipboardSetText(ctx context.Context, text string) error`; T016 wires
  Copy path through this injected call rather than a frontend-side clipboard API, and Reveal in file manager has
  no Wails built-in and is implemented as a composition-root OS command port instead:
  <https://pkg.go.dev/github.com/wailsapp/wails/v2/pkg/runtime>.
- Go's `time` package documents that a `Time` returned by `time.Now` carries a monotonic clock reading and that
  `t.Sub(u)` uses it when both operands have one; SC-FT-007's final-input-to-commit duration in T024 is therefore
  measured with monotonic subtraction rather than wall-clock arithmetic:
  <https://pkg.go.dev/time#hdr-Monotonic_Clocks>.

isolated test fixtures. No Feature 003 behavior task may begin before T002 passes.

  - **Branch/commit**: `feature/v1-implementation--003-t001-baseline-routing`; `fix(evidence): route Spec Kit baselines into feature specs`.

- [x] T002 Capture the trustworthy pre-edit baseline with `just baseline 003-real-files-and-tabs` under `specs/003-real-files-and-tabs/evidence/baseline/` and inspect `baseline.md`, `baseline.exit`, every `baseline.logs/*.log`, and every `baseline.logs/*.code` before allowing implementation.
  - **Outcome**: One immutable baseline records every gate exit, raw log, finding set, failing test, coverage value, commit, and dirty-state provenance; any `UNRELIABLE` verdict stops the feature.
  - **Prerequisites**: T001.
  - **Ownership**: Evidence foundation only; no FR/SC ownership.
  - **Tests/evidence**: `just baseline 003-real-files-and-tabs`; `just archtest` must be green outright; record an explicit reviewed-baseline note in `specs/003-real-files-and-tabs/evidence/baseline/review.md` without abridging raw output.
  - **Branch/commit**: `feature/v1-implementation--003-t002-baseline-capture`; `test(evidence): capture Feature 003 baseline`.

- [x] T003 [P] Add deterministic real-file fixture builders in `internal/file/document_fixtures_test.go` and `frontend/e2e/helpers/real-file-fixtures.ts` without committing giant or destructive fixtures. The builders MUST produce, by exact byte count and named scenario:
  - **Line endings**: uniform LF; uniform CRLF; mixed LF/CRLF with a clear dominant ending; mixed LF/CRLF at an exact tie (first-encountered ending wins); **`none` (valid UTF-8 with no LF and no CRLF)**; **lone ASCII CR (0x0D with no LF)**; and a file containing **NEL (U+0085), LINE SEPARATOR (U+2028), and PARAGRAPH SEPARATOR (U+2029)** as ordinary content.
  - **Encoding/safety**: UTF-8 with byte-order mark; UTF-8 without byte-order mark; invalid UTF-8; NUL-bearing bytes.
  - **Permissions**: non-default permission modes (at least one mode that is not the process default).
  - **Sizes, exact and inclusive**: **2,097,152**, **2,097,153**, **10,485,760**, **10,485,761**, **52,428,800**, and **52,428,801** bytes. Generate them at test time into a temporary directory; never commit them.
  - **Path shapes**: identical basenames in different directories; identical basenames whose immediate parent names are also identical (requiring a two-or-more-segment suffix); symlink aliases; and **hostile names containing C0 control characters, DEL (U+007F), and bidirectional formatting controls (U+202A–U+202E, U+2066–U+2069)**.
  - **Outcome**: All later slices share byte-exact, temporary, cleanup-safe inputs and can identify every generated file by scenario. No fixture is described in decimal MB.
  - **Prerequisites**: T002; may run in parallel only with read-only task preparation, not behavior-bearing work.
  - **Ownership**: Test infrastructure only; no FR/SC ownership.
  - **Tests/evidence**: `go test -race ./internal/file -run TestDocumentFixtureBytesAndBounds` asserting each size fixture's exact `Stat().Size()`; `npm --prefix frontend test -- --runInBand` with named test `real file fixture metadata is deterministic`.
  - **Branch/commit**: `feature/v1-implementation--003-t003-file-fixtures`; `test(files): add deterministic lifecycle fixtures`.

## Phase 2: FT-VS-01 — New/Open and bounded document ingestion

**Goal**: Establish a zero-safe backend-authoritative ordered document model and deliver canonical New/Open with
real local-file classification, exact MiB boundaries, and the process-local identity reservation. This slice owns
FR-FT-001–007, FR-FT-028, and FR-FT-038 exactly once.

**Independent test**: Through the real bridge and native picker, cancel Open; create New; open LF/CRLF/BOM/`none`/
lone-CR, unsafe, 10,485,761-byte and 52,428,801-byte inputs; focus a duplicate alias; replace only an unchanged
empty untitled tab; race two Open requests for the same path; and refuse a 41st distinct document without partial
projection or recent-file state.

The task MUST implement both named contracts:
  - **Two-phase ordering**: native Open selection completes with **no document or recent-file mutation**; the backend then prepares canonical identity, duplicate, capacity and reservation outcomes **without activating a document** (New prepares only revision and capacity); the frontend flushes and awaits the outgoing identity-bound content and view state; the backend then **revalidates the expected tab-set revision and the reservation** and applies **exactly one** authoritative transition. Cancellation, failed flush, stale revision, lost reservation, classification failure, or refusal leaves active identity, ordered tabs, documents, recents, and active buffer unchanged.
  - **Process-local canonical identity reservation**: Open, recent-file, reopen and Save As **hold a reservation for the prepared canonical identity until activation, commit, cancellation, or failure**. A novel prepared Open **reserves both its canonical identity and one document slot**, and **pending novel reservations count toward the 40-document limit**. Canonicalization and deduplication happen **before** the limit is applied, so focusing an already-open identity stays valid at capacity and consumes no slot. **Concurrent requests resolving to the same open or pending identity join one authoritative outcome** rather than reserving another slot or creating another document. No application-wide or persistent file lock is added.
  - **Outcome**: Open inserts or focuses exactly one backend document and acknowledgement, never replaces nonempty untitled work, never restores a session, and uses the existing KV store without a migration or persisted source.

## Phase 3: FT-VS-02 — Atomic Save, Save As, and committed projection convergence

**Goal**: Deliver one awaited, revision-safe write path with cross-platform atomic replacement and no lost last
keystroke. This slice owns FR-FT-008–016 exactly once.

**Independent test**: Immediately save LF/CRLF/BOM/`none`/mode fixtures after typing; exercise mixed normalization,
suffix rules, cancellation, collisions, target drift, reservation release, every injected pre-commit failure, newer
edits during write, the full status-precedence table, and committed projection loss with its bounded retry schedule
while inspecting bytes and modes.

- [x] T010 [US1] Replace separate/fire-and-forget content and view drains with one identity-and-activation-token-bound imperative lifecycle barrier in `frontend/src/logic/hooks/useSyncedBuffer.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/hooks/useSyncedBuffer.test.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/hooks/useLifecycleBarrier.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/hooks/useLifecycleBarrier.test.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/store/docViewCommands.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/store/docViewCommands.test.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/adapter/appModelAdapter.ts`, and `frontend/src/logic/adapter/appModelAdapter.test.ts`.
  - **Outcome**: Save/switch/close/reload/hide either await newest content and complete view together for the captured activation or abort without an identity transition.
  - **Prerequisites**: T009.

  - **Branch/commit**: `feature/v1-implementation--003-t010-lifecycle-barrier`; `feat(editor): add awaited lifecycle barrier`.

- [x] T013 [US1] Implement the per-document write coordinator, immutable revision snapshots, disk-baseline dirty truth, the five-value save-status projection, committed revision results, deduplicated failures, and the bounded projection rehydrate/recovery sequence in `internal/appmodel/write_coordinator.go`, `internal/appmodel/write_coordinator_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/save_status.go`, `internal/appmodel/save_status_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/resync_recovery.go`, `internal/appmodel/resync_recovery_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/runtime_emitter.go`, `internal/appmodel/runtime_emitter_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/service.go`, `internal/appmodel/service_test.go`, `internal/apperr/results.go`, `internal/apperr/results_test.go`, `frontend/src/logic/adapter/appModelAdapter.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/adapter/appModelAdapter.test.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/store/appModelProjection.ts`, and `frontend/src/logic/store/appModelProjection.test.ts`. Two named contracts are mandatory:
  - **Status precedence and baseline origin**: Go authoritatively projects exactly one status using this precedence — read-only capability wins and yields `read-only`; an empty untitled document is `not-saved`; dirty, detached, nonempty untitled, failed write, or a revision newer than an in-flight/committed snapshot is `unsaved-changes`; clean after Open, Reload, explicit Save, or Save As is `saved`; clean after autosave is `autosaved`. **Each committed baseline retains its originating kind** (`open`, `reload`, `explicit-save`, `save-as`, `autosave`) so returning exactly to that baseline restores the correct clean label. A successful stale-revision write updates disk truth but MUST NOT project a clean status for newer content, and a failed write MUST NOT change status.
  - **Bounded recovery**: rehydration runs **immediately, then retries after 250 ms and after one second**. If all three attempts fail, document commands and normal close stay blocked and a **persistent recovery surface** states that the file **was saved on disk** but editor-state recovery failed. **Retry restarts the same bounded sequence.** `Quit and discard newer unsaved changes` requires a **second confirmation naming the affected documents** and MUST NOT alter or replay any committed write.
  - **Outcome**: A pre-commit failure preserves disk/model truth; a committed write records its exact baseline, never retries/rolls back on event loss, pauses later document commands, and rehydrates until its revision is projected; newer edits remain dirty.
  - **Prerequisites**: T010, T012.
  - **Primary ownership**: FR-FT-014 and FR-FT-016; closes CP-03 and CP-06 plus EC-10 and EC-25.
  - **Tests/evidence**: `go test -race ./internal/appmodel -run 'TestWriteCoordinator|TestSaveStatusPrecedenceTable|TestBaselineOriginRestoresCleanLabel|TestFailedWriteLeavesStatusUnchanged|TestStaleRevisionWriteDoesNotProjectClean|TestCommittedWriteProjectionFailure|TestResyncRetriesAt250msAndOneSecond|TestResyncExhaustionShowsPersistentRecoverySurface|TestQuitAndDiscardRequiresSecondConfirmation|TestNewerEditRemainsDirty'`; `npm --prefix frontend test -- --runInBand` with named tests `committed result rehydrates without duplicate Save` and `dev bridge dirty state follows disk baseline`; `just archtest`.

## Phase 5: FT-VS-04 — External-change recovery and revision-bound authorization

**Goal**: Prevent silent overwrite after another process changes or deletes a file, with foreground-only checks and
a serialized conflict queue. This slice owns FR-FT-020–023 exactly once.

**Independent test**: Change, replace, and delete the backing file; prove the metadata-equal stable-re-read resume
and the unstable-re-read refusal; exercise Reload, one-use Keep mine, Skip, edit-while-open invalidation, second
disk change, read-only Reload-only behavior, stale acknowledgement, Save As post-confirmation drift, and **two
simultaneous conflicts resolving one modal at a time with the waiting tab visibly blocked**.

- [x] T019 [US4] Implement pre-write disk-version decisions, the stable re-read contract, foreground-only checks, the serialized conflict queue, detached/recreatable documents, bounded 12-line/4,096-byte-per-side transient previews, Reload, single-use Keep-mine authorization, Skip, and every invalidator in `internal/appmodel/conflict.go`, `internal/appmodel/conflict_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/conflict_queue.go`, `internal/appmodel/conflict_queue_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/write_coordinator.go`, `internal/appmodel/handler.go`, `internal/appmodel/handler_test.go`, `internal/file/document_reader.go`, and `internal/apperr/results.go`. Three named contracts are mandatory:
  - **Stable re-read**: a version mismatch suspends the write and performs a re-read **whose version is unchanged across classification** and whose raw-byte hash is captured with the baseline. If raw bytes, byte-order mark, line endings, and permission mode **still equal the recorded baseline**, refresh **only the disk version** and resume the suspended write. Any byte or file-characteristic difference prevents the write and opens the external-change decision. **An unstable re-read writes nothing** and retries only after a fresh foreground check. Stable Open and Reload reads likewise capture version before reading and require the same version after raw-byte classification and hashing.
  - **Foreground-only checks**: run stable version checks on **tab activation, window focus or resume, and before every write**, for path-backed documents **including read-only ones**. Introduce **no background file watcher and no polling timer**.
  - **Serialized conflict queue**: bind every conflict to document identity, canonical content revision, and detected disk version; present **one modal at a time**; **retain the current modal until it resolves or invalidates**; **visibly project every waiting document as blocked by conflict**; and select subsequent eligible decisions in **authoritative tab order**.
  - **Outcome**: No mismatch writes; Reload reclassifies atomically; Keep mine authorizes only the compared revision/version once; Skip changes nothing beyond cancelling one request; missing backing files retain path/source as dirty detached documents; simultaneous conflicts never race or stack modals.
  - **Prerequisites**: T013, T017.
  - **Primary ownership**: FR-FT-020, FR-FT-022, and FR-FT-023; closes EC-15, EC-16, EC-17 and EC-18.
  - **Tests/evidence**: `go test -race ./internal/appmodel -run 'TestExternalConflictDecision|TestStableRereadMetadataEqualResumesWrite|TestUnstableRereadWritesNothing|TestForegroundChecksOnActivationFocusAndWrite|TestNoWatcherOrPollingTimerIsRegistered|TestConflictQueueOneModalInTabOrder|TestWaitingDocumentsProjectBlockedByConflict|TestKeepMineAuthorizationInvalidation|TestSkipCancelsOneWrite|TestMissingBackingFileDetaches'`; include second-process/same/changed/deleted/read-only/second-change tables.
  - **Branch/commit**: `feature/v1-implementation--003-t019-conflict-state`; `feat(files): add revision-bound external recovery`.

- [x] T020 [US4] Add one shared accessible modal and the exact external-change comparison flow in `frontend/src/ui/primitives/ModalShell.tsx`, `frontend/src/ui/primitives/ModalShell.module.css`, `frontend/src/ui/primitives/ModalShell.test.tsx`, `frontend/src/ui/widgets/ExternalChangePrompt.tsx`, `frontend/src/ui/widgets/ExternalChangePrompt.test.tsx`, `frontend/src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.tsx`, `frontend/src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.test.tsx`, `frontend/src/logic/adapter/services.ts`, `frontend/src/logic/adapter/services.test.ts`, `frontend/src/dev/bridge-mock/go/appmodel/AppModelHandler.ts`, `frontend/src/dev/bridge-mock/appModel.test.ts`, `frontend/src/i18n/locales/en.json`, and `frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts`. A **metadata-only** difference shows the differing byte-order mark, line endings or permission mode instead of an empty content comparison. A **read-only** conflict offers `Reload from disk` plus structural `Cancel` only, with **Cancel initially focused**, and offers neither Keep mine nor Skip. **Tabs whose documents are waiting in the conflict queue render the blocked-by-conflict state.**

    for this criterion.
  - **t₀ and t₁ are single-clock Go monotonic stamps**, so the working-copy synchronization leg cannot fall outside
    the measured interval. **t₀** = the instant the harness receives the webview's acknowledgement, through
    `nativeEvidenceRuntime`, that the final input event's handler has returned for that trial's last character.
    **t₁** = the instant the appmodel write coordinator reports that atomic replacement committed **that exact
    content revision**. Both are read with `time.Now()` in the one Go process and subtracted monotonically (see the
    `pkg.go.dev/time#hdr-Monotonic_Clocks` fact check above). The interval therefore contains the 200 ms buffer
    synchronization seam, the one-second debounce, and the write itself by construction. Measuring from
    accept-content to commit — which would exclude synchronization — fails this task.
  - **Build parity and its stated limit.** Build the harness binary from the same commit and the same release
    settings as `just build`; the only permitted difference is the added scenario driver and its build tag. The
    write path (`internal/appmodel`, `internal/file`) MUST be byte-identical to the release build, with no timer,
    port, or coordinator substitution. Record the build-tag delta as a named limitation in the evidence — this
    harness is **not** the `just build` artifact, and the evidence MUST say so rather than imply it. T039
    independently spot-checks a sample of autosaves on the true `just build` binary to confirm the distribution is
    not a driver artifact; a disagreement between the two reopens this task.
  - Run **20 uncounted warmups (5 per size)** and **exactly 100 measured trials (25 per size)** for accepted revisions of exactly **1 KiB (1,024 bytes)**, **256 KiB (262,144 bytes)**, **1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes)**, and **2 MiB (2,097,152 bytes)**.
  - Each trial replaces **one character** in a clean, path-backed, writable document with autosave **on**, local temporary storage, and **no conflict**.
  - Timing is **monotonic**, starts when the **final input event completes**, and stops when **atomic replacement commits that content revision** — including working-copy synchronization and the **one-second debounce**.
  - **No retry, no outlier removal, no discarded failure.** A failed eligible write is a counted miss.
  - **At least 95 of the 100 measurements MUST be at or below 5,000 ms.**
  - Retain **all** durations, sizes, revision/commit identities, disk-byte results, **p50/p95/max**, the warmup record, and host/OS/filesystem/build metadata.
  - Successful autosaves MUST produce **zero toasts**; an overlapping explicit Save MUST produce **exactly one** success notification.
  - **Outcome**: The acknowledged setting and in-flight/dirty status are truthful at all widths; automatic success is silent; and SC-FT-007 is proven by a real-binary, real-disk, monotonic measurement rather than mock or fake-clock timing.
  - **Prerequisites**: T023.
  - **Primary ownership**: SC-FT-007; supporting proof for FR-FT-017–019; closes EC-36.
  - **Tests/evidence**: `npm --prefix frontend test -- --runInBand` with named suites `autosave setting is acknowledged before it applies`, `autosave success emits no toast`, and `dirty dot is muted only while a write is in flight`; `npm --prefix frontend run verify:ui -- e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts --grep 'FT-VS-05'` for control/status wiring only; for the criterion, `just build` (parity reference) then `go run -tags native_evidence ./cmd/native-evidence -scenario autosave-latency` with named tests `TestAutosaveLatencyScenarioUsesSystemTimer` and `TestAutosaveLatencyStampsSpanSynchronizationAndDebounce`; retain the full 100-row sample table, warmup table, percentile calculation, host metadata, and the stated build-tag limitation in `specs/003-real-files-and-tabs/evidence/ft-vs-05/`.
  - **Branch/commit**: `feature/v1-implementation--003-t024-autosave-ui-proof`; `feat(files): expose and prove autosave`.

## Phase 7: FT-VS-06 — Transactional close plans and native shutdown

**Goal**: Account for all unsaved work before removing tabs or quitting. This slice owns FR-FT-024–027 exactly
once.

**Independent test**: Exercise clean/dirty single and multi-target closes, Save/Discard/Cancel, Save all ordering,
untitled paths, normalization/conflicts, first failure, stale plans, in-flight autosave, final zero state, repeated
native close, one-shot Quit, drain failure, shutdown ordering, and the twice-confirmed recovery quit.

A close requested while a dirty tab has an autosave scheduled or in flight MUST first flush the latest working copy and wait for the write coordinator, then **re-evaluate the committed/current revision**: a now-clean tab may close without a dirty prompt, while a newer or failed revision stays open for an explicit choice. Queued close-plan normalizations MUST resolve **one at a time in authoritative tab order** before any batch write.

## Phase 8: FT-VS-07 — Reopen, recents, launcher, actions, identity, and status

**Goal**: Complete daily file/tab entry surfaces without activating deferred workspace behavior, with transactional
cross-instance recents. This slice owns FR-FT-034 and FR-FT-039–044 exactly once.

**Independent test**: Verify six persisted MRU paths and lazy pruning, **two instances promoting concurrently**,
40 unique recently closed entries, newest-first reopen/focus/missing/retry/fresh identity/current disk, final-tab
launcher, shortcut collisions including Move tab and Table, clean restart, and accessible responsive status details.

- [x] T029 [US5] Implement the six-entry persisted recent-file repository and 40-entry in-memory recently-closed history in `internal/appmodel/recent_files.go`, `internal/appmodel/recent_files_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/recent_files_repository.go`, `internal/appmodel/recent_files_repository_sqlite.go`, `internal/appmodel/recent_files_repository_sqlite_test.go`, `internal/appmodel/tab_session.go`, `internal/appmodel/tab_session_test.go`, and `internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle.go`. The promotion contract is exact:
  - Every successful canonical **Open/focus, explicit Save, or Save As** promotes its path in **one SQLite transaction against the latest committed list**: remove canonical duplicates, prepend the path, truncate to six, commit. **Autosave and Reload MUST NOT change recency.**
  - Across independent instances, **SQLite commit order defines global recency**. `last writer wins` means the **latest transactional promotion**, never replacement by a stale whole-list snapshot. Two instances promoting concurrently each read and update the latest committed list inside their own transaction.
  - A promotion persistence failure (busy timeout or other metadata failure) **MUST NOT roll back an already successful Open or committed document write**, MUST leave the last committed list authoritative, and MUST produce exactly **one classified `persistence-warning`** without claiming promotion success.
  - Display and explicit choice are the **only** validation points — never a background watcher or timer. Display transactionally reads the latest committed list; a missing entry found during display is removed silently through the same latest-value transaction; an explicit stale choice also shows a classified `not-found` error.
  - **Outcome**: Open/successful Save update deduplicated MRU transactionally; display/choice alone validates and prunes; close retains unique path+view but no untitled/source; reopen uses canonical Open, consumes success/missing, retains retryable failure, and reads current disk with a fresh identity.

## Dependencies and execution order

```text
T001 baseline routing -> T002 trustworthy baseline -> T003 fixtures
  -> FT-VS-01 (T004–T009)
  -> FT-VS-02 (T010–T015)
  -> FT-VS-03 (T016–T018)
  -> FT-VS-04 (T019–T021)
  -> FT-VS-05 (T022–T024)
  -> FT-VS-06 (T025–T028)
  -> FT-VS-07 (T029–T032)
  -> FT-VS-08 (T033–T037)
  -> FT-EV-09 (T038–T039)
```

- The completion/merge order above is strict. No behavior-bearing task may bypass an incomplete prior slice.
- T003 is the only marked parallel opportunity: isolated fixture preparation after the baseline. Within later
  tasks, subtests may run concurrently only when they do not mutate shared files/state; this does not relax task
  prerequisites or merge order.
- A task is not complete until its named test/evidence is inspected, task-appropriate architecture/generator gate
  passes, its bounded live UI check is performed when visible behavior changed, and its one commit is merged.
- A red gate caused twice by the same issue stops advancement and reports the exact raw evidence. An `UNRELIABLE`
  gate stops immediately.
- T024 and T032 run a `just build` binary inside their slice. That is deliberate: their criteria are disk-timing
  and cross-instance facts the mock bridge cannot establish. It does not move them into Phase 10.

## Parallel execution example

After T002 is confirmed reliable, T003 may be prepared independently while the orchestrator reviews T004's
existing zero-state contracts. T003 must merge before T005 consumes its fixtures. No FT-VS-02 work may start while
any FT-VS-01 task remains incomplete.

## Implementation strategy

### MVP first

1. Complete T001–T003.
2. Complete all of FT-VS-01 (T004–T009).
3. Complete all of FT-VS-02 (T010–T015).
4. Stop and independently demonstrate US1: open one existing supported file, edit, immediately Save, and inspect
   bytes/mode plus the failure-preservation path. This is the smallest trustworthy MVP.

### Incremental delivery

1. Add FT-VS-03 for identity-safe multi-document tabs with reorder and hostile-safe labels.
2. Add FT-VS-04 and FT-VS-05 for explicit external recovery and measured autosave.
3. Add FT-VS-06 for lossless close/shutdown.
4. Add FT-VS-07 for recents/reopen/launcher and completed canonical surfaces.
5. Finish FT-VS-08 exact conformance, then FT-EV-09 release evidence.

## Format validation

- Every implementation entry starts with `- [ ] TNNN`, uses `[P]` only for the one truly independent fixture
  task, and uses exactly one `[US1]`–`[US5]` label for story-bearing tasks.
- Every task states one bounded outcome, exact repository paths, prerequisite task IDs, named tests/evidence,
  and one task branch/Conventional Commit scope.
- FR-FT-001–057, SC-FT-001–013, CL-01–14, CP-01–11, EC-01–36, and every deferral above remain primary-owned
  exactly once.
- Every size threshold is written as an exact byte count, never as decimal MB.
- `docs/delivery/` stays read-only; generated bindings/database files are never hand-edited; `just package` is
  not run or claimed.

## Phase 11: Convergence — Session 2026-08-09 production parity decisions

**Purpose**: Implement the four approved decisions recorded in the 2026-08-09 clarification session and close the
gaps found by the read-only convergence review. This section is append-only so the historical T035–T039 task text and
its evidence history remain intact.

**Dependency note**: T040–T043 are prerequisite remediation for the still-open T035 matrix execution. They MUST be
implemented and verified before T035 is rerun, even though this append-only convergence phase follows the historical
FT-VS-08 task entries. No mockup, reference image, comparator tolerance, or mask may be changed to avoid the work.

**Clarification ownership addendum**: The four new decisions are CL-15 (production UI is the parity target), CL-16

## Phase 18: Convergence — minimum-window behaviour and rescoped parity contracts

Decisions taken 2026-08-13/14 in review. Each task below carries its decision inline so it
can be executed from a fresh context without replaying the conversation. Evidence for the
reasoning lives in `evidence/ft-vs-08/phase-18/`.

- [x] T076 **CRITICAL** Record the minimum-window clarification in `specs/003-real-files-and-tabs/spec.md` through the clarification workflow, per Constitution I and FR-FT-055 (`contradicts`). The approved behaviour at the 375x480 minimum window (`main.go:104-105`) diverges from the binding, which stacks both panes (`mockup.html:54` `.app[data-w="375"] .body{flex-direction:column}` with `.pane{flex:1}`) and keeps the sidebar present as a closed slide-over (`:51-53`). Approved instead: show exactly ONE pane matching the selected mode; Split collapses to the **editor**; the collapse is view-only and Split returns when the window is widened; the workspace panel is **not rendered at all** at that size; menus, tabs, toolbar and a shortened status bar stay; the Editor/Split/Preview switch is hidden and the mode is changed through the View menu. Record what this means for the 375 parity captures — the `editor-split` family cannot show two panes at that width — and whether those cases need a reviewed reference variant or a revised assignment. Do not implement T077-T080 until this is encoded.
- [x] T077 **CRITICAL** Stop rendering the workspace panel at the 375px minimum window in `frontend/src/ui/widgets/AppShell.tsx`, per Constitution VI (`contradicts`). Today `workspaceVisible` reads the raw preference (`AppShell.tsx:67`), the panel opens over the tab strip, and Playwright reports `<aside aria-label="Workspace"> intercepts pointer events` on the new-tab control — the `+` is unreachable on first sight. **Two implementations were tried and reverted; read `evidence/ft-vs-08/phase-18/narrow-workspace-default.md` before starting.** Overriding the projected value in `AppShell` splits the source of truth, because `App.tsx` passes the raw preference to `ShellMenuRow` for the toggle and the two then disagree (measured: `editor-stage` 108 -> 90 passing). Dispatching `setWorkspaceVisible(false)` on mount is architecturally clean but `SidebarVisible` is persisted and restored (`internal/appmodel/service.go:398`), so one narrow launch would hide the workspace on every later wide one. Not rendering it at that width avoids both: there is no "is it open" state to own at that size, and the stored preference continues to govern wide layout untouched.
- [x] T078 [US5] Collapse the editor region to a single pane at the 375px minimum window in `frontend/src/ui/widgets/EditorView.tsx` and `frontend/src/ui/widgets/AppShell.tsx`, per FR-FT-051 and the T076 clarification (`missing`). Editor mode shows the editor, Preview mode shows the viewer, and **Split collapses to the editor** — chosen because this is a Markdown editor, typing is the primary job, and a fixed answer means nobody has to guess which pane they will get. No viewport-based pane collapse exists anywhere today. Assert the surviving pane fills the region and the hidden pane is removed rather than merely zero-width.
- [x] T079 [US5] Make the minimum-window collapse view-only in the same components, per Constitution III and the T076 clarification (`missing`). Widening the window past the minimum MUST restore Split without the user re-selecting it, and the collapse MUST NOT write the stored arrangement preference — shrinking a window is not a mode change and must not silently overwrite a choice. The backend keeps owning the preference; the collapse is presentation only. Prove both directions with an automated resize case, and prove the persisted value is unchanged after a shrink/widen cycle.
- [x] T080 [US5] Confirm the retained chrome inventory at the 375px minimum window against the T076 clarification in `frontend/src/ui/widgets/EditorChrome.module.css`, `StatusBar.module.css` and `ShellMenuRow.tsx` (`partial`). Approved to stay: the menu row, the tab strip, the toolbar with its overflow menu, and the status bar shortened in the binding's fixed drop order (`mockup.html:82-83`). Approved to go: the workspace panel (T077), the Assistant, and the Editor/Split/Preview switch. Verify the mode remains changeable through the View menu at that width, since the switch is hidden, and that every toolbar action stays reachable — the narrow overflow previously omitted Bold, Italic, Strikethrough, Inline code and all three headings entirely.
- [x] T081 [US5] Investigate each of the 26 remaining failing end-to-end cases individually before changing any test, per Constitution VII (`contradicts`): 22 in `frontend/e2e/window-shell.test.ts`, 3 in `frontend/e2e/core-editor.test.ts`, 1 in `frontend/e2e/appearance.test.ts`. For each, determine whether production or the test is wrong and record which, with the evidence. **This ordering is mandatory, not cautious:** in this phase a case dismissed as an out-of-date test turned out to be correctly reporting that Bold, Italic, Strikethrough, Inline code and all three headings were unreachable at the minimum window — a real defect that had been reported for weeks. Fix production where production is wrong; update the test only where the binding and the specification show it describes a superseded surface, and say so in the commit.
- [x] T082 [US5] Rescope T075 to the states the immutable mockup can actually express, per Constitution I and FR-FT-056 (`partial`). The six `status-*` IDs require six paired reference conditions and the binding contains exactly one, so five cannot exist — the criterion was unsatisfiable as written. Approved: compare only the two states the binding can express (`status-mixed-ending` via `.sb-eol`, `status-large-file` via `.sb-count`), prove the other four (`saved`, `autosaved`, `unsaved-changes`, `read-only`) with behaviour assertions on the authoritative `data-status-state` attribute and the title bar rather than pictures, and amend the manifest count honestly. Remove the forbidden production-only `comparisonAttempted: false` artifacts (two remain in `frontend/e2e/targeted-parity.test.ts`). `evidence/ft-vs-08/phase-18/t075-machinery.md` maps the six files involved and records two traps: `REFERENCE_ADAPTER_HASH` is hand-listed, and the reference server on port 4174 is reused across runs so it must be killed after every adapter edit or it serves a stale adaptation.
- [x] T083 [US5] Rescope T045 from 18 width/palette combinations to 3 representative ones at 1280px — one per colour family — per FR-FT-045 (`partial`). One combination is measured and fully attributed at 182 pixels; the other 17 would all carry the same Feature 002 editor font and scrolling difference, so they cannot pass either and add 17 runs for one signal. Three is enough to catch a palette-specific mistake. Record the reduction and the reason.

Cover each state once where it can genuinely look or behave differently, skip combinations that only repeat an existing check, and **log every skip with the test that already covers it** — a silent reduction reads as full coverage when it is not. These tasks were written without checking their cost and overlap heavily with tests that already exist.
- [x] T085 [US5] Add a shrink rule to the attributed-residual mechanism in `frontend/e2e/parity/attributed.ts`, per the 2026-08-13 clarification on what a parity check means (`missing`). Growth beyond `measuredPixels` already fails. Shrinking currently passes silently, which leaves the recorded ceiling too generous — a term recorded at 416 that now needs 100 leaves room for 316 pixels of new drift to hide inside it. Fail when a term drops well below its recorded size so the number gets tightened, with a tolerance band wide enough that a harmless renderer nudge does not break the build. Update the declared ceilings in `frontend/e2e/parity/attributed-residuals.ts` when it fires.

T076, T082, T083, T084 and T085 are all recorded as answered clarifications in

  `path-hostile-disambiguated`) — use a Feature 003 reference variant that hides the
  non-selected `.pane`, on the same FR-FT-056 footing as the File menu, View menu and toolbar.

  and **24 behaviour-verified** keys; three repetitions execute **1,566 pixel comparisons plus
  72 behaviour verifications = 1,638 verifications**. This supersedes the 2026-08-13
  six-variant answer.

- **Coverage sweeps (T084).** Each state once where it can genuinely differ; every skip logged

  ceiling gets tightened rather than quietly rotting.

Whole-screen comparison against the binding is **withdrawn**: the mockup depicts the product's
final state while this feature delivers a subset, so a whole screen contains differences no
production work can close. Two full runs passed 0 of 1,638; 240 of 510 keys differed in the
**position** of the mapped region, not merely its pixels; and the reference adapter had grown to
nine variants whose only purpose was deleting unbuilt functionality from the reference. The
measurement is `evidence/ft-vs-08/phase-18/t035-run-after-repair.md`.

- [X] T107 **CRITICAL — production defect** Carry the classified refusal message to the user on every entry path, per FR-FT-005, FR-FT-004 and the classified-error contract (contradicts). The specification requires the over-50-MiB refusal to name the 50 MiB limit and remediates `capacity-limit` "message-only, naming the limit". The backend writes both messages — `internal/file/document_reader.go:211` (`The document exceeds the 50 MiB limit.`) and `internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle.go:184` (`The window already contains 40 documents.`), each `ClassifiedCapacityLimit` with `RemediationCancel` — and both cross the bridge intact (`internal/apperr/results.go:506-513`, `frontend/wailsjs/go/models.ts:323-343`), preserved by the adapter (`frontend/src/logic/adapter/index.ts:184-200`). **Root cause located**: every entry handler read only its success field and never inspected `result.error` — `App.tsx:386-398` (New, reads `result.data`), `:399-411` (Open), `:412-424` (Open Recent) and `:426-438` (Reopen), while `DocumentTabs.tsx:583-586` discarded the `+` control's result outright with `void (onNewDocument(…))`. The save path had this right at `App.tsx:970-971`; the open path had no counterpart. Corroborating: `50 MiB`, `50 MB`, `52428800`, `exceeds` and `too large` each returned zero hits across `frontend/src`, and `capacity-limit` returned exactly one — the unused type-union member at `appModelTypes.ts:198`. **This is T104's defect class with the arrow reversed**: T104 was the frontend never calling the backend, this is the frontend receiving the backend's answer and throwing it away.
  - **Why nothing caught it**: the behavioural suites drive the seeded parity route, where nothing refuses, and the mock bridge implemented **no capacity check at all** (`AppModelHandler.ts` `NewDocument`/`OpenDocument` appended unconditionally), so no browser test could reach a refusal even in principle. The Go tests assert the model contract and stop at the boundary. Nothing in between asked whether the message arrived.
  - **Fix**: `frontend/src/logic/store/classifiedNotification.ts` (new) extracts `reportClassifiedError` from `DocumentTabs.tsx` and adds the missing `capacity-limit` arm to the code map. It dispatches `notifyToast`, which passes `error.message` through verbatim — **not** `notifyError`, whose `localizedErrorCopy` (`notificationsSlice.ts:48-58`) substitutes generic catalog copy keyed by code, and the catalog has no string naming either limit. All four `App.tsx` handlers and the `+` control now report.
  - **Tests/evidence**: four tests, each confirmed to fail before the fix — `FR-FT-004 surfaces the 40-document refusal raised by the new-tab control` (`DocumentTabs.test.tsx`), `FR-FT-005 reports the 50 MiB open refusal with the limit named` (`App.test.tsx`), and the two `FT-VS-09` cases in `frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts`. Full reproduction and the scope limit on what a Playwright case can prove: `evidence/ft-ev-09/sc-ft-002/capacity-refusals-2026-08-15.md`.

- [X] T109 **Closed 2026-08-15, and deliberately not left latent.** `ActionResult` gains a `refused` member carrying the backend's `ClassifiedError`, and `actionDispatcher.ts` gains the matching guard beside the existing `document-mismatch` and `unavailable` arms — every other backend status (`refused`, `conflict`, `cancelled`, `needs-normalization`) previously fell through to `return { status: 'mutated' }` with `invocation.error` never read. **The blast radius was as small as the task predicted**: `ActionResult` is imported by exactly one file (`EditorContextMenu.tsx:20`), there is no exhaustive `switch` anywhere, and the two runtime discriminators (`EditorView.tsx:266` `!== 'mutated'`, `shellActions.ts:124` `=== 'mutated'`) already behave correctly under a widened union — so nothing needed changing to keep compiling. **A widened union with no consumer would have been a test-only symbol**, proving arithmetic rather than behaviour, so one real consumer is wired: `ShellMenuRow` takes an optional `onActionResult` prop — following `EditorContextMenu`'s existing prop of that name rather than importing `logic/store`, which also means the row's tests still need no Provider — and `ApplicationShellMenu` reports a `refused` result through `reportClassifiedError`, passing the backend message verbatim. **Both File surfaces route through that one handler**: the click via `dispatchFileAction`, and the accelerator via a new optional `onResult` on `ShortcutAction`, so the click and the keystroke cannot disagree about whether a refusal says anything — the same invariant T110 established for dispatch itself. **Test**: `T109 reports a refused invocation as refused and carries its classified error` (`actionDispatcher.test.ts`), confirmed red before the fix, where it returned `mutated`. **Gates**: `just check` exit 0, 548 frontend tests / 77 suites; `just e2e-test` 259 passed, matching baseline. Original text follows. Stop `dispatchAction` reporting a refused command as a successful mutation, per the classified-error contract (partial). `frontend/src/logic/actions/actionDispatcher.ts:105-146` guards `document-mismatch` and `unavailable`, then falls through to `return { status: 'mutated', actionId, documentId }`. A `refused` outcome matches neither guard, so a refused Open or New is reported upward as a mutation that happened. **Latent today**, which is why it is filed rather than fixed alongside T107: every caller discards the result (`ShellMenuRow.tsx:424`, `ViewMenu.tsx:87`, `EditorChrome.tsx:207`, `TabContextMenu.tsx:146` all use `void dispatchAction(…)`) except `EditorView.tsx:262`, which checks `status !== 'mutated'` only for `refresh-preview` — a path no open refusal reaches. Fixing it means widening the shared `ActionResult` union, which touches six surfaces for no current behaviour change; do it deliberately, with a test that a refused invoke returns a non-`mutated` status.

- [X] T110 **Closed 2026-08-15.** All six advertised accelerators dispatch, verified on the rebuilt binary (built 13:27:50, process started 13:27:56 — newer than the binary, so not a stale instance): `⌘N` one tab to three, `⌘W` three to two then two to one, `⌘S` an explicit save reporting `Saved` with the edit on disk and no `.gomarkedit-*` temporary left, `⌘O` and `⌘⇧S` opening the real native dialogs; `⌥⇧⌘T` is advertised and correctly greyed with no reopenable file. **The root cause was confirmed by experiment before any code was written**, because the existing evidence for it was circular — `host-walkthrough-2026-08-15.md:99` argued keystrokes reach the application from the very `⌘S` observation T110 asks to re-examine. On the unfixed binary `⌘,` (already in `createShellActionCatalogue`) opened Settings while seven `⌘N` presses did nothing: same webview, same modifier, the only difference being whether anything listened. That retires the native-menu contingency and confines the defect to the frontend; `internal/application/native_menu.go` is untouched. **Fix**: `ShellMenuRow.tsx` now derives the accelerator text, the row's click and the keystroke from one table — `fileActionInvoker` and `fileActionDisabled` are hoisted above the hook call and shared, and the entries are built from `actionsForSurface('file-menu')`, so a row cannot advertise a shortcut without also dispatching it. `exit` self-excludes (no registry shortcut; native quit owns it) and deferred ids self-exclude through `fileActionDisabled`. `App.tsx` threads `onCloseDocument` into the menu subtree, which had no close callback at all. `useShellShortcuts.ts`, `shellActions.ts` and `actionRegistry.ts` are unchanged — the `ShortcutAction` branch already did what was needed. **Gates**: `just check` exit 0 against a baseline captured at `4e141037` (all gates parsed, 0 failing tests, 2 pre-existing findings); `just e2e-test` **259 passed, exit 0** — the 258 baseline plus exactly `FT-VS-10`, with no `T026` flake; frontend unit suite 540 passed. Full record in `evidence/ft-ev-09/accelerators-2026-08-15.md`. **One item is recorded unresolved rather than claimed**: `File ▸ Close Tab` by *click* still does not close on the host though `⌘W` does, while the same click passes in Chromium and at unit level — filed as T111, and not a regression, since the row was a silent no-op before this task. Dispatch the File-menu keyboard accelerators, or stop advertising them, per FR-FT-001, FR-FT-002 and FR-FT-013 (contradicts). The File menu renders `⌘N`, `⌘O`, `⌘S`, `⌘⇧S` and `⌘W` beside its items, and **no keyboard handler dispatches any of them**. Observed on `build/bin/GoMarkEdit.app` rebuilt from `790375fb`: 39 consecutive `⌘N` presses created no document, with the editor both focused and unfocused, while the `+` control and `File ▸ New File` both worked reliably in the same session using the same click mechanism.
  - **Root cause located.** Three keydown handlers exist and none owns the file actions. (1) `useShellShortcuts` (`logic/actions/useShellShortcuts.ts:26-50`) is the only global `window` listener, and `ShellMenuRow.tsx:343` feeds it exactly `createShellActionCatalogue(...)`, whose entire catalogue is six **shell** ids — `settings`, `view`, `about`, `fullscreen`, `keyboard-shortcuts`, `toggle-sidebar` (`logic/actions/shellActions.ts:50-100`). No file id is in it. (2) `EditorChrome.tsx:250-270` handles only formatting and deferred ids, and returns early unless focus is inside `[data-editor-surface]`. (3) `DocumentTabs.tsx:252-262` returns early for anything that is not `next-tab`/`previous-tab`. (4) The native menu cannot cover it either: `internal/application/native_menu.go:8-14` returns `menu.NewMenuFromItems(menu.AppMenu(), menu.EditMenu())` on darwin and `nil` elsewhere — there is **no native File menu and no accelerator registration**. The accelerator text is therefore purely decorative: `actionRegistry.ts:199` declares `shortcut: 'Mod+N'`, `formatShortcut` renders it `⌘N`, and nothing listens.
  - **Why nothing caught it.** `actionRegistry.test.ts:55` asserts `getAction('new-file').shortcut` is `'Mod+N'` — that the registry *declares* the binding, not that pressing it does anything. `useShellShortcuts.test.tsx` drives the hook with synthetic actions it constructs itself, so it proves the hook dispatches what it is given and never that the shell passes it the file actions. This is the self-asserting shape again: every assertion passes while the wiring between them is absent.
  - **Re-examine, do not assume**: T104's evidence reports an explicit `⌘S` reporting `Autosaved` rather than `Saved`, which was attributed to the autosave race. Under this finding `⌘S` may simply have done nothing, with the label reflecting the autosave that had already committed. Verify which it was before closing T104's second symptom.
  - **Tests/evidence**: a case per accelerator that dispatches the real key event at the window and asserts the action ran — failing first. Then decide per accelerator whether to wire it or remove the advertised text; leaving a menu advertising a shortcut that does nothing is the contradiction. Full record in `evidence/ft-ev-09/host-walkthrough-2026-08-15.md`.

- [X] T111 **Closed 2026-08-15. The reported symptom did not reproduce and the recorded prime suspect is refuted; a real defect in the same row was found on the host and fixed.** `File ▸ Close Tab` by click closed the tab in **6 of 6** attempts on the rebuilt binary (built 14:38:23, process started 14:38:46 — not a stale instance) across five states, including the "stable two-tab state" this task names: 3→2, 2→1, 2→1 with the active tab switched *by clicking the tab*, 2→1 with a real `fixture-a.md`, 1→0, and 2→1 at a ≈640px window — the last exercising the **narrow** render site (`ShellMenuRow.tsx:918`, a plain `<button onClick>` portalled to `document.body`) rather than the wide Radix `onSelect`. **The prime suspect below is false, by reading rather than by walking**: `ShellMenuRow.tsx:423-436` builds the ⌘W `dispatchContext` as `{applicationFocused, documentId, sessionDocumentId, writable}` and `:519-525` builds the click's as the same four fields plus `invoke`, both resolving `invoke` through the same `fileActionInvoker` from the same un-memoised `onCloseDocument` prop — so the two surfaces are byte-identical from `dispatchAction` down, a stale-revision refusal would have refused **both**, and ⌘W demonstrably worked. The mock also honours the revision faithfully (`AppModelHandler.ts:1250-1257`), so Chromium passing was never evidence the check was skipped. **The real defect**: with **zero documents** — the launcher, one `Close Tab` away from a one-tab state — `activeDocumentId` is null, `App.tsx:246` passes `onCloseDocument` as `undefined`, and `fileActionDisabled` never consulted `fileActionInvoker`, so the row rendered **fully enabled** beside a correctly-greyed `Save`/`Save As…` and its click died silently at the `invoke === undefined` return. **Fix**: `fileActionDisabled` now greys any row whose invoker is absent, via an explicit id set (`open-recent` is excluded — its rule is the recent count); the `exit`/`onQuit === undefined` clause is folded in, being the same rule spelled twice. The popup dismissal is hoisted above the guard so the narrow site cannot strand an open menu. `native_menu.go`, `useShellShortcuts.ts`, `shellActions.ts` and `actionRegistry.ts` are unchanged, and `close-tab`'s registry availability is deliberately left `available()` — the contextual case already lives in `getActionAvailability`'s `TAB_ACTIONS` arm, and deferring the entry would grey it permanently and break the File-popup parity contract. **The `preventDefault` coupling is safe and proved, not assumed**: `fileShortcutActions` filters on `invoke === undefined` *before* constructing the entry (`:426`), so a greyed row is always an unregistered id and can never swallow a keystroke that should fall through — pinned by a guard test. **Also fixed, T107's treatment third arrow**: the close plan's refusals were reported but **message-stripped**, because `notifyError.prepare` unconditionally substitutes `localizedErrorCopy` keyed by code and `conflict` has no catalog entry, so Go's two distinct refusals (`close_plan.go:44` and `:88`) both reached the user as "The file operation could not be completed." The three close-plan arms now use `reportClassifiedError`; `reportWriteError`'s other seven callers keep their copy contract. **Tests**: two Go tests covering `PrepareClose`'s previously untested staleness branches, asserting category, **exact message** and remediation (characterization, not red-first — both branches already worked; they pin the literals the frontend now renders verbatim); and three frontend tests, two confirmed red before the fix. **A2 host walk on the rebuilt binary** (14:54:10 / 14:54:16) confirms the row greys at zero documents, `Exit` stays enabled, and the row re-enables and still closes with a document open. **Gates**: `just check` exit 0 (547 frontend tests, every Go package ok) diffed against a `258d1d32` baseline with no new findings; `just e2e-test` **259 passed, exit 0** — exactly the baseline, no `T026` flake, parity accounting 150/150. **B4 (a bounded retry on a conflicted close plan) was deliberately not built**: no stale-revision refusal was observed in six closes, and the plan made it conditional on observing one. Full record in `evidence/ft-ev-09/close-tab-click-2026-08-15.md`. Original text follows. Close the document when the File menu's `Close Tab` row is clicked on the real binary, per FR-FT-013 (partial). `⌘W` closes a tab reliably on the packaged binary; the **menu row's click does not**, observed twice from a stable two-tab state with the click verified to land (the row highlights under the pointer immediately before it). The same click **passes in Chromium against the mock bridge** — a scratch Playwright case clicking the row closed the tab with no console error — and passes at unit level in `ShellMenuRow.test.tsx`, where `../../logic/adapter` is jest-mocked. **Not a regression**: before T110 the row was an enabled silent no-op (`dispatchFileAction` had no `close-tab` arm, so `invoke` was `undefined`), so the click path is strictly better and is now demonstrably correct in two of three environments. **Why both test levels are blind to it**: neither reaches the real Go close plan. This is the mock-divergence class recorded against T104 and T107 — the browser suite drives `AppModelHandler.ts`, and the unit suite drives a jest mock, so the only instrument that can see this is the host.
  - **Where to look first**: the accelerator and the click call the identical `onCloseDocument` prop with the identical dispatch context (`ShellMenuRow.tsx` — `fileActionInvoker` is shared by `dispatchFileAction` and the `fileShortcutActions` memo), so the divergence is unlikely to be in the wiring. The prime suspect is downstream: `App.tsx:882` `onCloseDocument` → `closePlanAdapter.prepareClose(kind, targets, expectedTabSetRevision)`. `ApplicationShellMenu` binds `tabSetRevision` from its own `useAppSelector` when it constructs the prop, so a plan refused on a stale expected revision would be discarded silently — `resolvePreparedClosePlan` has no user-visible arm for it. Check whether `prepareClose` refuses, and whether the refusal is being swallowed the way T107's classified errors were.
  - **Tests/evidence**: a case that exercises the close through the real bridge rather than a double — the Go-side `PrepareClose` contract test paired with a host observation — plus, if the refusal is real and silent, the T107 treatment of reporting it. Full observation in `evidence/ft-ev-09/accelerators-2026-08-15.md` §5.

Kill any server on port 4174 after editing `e2e/parity/reference-adapter.ts` — it is reused across runs and will otherwise serve stale HTML. ### Appended 2026-08-15 by the T108 host walk

- [X] T113 **Done 2026-08-15. Fixed by a positioned dock — option (c) — and proven on the packaged binary.** `<footer role="status">` is unchanged: same box, same `overflow: hidden`, same 28px flex sizing, same `data-status-state`, same children. It gains a parent, `<div data-status-dock>`, which is `position: relative` and does not clip; `.details` is now a sibling of the row inside that dock, anchored with `bottom: 100%` instead of the old `transform`. **Two corrections to this entry, both established before any code was written.** *(1) The prescribed red-first swap does not go red.* `toBeVisible` cannot see a clip — Playwright defines visible as a non-empty bounding box and no `visibility: hidden`, and a clipped box still has a bounding box. `real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:286` **already asserted `toBeVisible()` on this exact region** and passed for the whole time the panel painted nothing, inside the 259-passing baseline. The assertion that does see it is `expectPainted` (`frontend/e2e/painted.ts`), which hit-tests painted output with `document.elementFromPoint`; red-first it reported `laid out at (236, 651.5) but the topmost paint there is div[role="presentation"]` — the Monaco editor — and the same red was reproduced in the parity route. It is now applied at both `Document details` sites. *(2) Option (a) is rightly rejected, but not for the stated reason.* `overflow` is in `METRIC_PROPERTIES`, but that list only runs where a reference/actual pair is measured and the status row has no pair: all six editor-status states are in `BEHAVIOUR_VERIFIED_STATE_IDS` (`e2e/parity/manifest.ts`), `targeted-manifest.ts` fails any targeted case naming one, and no manifest selector reaches the row. The real cost is the `AGENTS.md` one — dropping a scroll container changes Chromium's glyph antialiasing — and the clip is load-bearing anyway: `narrow-width.test.ts` T084 measures `scrollWidth <= clientWidth` on the row at four widths to prove it sheds items rather than wrapping. **Option (b) was rejected deliberately**: the menu popups portal into `.application-frame` because they are placed by binding coordinates *in the frame*, whereas this disclosure is placed by the row it belongs to, and the frame's right edge is not the row's — `.shell` reserves `--shell-assistant-collapsed-width` to the right of `.document`, so a frame-anchored panel needs offset arithmetic that re-breaks whenever the shell columns change; `.application-frame` also does not exist in the jsdom unit renders. **Cost, common to (b) and (c) alike**: the region is no longer a DOM descendant of the row, so six `within(status)` queries became `screen`/`page` queries (`StatusBar.test.tsx` ×4, `AppShell.test.tsx` ×2). `aria-controls`/`aria-expanded` on the trigger always carried the disclosure relationship; it is now pinned by `T113 docks the details region beside the row, outside the row that clips it`, which asserts the sibling arrangement, the trigger wiring, and that the row keeps its clip while the dock has none. `AppShell.test.tsx`'s T042 placement assertion was **tightened** through the dock rather than loosened to a `contains` check. **Gates**: `just check` exit 0, 552 tests / 77 suites (+1); `just e2e-test` exit 0, **259 passed**, parity accounting 150/150, all six T063 palette cases green ×3. **Host**: `build/bin/GoMarkEdit.app` binary mtime 16:26:51, process 4382 started 16:27:07 — sixteen seconds later and a different second. The panel opens on the 10,485,761-byte fixture and on a fresh `⌘N` document, captured by window ID in `evidence/ft-ev-09/host-screenshots/`. Full record: `evidence/ft-ev-09/details-disclosure-2026-08-15.md`. Original text follows. Make the `Document details` disclosure actually visible, per FR-FT-005 and the status-row contract (contradicts). On the packaged binary, clicking `Document details` in the status bar opens **nothing**, for every document — verified on `build/bin/GoMarkEdit.app` rebuilt at 15:25:26 with the process started 15:25:48 (not a stale instance), on a 50 MiB read-only document and on a fresh `⌘N` document, at full window width, with repeated single clicks on the button's measured centre. The panel is the only surface carrying `status.readOnlyWarning`, so T108's visible reason has nowhere to land until this is fixed.
  - **Root cause located, and it is pure CSS.** `.statusBar` (`frontend/src/ui/components/StatusBar.module.css`) is `position: relative` with **`overflow: hidden`** and `max-height: var(--status-bar-min-height)` (28px). `.details` is `position: absolute` translated by `transform: translateY(calc(-100% - var(--status-bar-min-height)))` — entirely outside its clipping ancestor. An absolutely positioned element inside an `overflow: hidden` positioned ancestor is clipped by it in every engine, so the panel is rendered, is in the accessibility tree, and paints nothing. This is not host-specific; it is equally invisible in Chromium.
  - **Why three test layers all pass.** `targeted-parity.test.ts:1653-1656` clicks the trigger and asserts `toContainText('Read-only')` — Playwright's text assertion does **not** require visibility, so it passes on a clipped element; `toBeVisible` would have failed. `StatusBar.test.tsx` runs in jsdom, which has no layout at all. The Go tests stop at the model boundary. Every instrument that "verified" this disclosure is structurally blind to clipping — the same shape as the attributed-is-not-green class already recorded in this tree.
  - **This is a design decision, not a one-line fix, which is why it is filed rather than fixed.** Three options and each costs something: (a) `overflow: visible` on `.statusBar` — but `overflow` **is** in `METRIC_PROPERTIES` (`targeted-parity.test.ts:96-122`), so it is compared against the reference and changing it fails parity outright; (b) portal the panel into `.application-frame` as the menu popups do — but T063 queries the region with `within(status)`, and portalling moves it out of the `role="status"` landmark, so the assertion and the accessibility grouping both have to be redesigned; (c) render the panel as a sibling of the footer inside a new positioned wrapper — least disruptive to the landmark, but adds an element to a row whose geometry is measured. Decide (b) vs (c) before writing code.
  - **Tests/evidence**: change the parity assertion from `toContainText` to `toBeVisible` **first** and watch it go red — that is the red-first proof, and it also permanently closes the clip-blind hole. Then fix, then re-measure the six `T063` palette cases and re-walk the host. Full observation in `evidence/ft-ev-09/readonly-reason-2026-08-15.md`.

## Phase 21: Convergence — the contracts that nothing enforces

> **Owner decisions: `decisions-phase-21.md`.** Twelve Phase 21 choices that needed the owner were
> made on 2026-08-16 and recorded there — T114, T121, T124, T130, T131, T137, T138, T139, T148,
> T151, T157 and T158, plus the binding order of work. Where a task below offers alternatives
> ("either … or", "decide the direction first", "owner decision required"), that file is the answer.
> **Read it before acting on any of those tasks, and do not re-ask.**

Assessed 2026-08-15 at `11c4c72d` against `spec.md`, `plan.md` and the constitution. **42 findings:
6 CRITICAL, 11 HIGH, 16 MEDIUM, 9 LOW.** Checked: 57 FRs, 13 SCs, 5 user stories, the eight-category
error contract, 14 plan decisions and 7 constitution principles. Constitution-violation tasks are
emitted first. Every entry carries `file:line`; the CRITICALs were verified directly.

**What is sound, so it is not re-litigated below:** the Go file lifecycle is largely correct — 24 of
37 assessed requirements clean, including atomic replacement, stable version-before/read/hash/
version-after reads, revision-safe tab commands and the conflict hunk's 12-line/4,096-byte caps. The
parity *counting* machinery is genuinely correct: `targeted-manifest.ts:321-331` declares exactly 14
pixel-compared keys and `parity/manifest.ts:402-405` derives exactly 36 behaviour-verified keys, both
failing the run at module load, with a real shrink rule (`attributed.ts:34-48,246-250`) and Monaco's
own cursor element frozen (`readiness.ts:32-34`). There is no network call anywhere in Go, and no
quality gate was weakened during the feature — `git diff 7744cc824 HEAD` over
`archtest-allowlist.json`, `.golangci.yml`, `justfile` and `.github/` is empty.

**The single pattern behind every CRITICAL**, stated once here rather than repeated per task: a thing
is built, a test asserts it, and nothing connects them. `SetDefaultOpenMode` is correct and uncalled.
`acceptsActivationAcknowledgement` is exactly the guard FR-FT-030 describes and is imported only by
its own test. `Toast.tsx` renders a remediation button correctly and no production site passes
`onRemediate`. The `t084` audit proves 61 of 61 states covered by citing a test that was deleted.
This is the same shape as T104, T107, T110 and T113 — the four defects that shipped behind green
gates — which is why the finding count is high despite 113 ticked tasks.

- [X] T115 **CRITICAL** Name the rule each proving test exercises, per Constitution II (partial). Constitution II makes this a MUST — "proving tests MUST identify the rule they exercise" — and **41 of 57 FRs are named by no test**. Only FR-FT-004, 005, 009, 011, 015, 021, 027, 034, 037, 046, 048, 049, 051, 052, 053 and 056 appear in any `*_test.go`, `*.test.ts` or `*.test.tsx` title. The existing `// Proves:` comment is adopted for both languages, so nothing already anchored needed rewriting, and it is recorded in `AGENTS.md` beside the Definition of Done together with the rule that matters more than the format: **never name a rule the test does not prove.**
  - **Partial anchors say so, in the anchor.** Where a test proves part of a requirement, the anchor names the clause it leaves out and points at T157 — e.g. FR-FT-018 proves the disk bytes but not that already-dirty documents *stay* dirty. An anchor that quietly overstates converts a coverage gap into a false record of coverage, which is how `t084-coverage-rescope.md` came to certify 61-of-61 from a deleted test.

(1) `onRemediate` is now **required** on `NotificationToast` (`Toast.tsx:14-27`); while it was optional any new render site could reintroduce this defect with every test still green. (2) It is passed at the one production site (`App.tsx:1541-1553`). (3) `NotificationRemediation` carries a `documentId` and a mandatory `intent`, and both `action` and `intent` are unions (`notificationsSlice.ts:8-32`) exhaustively switched in `App.tsx:1120-1160`, so an intent naming no command fails the build. (4) `reportClassifiedError` attaches a remediation **only when its caller declares an intent it can honour** (`classifiedNotification.ts:45-113`) — the caller earns the control by naming the command.
  - **Chosen against an App-held `dedupKey → callback` map:** the store prunes notifications on four paths, two of which App cannot observe — `enqueueToast`'s three-item cap splices silently (`notificationsSlice.ts:144-152`) and `dismissNotification` promotes a queued error (`:209-213`) — so a side map would leak or go stale. A serialisable descriptor also keeps the store a projection.
  - **Red first, verified:** `T116 renders the classified remediation and runs the command it names` (`frontend/src/App.test.tsx:787-843`) failed on `findByRole('button', {name: 'Copy path'})` with the toast rendering a Dismiss control and nothing else.
  - **Named evidence, e2e:** `FR-FT-037 offers an actionable Copy path when Reveal fails, and paints it` (`frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:87-148`) drives the whole chain in a browser — Save As adopts a path, Reveal is refused, the toast carries the backend message, the control is asserted with **`expectPainted`** (the suite's first layout assertion on any error toast; `toBeVisible` cannot see an ancestor clip, per T113), clicking it copies the path, announces `Copied path for …` in a polite live region and dismisses the resolved failure.
  - **New lever, mock-only:** `?refuseReveal=1` (`bridge-mock/go/appmodel/AppModelHandler.ts:343-366,1489-1509`). Nothing could make a classified failure carry a remediation from a browser before — `setMockRevealResult` is module-scoped and never attached to `window`, so Playwright could not reach it, which is part of why only hand-built unit objects ever exercised a remediation. Follows the `?rejectAppearance=1` precedent; the bridge mock is installed only in mock mode, and the production render path is identical with and without the flag, so this is **not** T138's DOM-branching defect.
  - **Scope deliberately not taken:** the write path carries no remediation at all until T117, because `notifyError` never sets one — so T116's own "failing write" wording is satisfied by T117, recorded there. Entry-path and close-plan `Retry` remain unhonoured and are filed as **T156**.
  - **Honest limit:** the e2e assertion runs against the dev bridge mock, so it proves the control renders, is painted, is actionable and that the command reaches the bridge — not that Go behaves identically.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green; `just verify` M1–M6 PASS, no new findings; `just e2e-test` 260 tests.

- [X] T117 **CRITICAL** Stop overwriting the backend's classified message on the write path, per the classified error and remediation contract and FR-FT-015 (contradicts). `reportWriteError` (`frontend/src/App.tsx:515-544`) dispatches `notifyError`, whose `prepare` runs `const copy = localizedErrorCopy(error);` (`logic/store/notificationsSlice.ts:150`) and substitutes generic catalogue copy keyed by code, discarding the specific message Go built. **This is precisely the defect T111 fixed for the close plan and explicitly scoped away from `reportWriteError`** — which still backs Save, Save As, external-conflict resolution and native close. Compounding it, the category-to-code ternary at `App.tsx:522-527` ends `: category === 'conflict' ? 'io' : 'io'`, so `conflict`, `capacity-limit` and `system-command-failure` all surface as `io` and become indistinguishable, contradicting `classifiedErrorCode` (`classifiedNotification.ts:16-33`), which preserves all eight.
  - **Fix shape already exists**: route these paths through `reportClassifiedError`, which passes `error.message` verbatim, exactly as T107 and T111 did for the entry and close paths. `reportWriteError`'s other callers keep their copy contract.
  - **Closed 2026-08-15.** `reportWriteError` is now a delegate to `reportClassifiedError` (`App.tsx:534-566`), and the eight-category ternary is **deleted** rather than repaired — `classifiedErrorCode` already maps all eight, so rewriting it would have duplicated that map. Six of seven call sites move unchanged (`:876`, `:903`, `:1046`, `:1067`, `:1082`, `:1182`).
  - **One caller deliberately does not move.** `reportNativeCloseError` (`App.tsx:591-604`) keeps `notifyError`. It is the only caller not carrying a Go-built `ClassifiedError`: it wraps whatever the bridge threw, and `parseError` falls back to `String(error)` for a non-`WireError` (`logic/utils/parseError.ts:65`) — raw JS/OS text. The contract forbids raw OS error text reaching the user, so the generic substitution is *protective* there, not lossy. Routing all seven uniformly would have fixed one contract clause by breaking another.
  - **A default the T107/T111 shape did not need.** `reportClassifiedError` returns early on `undefined` whereas `notifyError` always produced a toast, so the delegate supplies `error ?? {…}`. Without it a refusal carrying no error would have gone **silent** — a worse defect than the one being fixed.
  - **Copy defects fixed in the same change**, both previously dead because `localizedErrorCopy` overwrote them: `App.tsx:1085` was a bare untranslated English literal and is now `t('save.activeDocumentChanged')` (new key, `en.json:173`); and both pre-flight guards gained a `safeSubject`, or their titles would have fallen back to the generic "File operation failed".
  - **A double-report this exposed.** `onSave`/`onSaveAs` return the `WriteResult`, so a refused write was reported **twice** — once by the write path with its intent, once by T109's `onActionResult` arm without one — and `refreshDuplicate` copies the incoming remediation wholesale, so the second report erased the Retry control the first earned. Invisible before, because both reports rendered identical generic copy and the only symptom was a `×2` on a *single* failure, which reads exactly like the contract's dedup count for a failure that genuinely repeated. Guarded by `selfReportingActionIds` (`App.tsx:89-108`); the dispatcher is a pure function and cannot infer it.
  - **Red first, verified:** three Jest tests in `frontend/src/App.test.tsx` — the backend message and `conflict` code (was `io` / "The file operation could not be completed." / "File operation failed"), category distinguishability via a 50 MiB `capacity-limit` refusal (was `io`, identical to an unrelated write failure, against FR-FT-005), and one-report-not-two.
  - **T116's named failing-write evidence, discharged here:** `FR-FT-015 offers Retry on a refused Save and re-issues the write` (`frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:150-197`), using a new mock-only `?refuseSave=<n>` lever. The lever is a **count, not a boolean** on purpose: an unconditional refusal could only prove the control renders and does not throw, whereas a committed second write can only happen if the click re-issued it. `expectPainted` again, not `toBeVisible`.
  - **Retry runs `beginWrite`, not `finishWrite`** — `finishWrite` would reuse the exact `contentRevision` and decision token just refused and would deterministically refuse again.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green; `just verify` M1–M6 PASS, no new findings; 556 Jest tests (was 553).

- [X] T118 **CRITICAL** Refuse a non-existent path before any identity match or promotion, per FR-FT-040, FR-FT-004 and FR-FT-023 (contradicts). Opening a stale recent entry — the exact "explicit stale choice" FR-FT-040 says "MUST also show a classified `not-found` error" — instead silently focuses an unrelated tab or creates a blank document. With no untitled tab it creates a blank pathless document and calls `repository.Promote(ctx, "")`, surfacing a `persistence-warning` instead of the required `not-found`.

It uses `NewAppModelService` deliberately, since the empty constructor exercises only the blank-document arm. Sibling `TestReadClassifiedStableReportsAbsenceWithoutAnError` (`internal/file/document_reader_test.go:41-68`) pins the reader's absence contract so a later caller does not "fix" it.
