- [X] T120 **CRITICAL** Make "a state with no covering assertion fails closed" an executing gate, per FR-FT-051 and Constitution VII (contradicts). FR-FT-051 requires that a state with no covering assertion **MUST fail closed**. Nothing mechanically enforces it: the enforcement is a markdown table, and the table's evidence has evaporated. `spec.md:1310` names `evidence/ft-vs-08/phase-18/t084-coverage-rescope.md` as the audit closing coverage at 61 of 61 states; that file (`:39-40`) grounds its structural claim on `frontend/e2e/real-files-parity.test.ts:1577` `test('T035 proves all 546 binding comparisons across three unchanged repetitions')`. **That test does not exist** — the file now declares only `T050` (`:1070`) and `T057` (`:1102`), T035's case having been removed by T088. The per-state guard switch (`real-files-parity.test.ts:477-738`) is reachable only via `setupActual` (`:890`) from T057 (`:1120`) over four `empty`-family entries, so **38 of the 40 additional state branches never execute**. A document cannot fail a build.
  - **Fix shape**: derive every `ADDITIONAL_STATE_ASSIGNMENTS` id at run time, resolve each to a named assertion that actually ran, and throw on any id with none — the same construction `assertManifestIntegrity` already uses for key counts.
  - **Closed 2026-08-15.** Three parts. (1) `prepareActualState` now **returns the name of the assertion it ran** (`real-files-parity.test.ts:477-760`); a case that proves nothing has nothing to name, which is what makes "no covering assertion" detectable rather than merely undesirable. (2) A new test drives all 40 states (`:1208-1272`), collecting failures rather than throwing on the first — Playwright stops a case at its first failure, and 38 of these had never executed, so a throw would have reported one per run. (3) `accounting-teardown.ts:29-63` fails the run on any planned state with no recorded assertion.
  - **Planned per state id (40), not per manifest key.** The existing dimension is `manifestKey × 3 repetitions`; these ids expand across six palettes, so folding them in would have planned 720 verifications for a contract needing one covering assertion per state. Separate dimension, separate log, in `accounting.ts:60-113` and `accounting-io.ts:45-90`.
  - **The teardown guard is deliberately not shared.** `real-files-parity.test.ts` runs in the `chromium` project while the capture producer runs in `parity`, so a project-filtered run records one dimension and not the other. Reusing the capture guard would have let `--project=parity` skip the state gate silently — the shape of hole being closed.
  - **Proven to go red, both ways.** A state whose assertion **throws**: reported as `status-mixed-ending: expect(locator).toContainText(expected) failed`, and the teardown throws naming FR-FT-051. A state that **names nothing** (the `control-hovered` shape): reported as `ran no named assertion`, same teardown throw. Verified by temporarily breaking one case in each way and restoring.
  - **`control-hovered` proved nothing at all** (`:709-714` before): it drove a `.hover()` and returned with no assertion. It now resolves the row and asserts it. That state was "covered" by a mouse move.
  - **All 38 previously-unreachable assertions passed on first execution.** No stale repairs were needed beyond `control-hovered`, which is a better outcome than the "fixing a stale assertion reveals the next one" lesson predicted — stated plainly rather than claiming repair work that did not happen.
  - **`t084-coverage-rescope.md` corrected**, with the superseded bullet preserved and marked per Constitution I. Its every citation was stale: no `:1577` in a 1,159-line file, no `T035` test anywhere in `frontend/e2e/`, no `establishState` identifier, and `manifest.ts:54-92` long since moved.
  - **The 40 state list is unchanged.** Reducing it to what the in-force contract claims is T151's, and needs the owner.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green (560 frontend tests, was 556); `just verify` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` green with `[parity states] 40/40 additional states covered by a named assertion`.

- [X] T121 Reconcile SC-FT-007 against the real release build, per SC-FT-007 and T039 (partial). SC-FT-007 requires "the fresh current-host **release build**". `evidence/ft-vs-05/autosave-performance.md` reports a clean result — 100 measured trials, 100/100 at or below 5,000 ms, p95 1,394 ms, all rows retained — and then names its own substitution: it is the `native_evidence` build-tagged driver, "not the `just build` artifact", closing "T039 must independently spot-check the true `just build` binary and reopen this task if its distribution disagrees." The string `spot-check` appears in **that file and nowhere else in the repository**, so the reconciliation never happened while T039 is ticked. SC-FT-002 carries the same substitution through T105, named in `sc-ft-002/explicit-save-timings-2026-08-15.md`.
  - **Now runnable**: the note also records `just build` failing on this host, but `evidence/ft-ev-09/gates/exit-codes.txt` records `just build exit=0` and T113 walked `build/bin/GoMarkEdit.app`, so the blocker is gone.

  - **The two distributions agree, and the re-run is tighter.** 20 uncounted warmups and exactly 100 measured trials at HEAD: **100/100 committed, 0 misses, 100/100 at or below 5,000 ms**, p50 **1,253.896 ms**, p95 **1,333.884 ms**, max **1,339.916 ms**, against 2026-08-15's 1,276.937 / 1,394.934 / 1,722.859. Same four buckets, same percentile index `floor((n-1)*p)`, no retry, no outlier removal, no discarded row; all 120 rows retained. The re-run stands on its own merit too — the old numbers predate T123's rewrite of `newAtomicReplaceError` and T134's `close_drain.go`, both in the measured write path.
  - **The release artifact is fresh and was built here.** `just build` exit 0; `build/bin/GoMarkEdit.app/Contents/MacOS/GoMarkEdit`, 16,827,584 bytes, SHA-256 `ae9f581e…`, built 23:14:19Z. `go version -m` on the shipped binary reports `-tags=desktop,wv2runtime.download,production` and `-ldflags="-w -s"` — the tag set was read off the artifact, not copied from prose. **Stale-instance trap checked**: `ps -eo pid,lstart,comm | grep -i gomarkedit` was empty before and after, so nothing could be mistaken for the new build. **Mode-bit ordering honoured**: `just gen-check` was run *after* `just build` and `git ls-files -s frontend/wailsjs/runtime/` shows `100755` on all three files.
  - **The prose parity claim became a measurement.** The old note asserted "byte-identical write path" — unfalsifiable as written. `internal/appmodel` and `internal/file` produce **identical Go package build IDs** under the release tag set and under `native_evidence production`; `grep -rn native_evidence internal/` returns nothing, and neither package has a cgo file, so the release build's cgo flags cannot reach them and `-ldflags '-w -s'` is link-time only. The 100 trials therefore ran on the same object code the release binary links. The harness metadata strings in `cmd/native-evidence/autosave_latency_scenario.go:314-315` were rewritten to say this and to stop pointing at T039, so the next run reproduces the correct record rather than the stale one.
  - **What is NOT closed, stated rather than absorbed.** SC-FT-007 says the release build must *run* the protocol. It cannot, and could not be made to without ceasing to be the release build: `SetWriteCommitObserver` (`internal/appmodel/service.go:261`) has three call sites, all in `cmd/native-evidence/main_native_evidence.go` — the production composition root never calls it, so there is no t1 seam — and the embedded `frontend/dist` emits no `autosaveInputEvent` and contains no trial loop, so there is no t0 seam and no driver. The residual delta beyond the build tag is the production editor's own keystroke handling (Monaco input, React render, call into the adapter) and the packaged `.app` launch context; the evidence frontend mounts the real `src/App` and drives the real `appModelAdapter.updateBuffer`, so the 200 ms working-copy synchronisation *is* inside the measured interval. The interactive host spot-check is a GUI action, was **not** performed, and is filed as **T181**.
  - **A latent trap found by running the harness, half fixed and half filed.** `frontend/evidence/vite.config.ts` writes its bundle to `frontend/dist-native-evidence/<scenario>/`, and that directory is named by **none** of `.gitignore`, `frontend/.prettierignore` or the eslint ignore list — unlike `frontend/dist/`, which is in all three. So the first `just check` after any native-evidence run fails `frontend-format-check` on eight minified artifacts and then `frontend-lint` with **9,182 errors** in a Monaco worker bundle, and a `git add -A` would commit ~3 MB of build output. Only the `.gitignore` entry was added here, because it protects against the `add -A` hazard and cannot make any gate pass — proof: lint still failed with it in place. **The prettier and eslint ignores were deliberately NOT touched**: AGENTS.md forbids editing an eslint config to make a gate pass, and a task about autosave latency is the wrong place to argue the exception. The directory was deleted instead and the seam is filed as **T182**.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green; `go test -race ./internal/... .` clean; `go test -race -tags native_evidence ./cmd/...` clean; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS. No `frontend/` or `e2e/` source change, so `just e2e-test` was not required.

- [X] T122 Name the file, not the internal document ID, in every classified error, per the classified error and remediation contract (contradicts). The contract requires every classified message to "name only the safe basename or the document's shortest-unique disambiguated tab label (FR-FT-035)". Instead every one is titled with a synthetic ID. `refusedWrite` passes `documentID` as the **subject** argument — `apperr.NewClassifiedError(category, documentID, message, remediation, documentID)` (`internal/appmodel/save.go:599`) — and `NewClassifiedError` computes `safeSubject := filepath.Base(...)` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go:77`), which is a no-op on `doc-0000000000000003` as minted by `mintDocumentID` (`internal/appmodel/service.go:965`). The frontend renders `error.safeSubject` directly as the notification title (`frontend/src/App.tsx:533`, `logic/store/classifiedNotification.ts:64`). Same pattern at `internal/appmodel/conflict.go:566,570`, `tab_reorder.go:69`, `tab_session.go:57` and `close_plan.go:503`.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16. Escalated to CRITICAL first**: T117 stopped `localizedErrorCopy` overwriting the notification title, so the synthetic id became the *rendered* title rather than a field nobody read. It was always wrong; it only became visible.
  - **Two layers, deliberately.** (1) The paths that can name a file now do: `refusedWrite`/`conflictWrite` (`save.go:613-640`) and `classifiedConflictError` (`conflict.go:566-575`) resolve the document's own label — disambiguated tab label, else path basename, else title, else the generic word — via `safeDocumentLabelLocked`. (2) `NewClassifiedError` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go:77-80,96-125`) now **rejects the minted-id shape outright**. `mintDocumentID` produces every synthetic id in the application — documents, close plans *and* open reservations — so any of the ~46 helper call sites could reintroduce this; a per-call-site fix cannot prevent the next one, and the constructor can.
  - **A deadlock this nearly shipped.** The label helper first took `service.mu.RLock()`. `conflictRefused` runs *inside* locked regions and Go's RWMutex is not reentrant, so `TestReadOnlyConflictOffersReloadAndCancelOnly` hung for the full 10-minute timeout. Renamed to `safeDocumentLabelLocked` per this package's existing `…Locked` convention; `go test -race` across every package is clean, which is what proves each caller really does hold the lock.
  - **The handler's panic-recovery keeps the generic word.** `refusedWriteLabelled`/`conflictRefusedLabelled` exist because the handler holds `AppModelServiceAPI`, not the concrete service, and is recovering from a panic in the very code that owns the document map — reaching into that state to prettify an error would trade a safe word for a second crash.
  - **Red first, verified:** `TestRefusedWriteNamesTheFileNotTheDocumentID` failed with `refusal names "doc-0000000000000001"` — the exact string a user would have seen. Plus `TestRefusedWriteFallsBackToUntitledForAPathlessDocument`, and two constructor tests pinning that the guard catches the minted shape without swallowing a real file called `doc-review.md`.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green (560 frontend tests); `go test -race` clean; `just verify` M1–M6 PASS, no new findings.

- [X] T123 Bind each remediation to its category row, per the classified error and remediation contract (contradicts). The contract fixes one remediation set per category; nothing enforces it. `permission-denied` is specified message-only because "retrying the identical action cannot succeed", yet `internal/file/atomic_replace.go:260` sets `remediation := apperr.RemediationRetry` as the default and `internal/appmodel/save.go:307` hardcodes `RemediationRetry` for whatever category the atomic-replace error carried. `capacity-limit` (message-only, naming the limit) is emitted with `RemediationCancel` at `file_lifecycle.go:184` and `internal/file/document_reader.go:211`; `unsupported-input` (message-only) likewise at `save.go:135`, `copy_path.go:28,56` and `tab_reorder.go:26`. `ClassifiedError.Validate` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go:95-108`) checks only that the remediation is in the global vocabulary — there is no per-category table anywhere.
  - **Fix shape**: add the category-to-allowed-remediation map in `apperr` and enforce it in `NewClassifiedError`/`Validate`, then correct each violating call site. Coordinate with T116, which makes these remediations user-visible for the first time.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16.** `remediationsByCategory` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go:60-100`) is the contract table made enforceable. `Validate` now rejects a category/remediation pairing the contract forbids, and `NewClassifiedError` **coerces a forbidden pairing to message-only** — the same fail-safe shape as T122's subject guard, so a missed call site degrades to no action rather than offering one that cannot work.
  - **Root cause was a default, not a call site.** `newAtomicReplaceError` (`internal/file/atomic_replace.go:259-273`) set `remediation := RemediationRetry` and overrode it only for `conflict` and `persistence-warning`, so **every** other category — including `permission-denied` — inherited Retry. It now names each row. `save.go:307` compounded it by hardcoding `RemediationRetry` for whatever category the atomic replace produced; it now carries the classification's own remediation.
  - **Call sites corrected across seven files** — `document_reader.go`, `save.go`, `conflict.go`, `copy_path.go`, `file_lifecycle.go`, `tab_reorder.go`, `close_plan.go` — so the four message-only categories (`permission-denied`, `capacity-limit`, `unsupported-input`, `persistence-warning`) carry no action. The coercion is a net, not the mechanism.
  - **The mock was loosened to match, deliberately.** `bridge-mock/.../AppModelHandler.ts` hardcoded `remediation: 'Retry'` for every category it built, so a browser test would have shown a Retry button on a message-only refusal and passed. A test double looser than the backend makes the defect behind it unreachable; it now mirrors the same table.
  - **Red first, verified:** `TestClassifiedErrorRefusesARemediationItsCategoryForbids` failed on `Validate` accepting `Retry` for `permission-denied`, plus a positive test that the allowed pairings survive and one asserting every category declares a set.
  - **A spec/code contradiction found and filed, not resolved in code:** the contract's `conflict` row enumerates only the external-change actions, but this codebase also classifies **stale-tab-set and stale-revision refusals** as `conflict`, where re-issuing against the fresh revision is the only sensible action and is what T156 will honour. The table allows `Retry` for `conflict` with that reasoning stated at the definition, and **T159** now owns amending the row or reclassifying those refusals. Constitution I forbids resolving this silently.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green (560 frontend tests); `go test -race` clean; `just verify` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` 262 passed, `40/40` states, `150/150` verifications.

- [X] T124 Make the twice-confirmed Quit-and-discard path reachable, per FR-FT-016 and FR-FT-027 (missing). The whole of `internal/appmodel/resync_recovery.go` — `ProjectionRecovery`, `Start`, `Retry`, `Surface`, `RequestQuitAndDiscard`, `ConfirmQuitAndDiscard`, `QuitAndDiscardAuthorized` — has **no non-test importer**; `NewProjectionRecovery` appears only at its own definition (`:37`) and in `resync_recovery_test.go`. The immediate/250 ms/1 s cadence is re-implemented in TypeScript at `frontend/src/logic/adapter/appModelAdapter.ts:715-750`, but the second confirmation FR-FT-016 requires exists nowhere reachable: `quit-discard-newer` appears only in the dev bridge mock (`frontend/src/dev/bridge-mock/go/appmodel/AppModelHandler.ts:503`), and `internal/application/close_coordinator.go` has no recovery awareness at all.
  - **Decide the direction first**: either delete the dead Go module and implement the second confirmation on the reachable close path, or wire `ProjectionRecovery` into the composition root with bound surface/retry/quit-discard methods. Do not leave both.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16, on the direction the owner recorded in `decisions-phase-21.md`:** delete the dead Go module and implement FR-FT-016's second confirmation on the reachable close path. `ProjectionRecovery` was not wired into the composition root, and neither implementation was left standing.
  - **What the premise got right, and the one thing it got wrong.** `internal/appmodel/resync_recovery.go` really had no non-test importer, and it is gone with its test file. But the reachable path was not missing a second confirmation outright — it was missing the *clause* that makes one useful. `App.tsx` already gates a recovery quit twice: the persistent banner's `Quit and discard newer unsaved changes` calls `requestQuit`, and the resulting close request opens a modal that must be confirmed before any close plan is prepared. What that modal never did was **name the affected documents**, which FR-FT-016 requires in the same sentence. It asked the user to discard "the affected documents" without ever saying which, so the second confirmation could not be an informed one.
  - **Root cause of the naming gap.** The TypeScript `RecoverySurface` (`frontend/src/logic/store/appModelTypes.ts`) carries `message` and four booleans and no document list; the deleted Go `RecoverySurface` had `DocumentNames`, and it was the only place the clause had ever been expressed. So the field existed exactly where nothing could read it.
  - **The fix names the set the discard actually destroys.** `recoveryDiscardNames` in `App.tsx` selects the projection's dirty documents — the ones FR-FT-016 keeps "modified against the committed baseline" — and labels them with `tabLabelsFor`, the same disambiguated labels the tab strip uses, so two open `notes.md` read as two rows rather than one repeated name. A clean tab loses nothing and is deliberately excluded. When the set is empty the prompt says so rather than falling silent: a user being asked to approve a discard is entitled to know that nothing will be discarded.
  - **Reading the projection is the right source here, not a shortcut.** Rehydration is precisely what failed on this path, so the last delivered snapshot is the only record of what is about to be lost, and commands are blocked, so it cannot move underneath the prompt.
  - **Red first.** `TestingLibraryElementError: Unable to find an accessible element with the role "list" and name "Documents with newer unsaved changes"` — the modal rendered its two paragraphs and its two buttons and named nothing.
  - **The deleted tests, stated rather than dropped quietly.** `internal/appmodel/resync_recovery_test.go` went with the module: all four of its cases (`TestResyncRetriesAt250msAndOneSecond`, `TestResyncExhaustionShowsPersistentRecoverySurface`, `TestResyncRetryRestartsBoundedCycle`, `TestQuitAndDiscardRequiresSecondConfirmation`) had `ProjectionRecovery` as their only subject, so they proved the arithmetic of a module that never ran — the self-asserting shape already recorded against T099 and T110. `TestQuitAndDiscardRequiresSecondConfirmation` in particular recorded a second confirmation no user could reach, which is worse than no coverage. The first half of `TestRecoveryQuitStillDrainsAndPermits` (`internal/application/close_coordinator_test.go`) was the same exercise and is removed; its second half is real — it drives `ApplicationContextHolder` through the drain and the one-use permit — and stays, now with an anchor naming the FR-FT-027 clause it actually proves. The reachable cadence is covered by `appModelAdapter.test.ts`'s bounded-recovery case, and the two confirmations by `App.test.tsx`.
  - **Deliberately not done.** `RecoverySurface` was not widened with a `documentNames` field. The adapter that builds it has no view of dirtiness, so the field would have to be filled by the same component that already renders the prompt — an indirection that adds a place for the two to disagree. The Go backend gained no recovery awareness: the owner's decision rules out wiring `ProjectionRecovery` in, and FR-FT-027's backend obligation for this path is the drain and permit sequence, which `AuthorizeQuit` already applies to it unchanged (proved by `TestRecoveryQuitStillDrainsAndPermits`).

- [X] T125 Report the Reveal disappearance race instead of swallowing it, per FR-FT-037 (contradicts). `internal/appmodel/copy_path.go:61-67` handles a file that disappears only at invocation by calling `service.markDetached(...)` and returning `apperr.RevealResult{Status: apperr.PathCommandUnavailable}` with **no error at all**, collapsing it into the already-known-missing case at `:57-59`. FR-FT-037 requires that case to "mark the document detached and report one deduplicated `not-found` error offering `Save to recreate` and `Copy path`". Separately, `ClassifiedError` carries a single `Remediation` field (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go:70`), so the required **pair** cannot be expressed even once the error is raised — the same structural limit that blocks Reveal's `Retry` + `Copy path` fallback.
  - Widen `ClassifiedError` to carry a remediation set, which T116 and T123 both depend on.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16. Two defects, one structural and one behavioural.** The field is now `Remediations []ClassifiedRemediation` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go:112-125`), because **three contract rows specify sets and none of them was satisfiable**: `not-found` for a detached document is "`Save to recreate` plus `Copy path`", `system-command-failure` is "`Retry`; a Reveal failure also offers `Copy path`", and `conflict` names three at once. No call site could have got those right.
  - **The constructor kept its arity, deliberately.** 164 production lines pass one remediation constant; changing the signature would have touched every one of them to say nothing new. `NewClassifiedError` now wraps a single value and `NewClassifiedErrorWithRemediations` serves the three rows that need a set, so the diff lands where the meaning changed.
  - **Coercion generalised without weakening.** `permittedRemediations` drops each forbidden member rather than voiding the whole set. The property that matters is "never offer an action that cannot work"; discarding a legal `Copy path` because the same caller also asked for an illegal `Retry` would serve nobody. For a one-element set the outcome is identical to T123's coercion to message-only, so nothing regressed. `Validate` still reports every forbidden member, and now also rejects `RemediationNone` *inside* a set — message-only is the empty set, not a member of one.
  - **The behavioural half: the race is reported.** `copy_path.go:61-68` called `markDetached` and returned `PathCommandUnavailable` with **no error at all**, so a user who chose Reveal on a file that had vanished saw nothing happen. It now detaches and returns one classified `not-found` offering both actions. The two pair-producing outcomes are helpers — `revealDetachedFailure` and `revealCommandFailure` — so a third call site cannot offer half a pair.
  - **Red first, verified:** `TestRevealDisappearanceAtInvocationReportsNotFoundOfferingBothActions` (`copy_path_test.go`) failed with `status = "unavailable", want "refused" — the race was swallowed`.
  - **An existing test was pinning the defect, and is corrected rather than dropped.** `TestRevealInFileManagerRevalidatesExistenceAndClassifiesFailure` asserted `Error == nil` for a vanished file under the name "known-missing" — but the document was not detached yet, so it was exercising the race, not the known-missing branch. It now asserts the race reports and that a *second* reveal is the silent no-op that keeps one failure from being reported twice.
  - **The e2e suite caught a regression the 560 unit tests missed.** Narrowing the set to one control by taking the first honourable member handed the Reveal failure a `Retry` button, because the contract writes that row Retry-first and `Copy path` is the member that still helps when the file is gone. `remediationFor` now expresses a *preference*, not set order (`classifiedNotification.ts`). Rendering the whole set is T142's; adding a control without a command behind it is the defect T116 exists to remove.
  - **The mock was tightened in the same commit.** `MOCK_REMEDIATION_BY_CATEGORY` yields sets, the Reveal override sends the real `['Retry', 'Copy path']` pair, and the two `capacity-limit` + `Cancel` fixtures in `App.test.tsx` became message-only — Go strips that pairing, so a double that kept it was looser than the backend.
  - **Gates:** `just check` green (560 frontend tests, unchanged); `go test -race ./internal/... .` clean; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS, no new findings; `just e2e-test` **262 passed, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`** — baseline exactly.

- [X] T126 Close the clip-blind hole beyond the two `Document details` sites, per FR-FT-045, FR-FT-046 and SC-FT-013 (partial). `expectPainted` (`frontend/e2e/painted.ts:26`) is the only assertion in this repository that detects clipping by an ancestor, and it is called at exactly two sites — `real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:297` and `targeted-parity.test.ts:1672`, both `Document details`. Every other surface whose entire purpose is to be seen is asserted clip-blind. Prioritised by real clipping risk: the error toast (`window-shell.test.ts:723-728` — `toContainText`/`toHaveCount` with **no layout assertion at all**); the **Tab actions menu** (`real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:68-78`), which is the only `data-viewport-popup` widget rendered without `createPortal` (`TabContextMenu.tsx:181-186`) and is asserted only with `toBeEnabled`/`toBeDisabled`; the Close and ExternalChange prompts (`real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:97-103,172-175,348-354`), which render inline because `ModalShell.tsx:195-197` portals only on the narrow parity route; the About dialog, editor context menu and toolbar overflow (`editor-stage.test.ts:294,518,560,630-631`); and the narrow-width popups and paused-preview bar (`narrow-width.test.ts:190,750-752`), guarded only by `expectInsideViewport`, which is `boundingBox()` arithmetic and equally clip-blind.
  - **Carry the T113 lesson**: `toBeVisible` will not go red on a clipped element, so a red-first proof requires `expectPainted` from the start.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16.** The root cause is not that anyone forgot to assert visibility — every one of these surfaces was
    asserted, and asserted heavily. It is that all four of the assertions the suite reached for answer a question adjacent to
    the one that matters. `toContainText` reads the text tree and never touches layout. `toBeVisible()` is defined as a
    non-empty bounding box plus no `visibility: hidden`, and an element an ancestor has clipped to nothing keeps its box.
    `toBeEnabled`/`toBeDisabled` read the accessibility tree. `expectInsideViewport` in `narrow-width.test.ts` is pure
    `boundingBox()` arithmetic, so it proves a clipped element is clipped *in the right place*. `document.body.contains(...)`
    in `editor-stage.test.ts` proves a portal target and nothing about paint. Nine surfaces were therefore covered by
    assertions that would all stay green through the exact defect T113 shipped.
  - **Red-first, and specifically the contrast.** Adding `expectPainted` to a surface that already paints goes green
    immediately, which proves the call site compiles and nothing else. So each surface was reached, a clipping ancestor was
    injected, and the old and new assertions were run against the same DOM. A temporary `frontend/e2e/t126-clip-contrast.test.ts`
    (deleted before commit) did this for all nine; **all nine went red on `expectPainted` while every pre-existing assertion
    around it stayed green.** Verbatim, for the two the task named:

    ```
    [T126] error toast: position:static clipper:<ol class="_viewport_1p1s4_1"> via overflow:hidden;height:0
    [T126] error toast: OLD assertions all PASSED while clipped
      ✘  1 [chromium] › e2e/t126-clip-contrast.test.ts:72:1 › CONTRAST 1 error toast (497ms)

      Error: the appearance-refusal error toast is laid out at (1114, 730) but the topmost paint there is
      nothing. It is clipped away or covered, and neither toBeVisible() nor toContainText() can see that (T113).
    ```

    The four "OLD assertions" that passed under that clip were `toContainText('Invalid input')`,
    `toContainText('A value needs to be corrected.')`, `toHaveCount(1)` and `toBeVisible()` — i.e. the entire existing
    coverage of the toast, plus a `toBeVisible()` the case did not even have.

    ```
    [T126] tab actions menu: position:absolute clipper:<div class="_shell_1i6f1_10"> via clip-path:inset(0 0 100% 0)
    [T126] tab actions menu: OLD assertions all PASSED while clipped
      ✘  2 [chromium] › e2e/t126-clip-contrast.test.ts:93:1 › CONTRAST 2 tab actions menu (486ms)

      Error: the Tab actions menu is laid out at (1143, 204) but the topmost paint there is div. It is clipped
      away or covered, and neither toBeVisible() nor toContainText() can see that (T113).
    ```

    The seven others produced the same shape and are recorded here in one line each, each with its pre-existing assertions
    green in the same run: close prompt — "laid out at (640, 360) but the topmost paint there is div#root", with
    `toBeVisible()` and `toBeFocused()` on Cancel both green; external-change prompt — "(640, 360) … topmost paint there is
    main", with `toBeVisible()` and the three-button order assertion green; About dialog — "(640, 360) … div#root", with
    `toBeVisible()` green; editor context menu — "(359, 383.5) … html", with `toBeVisible()` and `document.body.contains`
    green; toolbar overflow — "(412.484, 185) … html", same two green; document launcher at 768 — "(407, 68) … div", with the
    full `expectInsideViewport` bounds arithmetic green; paused preview bar — "(748, 295.5) … section", same. So all nine are
    proven load-bearing; none were merely added.
  - **Two mechanics worth writing down.** First, `overflow:hidden;height:0` — the literal T113 shape — reaches only the
    toast and the launcher. It cannot clip a `position: fixed` descendant, and it cannot collapse a flex item whose grow
    factor overrides `height`; `clip-path: inset(0 0 100% 0)` was needed for the other seven. Second, the paused preview bar
    resisted *every* clip until its ancestry was read: its three nearest ancestors (`section`, `.previewContent`, `.pane`)
    are all `display: contents`, which generates no box at all, so `overflow`, `height` and `clip-path` on them are inert.
    The real clipping ancestor is `div._panes`. A future clip-injection harness must skip `display: contents` ancestors or it
    will silently prove nothing.
  - **Nothing was found genuinely clipped.** All nine surfaces paint correctly in production; the new assertions were green
    on the first unmodified run and stayed green through the full suite. This task hardened coverage, it did not uncover a
    defect.
  - **Where the assertions went.** `window-shell.test.ts` T026 (error toast); `real-files-and-tabs.test.ts` FT-VS-03 (Tab
    actions menu), FT-VS-04 (external-change prompt), FT-VS-06 and FT-VS-08 (close prompt); `editor-stage.test.ts` T070
    (About dialog) and T055 (editor context menu at three widths, toolbar overflow at 375 and 768); `narrow-width.test.ts`
    — the paint check moved *into* `expectInsideViewport` itself, so all thirteen of its call sites gained it at once, plus
    `openToolbarOverflow`. Every anchor added is a `FR-FT-046 (partial — …)` naming the one clause proven and the widths it
    was proven at; the anchor for the About dialog was initially placed on T019, which contains no such assertion, and was
    moved to T070 before commit. Line numbers in the task text had drifted: the toast is at ~721 not 723, the tab menu at
    ~68 (unchanged), the prompts at 248/321/497 not 97/172/348, `editor-stage.test.ts` at 292/519/560/634, and
    `narrow-width.test.ts:190` is the `openToolbarOverflow` helper.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — 79 test suites / 575 tests, lint 0 errors / 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings, no
    new findings against baseline. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` — M1–M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test` — **262 passed**,
    `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted`.

- [X] T127 Replace the macOS accelerator pixel-skip with a reference variant, per FR-FT-055 and FR-FT-056 (contradicts). The T059 accelerator exception is implemented as a mask, inside the module whose header forbids masks. `targeted-parity.test.ts:1437-1441` collects `acceptedRects` from the exception and passes them to `attributeDifferences` as `extraAccepted`; `frontend/e2e/parity/attributed.ts:200` executes `if (extraAccepted.some((rect) => withinRect(x, y, rect))) continue;` **before** the attribution lookup, so those pixels land in neither `attributedPixels` nor `unattributedPixels` and can never fail the slice. They carry no `measuredPixels` ceiling, no `maxChannelDelta` and no shrink rule, and they cover glyphs — which FR-FT-055 forbids a mask from hiding. The same file states the rule it breaks at `attributed.ts:8-16`: "A mask deletes pixels from the count. Nothing reports them, and drift inside a masked area is invisible forever."

  - **Line drift.** The `acceptedRects` collection had moved to `targeted-parity.test.ts:1443-1445` and the skip to `attributed.ts:202`. Located by content.
  - **Root cause, and it is worse than "a mask in the wrong file".** `attributedPixels` is derived as `differing - unattributed`, so a pixel skipped by `extraAccepted` before the lookup was **counted as attributed while no declared term accounted for it**. The mechanism's whole promise — every excused pixel names a cause, cites its evidence and carries a measured ceiling — was silently false for exactly these pixels, and they were glyphs, which FR-FT-055 forbids a mask from hiding.
  - **The invariant that catches it.** Not "does the slice pass" but **"do the declared terms account for every pixel the result calls attributed"**. That is checkable on every call and cannot be satisfied by a rectangle that excuses without declaring. Red-first, verbatim: `● T127 accounts every attributed pixel to a declared term, ignoring any extra rectangle` / `Expected: 12` / `Received: 0` — the terms accounted for 0 of the 12 pixels the result called attributed — reported as `Tests: 1 failed, 9 passed, 10 total`.
  - **The fix, in this codebase's own idiom.** `fileMenuReferenceAccelerators` gained `new-file`, `open-file`, `save` and `save-as` for both hosts (`⌘N`/`⌘O`/`⌘S`/`⌘⇧S`, `Ctrl+N`/`Ctrl+O`/`Ctrl+S`/`Ctrl+Shift+S`) and `adaptFileMenu` writes them through the existing `acceleratorSpan` helper into the mockup's own `.k` primitive — the same treatment `close-tab` has had since T070, which is why this was always available. The source-primitive guard still asserts the mockup writes `New File<span class="k">Ctrl N</span>` and the other three, so a source that stops writing them fails loudly instead of leaving the reference unadapted. `REFERENCE_ADAPTER_VERSION` is bumped to `feature-003-reference-adapter-v3`; `fileMenuReferenceAccelerators` was already inside `REFERENCE_ADAPTER_HASH`, so the hash moves with it.
  - **The result: the exception was not needed at all.** With the reference carrying the host accelerators, `targeted:file-menu:1280:minimal-light` passes with **zero** accepted pixels — three repetitions, `3 passed`, and the whole run at `[parity accounting] 150/150`. Its one declared residual, `popup-antialiased-boundary` at 181 pixels, is unchanged and still within its shrink band. **No residual was added or grown to absorb the retired mask.**
  - **What was deleted, not narrowed.** `extraAccepted` (parameter and skip) from `attributed.ts`; and from `targeted-parity.test.ts`: `classifyFilePopupPixelException`, `FilePopupPixelException`, `FILE_POPUP_ACCELERATOR_GLYPH_EXCEPTION_ACTIONS`, `PixelRect`, `relativePixelRect`, `allPixelDifferencesWithin`, `unexplainedPopupPixels`, the `acceptedRects` derivation, the `platformExceptions` list and every artifact field that carried it (`file-rows.json` `accepted`/`acceptedPixelException`/`platformExceptions`, `metrics.json` and `status.json` `platformExceptions`, and `raw-status.log` `platform_exception_count`). Every File row now fails closed on pixels; the per-row evidence stays, as additional evidence rather than a substitute.
  - **`unexplainedPopupPixels`: yes, the same problem, and it is gone with it.** It was the whole-popup counterpart of the identical rectangle skip, selected when `declaredResiduals.length === 0 && acceptedRects.length > 0`, and it excused by rectangle with no ceiling, no `maxChannelDelta` and no shrink rule — the same three absences. It was not a second defect so much as the same one applied at popup scale. With `acceptedRects` gone the selector collapses to `declaredResiduals.length > 0 ? attribution.unattributedPixels : comparison.metrics.differentPixelCount`, so the function became unreachable and was deleted.
  - **Deliberately not done.** No production code changed — the four accelerators production renders were already correct and registry-derived; this was entirely a reference-side and harness-side fix. `comparator.ts`'s reviewed-mask list stays empty and untouched, no attributed residual was added or widened, and no other reference variant was altered.
  - **Gates:** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites / **613** frontend tests (+2: the attribution invariant and the adapter accelerator case), lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS; `just e2e-test` **267 passed**, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted`. The port-4174 reference server was killed before every run after the adapter edit.
  - **The fix already exists in this codebase's own idiom**: `adaptViewMenu`, `adaptAboutMenu` and (since T112) `adaptDeferredSettingsRows` rewrite their accelerators from the mockup's own `.k` primitive, so the rows are measured exactly. Do the same for the four File rows and delete the `extraAccepted` path. Fold any new adapter markup into `REFERENCE_ADAPTER_HASH`, and kill the port-4174 server afterwards.

- [X] T128 Guard every activation acknowledgement before installing it, per FR-FT-030 (missing). FR-FT-030 requires an identity-and-revision-bound acknowledgement to "be applied only while both values still match the confirmed active projection". The guard exists and is unused: `acceptsActivationAcknowledgement` (`frontend/src/ui/widgets/editorSession.ts:137-155`) has exactly one importer, its own `editorSession.integration.test.tsx:21`. Production applies acknowledgements unconditionally — `if (result.data !== undefined) setActiveBuffer(result.data);` (`App.tsx:509-511`), repeated unguarded at `:445-447`, `:459-461`, `:474-476` and `:488-490` — with no generation, projection-revision, active-identity or `contentRevision` check, so a stale or superseded acknowledgement installs its content. This is the cross-document text installation SC-FT-003 requires to be impossible.
  - **Tests/evidence**: a test that resolves a superseded acknowledgement after a newer activation and asserts its content is never installed, failing first.
  - **Closure.** The root cause was that the rule had a correct implementation and no caller. `acceptsActivationAcknowledgement` was written, tested against itself, and imported by nothing else, while all five acknowledging handlers in `App.tsx` installed the answer the instant it arrived. The fix does not repeat the guard at those five sites — a rule that has to be remembered five times is what produced this — but routes every install through one `useGuardedActivation` hook (`frontend/src/ui/widgets/editorSession.ts`): a handler claims a generation with `begin()` before issuing its command and hands the answer to `acknowledge()`. A pending acknowledgement waits there and is re-tested on each projection change until either its generation is superseded or the projection confirms it, because Go publishes its patch on the event bus while the answer returns on the call, so the store routinely still describes the previous document when an acknowledgement lands; rejecting outright at that moment would drop legitimate ones. To make a sixth call site fail rather than drift, `App.tsx`'s raw setter is renamed `setActiveBuffer` → `replaceActiveBuffer`, so a handler reaching for the familiar name to install a command's answer no longer compiles. `replaceActiveBuffer` is left in place for the four handoffs that are not acknowledgements: startup, editor-state recovery, a completed close plan, and an external-change reload.
  - **Red first.** `npx jest src/App.test.tsx -t "T128"` against the unguarded code, on the case SC-FT-003 names — a switch to `document-2` superseded by a switch back to `document-1`, its acknowledgement resolving last: `expect(element).not.toHaveTextContent() / Expected element not to have text content: superseded cross-document source / Received: superseded cross-document source` at `src/App.test.tsx:2214`. The failure is the installation itself, not a missing mock: the `winning source` assertion two lines earlier passed in the same run.
  - **The wiring exposed a live defect in the guard, not only a missing call.** Its first green unit run still failed three e2e cases (`T084 768px renders, contains and operates the real launcher`, `FT-VS-07`, `FT-VS-10`). Cause: `DocumentMetadata.ContentRevision` is tagged `contentRevision,omitempty` (`internal/apperr/results.go:132`) while the acknowledgement's `ActiveBuffer.DocumentRevision` is tagged plainly (`:160`), so a document that has never been edited publishes no `contentRevision` and acknowledges `documentRevision: 0`. The raw comparison was `undefined === 0`, which rejected every File New and every freshly opened file — in production, not only under the mock. Both sides are now defaulted, with the "document is absent from the projection" case kept as a separate, still-rejecting condition.
  - **Second fidelity gap, in the mock bridge.** `frontend/src/dev/bridge-mock/.../AppModelHandler.ts` advanced `MockDocument.documentRevision` but never republished it, and its local `DocumentMetadata` type had no `contentRevision` field at all, so the mock could not express a value the real bridge sends. Nothing read the projected revision until this guard did, so the divergence was invisible. `cloneMetadata` now republishes it.

The "edge move is a successful no-op without a revision bump" rule has no code path either. **This is T110's defect class, unfixed for these two ids** — `shortcutRegistry.test.ts:45-47` asserts only that the registry table contains the strings, which is arithmetic, not behaviour.

  - **Root cause, and it was one line further back than the brief suggests.** The narrowing at `:291` was the visible half. Removing it was not enough: the listener resolves its action with `actionsForSurface('shortcuts').find(...)`, and `move-tab-left`/`move-tab-right` declare exactly one surface, `tab-context`. So the lookup could not return them however wide the narrowing became — `shortcutForKeyEvent` resolved `Mod+Shift+PageUp` correctly, handed back a binding, and the `find` returned `undefined`. Two lists had to be searched, not one.
  - **Why the registry was left alone.** Adding `shortcuts` to those two entries would have been the other fix, and it would also have listed both actions in `ShortcutsDialog`, which renders `actionsForSurface('shortcuts')` verbatim. The e2e case failed with `expect(locator).toContainText(expected) failed` / `Locator: getByRole('status').filter({ hasText: 'Moved' })` / `Expected substring: "position 1 of 2"` / `Error: element(s) not found` — the announcement never fired because the key reached nothing.
  - **Red-first evidence, the edge no-op — triangulated on purpose.** An edge test written against the *absent* feature passes trivially: no code means no command means no revision bump, and the test proves nothing. So the widened listener was first written the plausible wrong way, clamping the index with `Math.max(0, current - 1)` and `Math.min(length - 1, current + 1)`. Against that, the edge case failed verbatim: `expect(jest.fn()).not.toHaveBeenCalled()` / `Expected number of calls: 0` / `Received number of calls: 1` / `1: "one", 0, 4` — a reorder issued to the position the tab already occupied, which the backend would answer with a fresh tab-set revision. The clamp was then replaced with a bounds check that returns. That intermediate red is the only honest proof this clause is covered, and it is recorded rather than implied.
  - **What the edge no-op means here.** Succeeding, not refusing: the key is consumed with `preventDefault()`, no command is issued, no revision moves, no announcement is made, and no notification is raised. The unit cases assert all five, including `store.getState().notifications.items` being empty, because "succeeds as a no-op" and "is refused silently" look identical if you only check the tab order.

Nothing about the announcement or the revision handling is new code. The keydown effect moved below `handleTabAction` in the file so it can call it; that relocation is the only structural change.

- [X] T130 Implement tab drag reordering, per FR-FT-036 (missing). The requirement is wholly unbuilt. A repository-wide grep over `frontend/src` for `draggable`, `onDragStart`, `onDragOver`, `onDrop` and `dataTransfer` returns nothing, and so does one for `scrollIntoView`/`autoScroll`. `DocumentTabs.tsx:459-535` renders each tab with `onClick` and `onContextMenu` only. There is no insertion indicator, no reduced-opacity dragged tab, no Escape cancel, no strip auto-scroll near the edges, and no same-position-drop suppression; `DocumentTabs.test.tsx` contains no drag case. Note this is distinct from T129: FR-FT-036 is the pointer contract, FR-FT-034 the keyboard one.
  - **Root cause: it was simply never built.** Nothing was broken and nothing regressed — the audit grep was accurate, there was no drag code of any kind in `frontend/src`, and `DocumentTabs.tsx` bound only `onClick` and `onContextMenu` to a tab. This closure adds the first pointer drag the tab strip has ever had.
  - **Scope, per `decisions-phase-21.md`.** The owner cut FR-FT-036 to its core: **grab, insertion indicator, drop**, plus the two cheap guards — **Escape cancels** and **a same-position drop issues nothing**. **Deferred: the strip's edge auto-scroll and the reduced-opacity dragged tab**, both filed as **T177** so the gap survives this closure. Every `// Proves:` anchor written here is partial and names the two clauses it does not cover; none of them claims FR-FT-036 whole.
  - **Pointer events, not the HTML5 drag-and-drop API.** There was no existing drag code to follow, so this was a free choice. DnD loses it on three counts: its Escape handling belongs to the browser and cannot be observed reliably, it imposes a drag image that would fight the custom ghost T177 owns, and `dataTransfer` is awkward to drive from Playwright. `pointerdown` on the tab arms a grab; `pointermove`/`pointerup`/`pointercancel`/`keydown` are taken on `document` for the life of the drag and removed with it. No pointer capture is used, so the same code path is drivable from jsdom and from a real mouse.
  - **A drag is N one-position commands, not one N-position command.** `internal/appmodel/tab_reorder.go:25` refuses any `targetIndex` further than ±1 from the current index as `unsupported-input`, so a drag across two tabs issues two reorders. Each carries the tab-set revision it is issued against — for the second, the revision the **first returned**, not the stale one the render closed over — which is FR-FT-033. Nothing is projected locally: the order the strip shows is whatever the backend's `state:patch` says, so a refusal part-way leaves the last confirmed order rather than a guess.
  - **ARIA decision: no `aria-grabbed`, no `aria-dropeffect`.** Both were deprecated in ARIA 1.1 and removed in 1.2, and no current screen reader acts on either; adding them would put a dead attribute on a `role="tab"` whose roving `tabIndex`, Home/End and Arrow handling already work and must not regress. The accessible equivalent of this drag is FR-FT-034's Move tab left/right, and a completed drag announces through the *same* polite live region and the same `editor.tab.moved` catalogue key (`en.json:43`) — `Moved {filename} to position {position} of {count}`, once per drag at the final resting position, not once per hop. The insertion indicator is `aria-hidden`: it is a picture of where a pointer is.
  - **Red-first evidence — the honest red.** Against the unbuilt component all five new unit cases fail: `Tests: 5 failed, 45 skipped, 50 total`, four of them reporting `expect(received).toBe(expected) / Expected: "2" / Received: undefined` on the absent `[data-tab-insertion-slot]`. The e2e case fails at `locator.boundingBox: Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded` because `[data-tab-item]` does not exist either. That red is weak by construction, which is why each clause was also **triangulated** against a plausible wrong implementation:
  - **Triangulation 1 — no grab threshold** (`TAB_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX = 0`). A two-pixel move is a click, not a grab; without a threshold every click on a tab becomes a zero-length drag. `expect(received).toBeNull() / Received: "1"` at the "under the threshold shows no indicator" assertion.
  - **Triangulation 2 — Escape hides the indicator but leaves the drag armed** (`setInsertionSlot(null)` in place of `endDrag()`). The release that follows then lands as a late drop: `expect(jest.fn()).not.toHaveBeenCalled() / Received number of calls: 2 / 1: "one", 1, 4 / 2: "one", 2, 5`. This is why the Escape case fires a `pointerup` *after* the key.
  - **Triangulation 3 — the naive single-hop reorder** (one unconditional `reorderDocument(documentId, finalIndex, tabSetRevision)`, no same-position guard, no revision chaining). Two reds at once: the drop case reports `Expected number of calls: 2 / Received number of calls: 1`, and the same-position case reports `Expected number of calls: 0 / Received number of calls: 1 / 1: "two", 1, 4`. That single call is exactly the trap the clause exists for — the backend answers it `noop`, the order does not change, and `tabTransitionSuccess` still hands back a tab-set revision. "The order looks unchanged" proves nothing; **no command at all** is the clause.
  - **Triangulation 4 — an optimistic local projection** (dispatch the reordered ids before issuing anything). `expect(received).toEqual(expected)` — the store held `["two","three","one"]` where the backend had confirmed nothing. The Redux store is a projection; the drop path writes to it never.
  - **Triangulation 5 — `pointer-events: none` on the indicator**, the obvious-looking choice for a drag decoration. It is the reason `expectPainted` is used rather than `toBeVisible`: `the tab drag insertion indicator is laid out at (466.578, 68.5) but the topmost paint there is div[role="tablist"]`. The bar is deliberately left hit-testable — the drop slot is measured from the tab boxes and the pointer's own coordinate, never from a hit test, so being hit-testable costs nothing and is what makes the paint check able to see it.
  - **Where each clause is proved, and why there.** Grab/threshold, insertion-slot arithmetic, the multi-hop drop with its revision chain, Escape-cancel and the same-position no-op are in `frontend/src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.test.tsx` (five cases, `T130 …`), because the **absence of a command** is only observable against a spy adapter. jsdom runs no layout, so those cases stub the strip's boxes at 100px per tab and dispatch `MouseEvent`s named `pointerdown`/`pointermove`/`pointerup` — jsdom 26 still ships no `PointerEvent` constructor, and `fireEvent.pointerDown` would fall back to a bare `Event` and lose `clientX`. The *painted* insertion indicator and the backend-confirmed reorder are in `frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts` (`T130 paints the drop position during a tab drag and reorders on release`), driven with the real mouse, because both depend on layout and neither can be seen from the accessibility tree.
  - **Not regressed:** the T145 tablist ownership, the roving `tabIndex`, and Home/End/Arrow handling are untouched; `data-tab-item` is an added attribute on the existing wrapper, and the indicator is a flex child that exists only while a drag is in flight, so no parity capture can contain it.
  - **Gates.** `just check` 79 suites / 618 tests (613 baseline + the 5 new unit cases; the sixth case is the Playwright one, which `just check` never runs), lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings in `DocumentIdentity.tsx` and `Launcher.tsx`. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1-M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` ok. `just e2e-test` 268 passed (267 baseline + 1), `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.
