- [X] T133 Run foreground external-change checks on window focus and resume, per FR-FT-020 (partial). Tab activation is covered — `internal/appmodel/tab_session.go:26,38` call `attachForegroundConflict` → `CheckExternalChanges` — but the focus/resume half of the clause is not. `main.go:114-135` registers only `OnStartup`, `OnShutdown` and `OnBeforeClose` with no focus or resume hook, and the bound `CheckExternalChanges` has no application caller: it appears in `frontend/src` only as the adapter passthrough at `logic/adapter/index.ts:310`, and there is no `focus` or `visibilitychange` listener anywhere. FR-FT-020 requires the check on tab activation, window focus **or resume**, and before writes, for every path-backed document including read-only ones — without introducing a watcher or polling timer.
  - **Closure.** Backend and adapter were already complete; what was missing was an event and a caller. Wails v2's `options.App` registers no focus or resume hook, so the webview is the only party that can observe either, and the bound `CheckExternalChanges` had no application caller at all. `frontend/src/ui/widgets/foregroundFocus.ts` gains `onApplicationForeground`, listening for `window` `focus` and for `visibilitychange` settling on `visible`, and `DocumentTabs` — which already owns the conflict prompt and already receives the conflict adapter — arms it and sweeps the open documents.
  - **Why not re-arm the existing helper.** `whenApplicationRegainsForegroundFocus` is one-shot by design and carries a one-second grace timer that fires when the foreground was never actually handed away. Re-arming it in a loop would convert that floor into a check every second, which is precisely the polling timer FR-FT-020 forbids. The new function lives in the same module, so foreground observation still has one home, but it answers a different question about it.
  - **Decision: every path-backed document, not only the active one.** FR-FT-020 says "for path-backed documents, including read-only documents", and the value of the sweep is exactly for the tabs nobody is looking at — the backend records `conflictBlocked` per document, which is what paints the strip's blocked marker and what refuses a later write. A document with no path has no disk version to compare and is skipped; read-only documents are not skipped, because the clause names them. Cost is bounded by the 40-document limit, the work is one `stat` per document unless the version actually moved, and one resume that raises both `focus` and `visibilitychange` is coalesced by an in-flight flag rather than doubling the filesystem work. The prompt opens only for the active document: `conflictPreview` is a single slot and FR-FT-021 describes one modal, so a background conflict waits behind its marker until activation, which runs the same check again.
  - **Red first.** `npx jest src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.test.tsx -t "T133"`: `expect(jest.fn()).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(expected) / Expected number of calls: 2 / Received number of calls: 0` for the sweep, and a `waitFor` timeout on `getByRole('dialog', { name: 'File changed on disk' })` for the prompt. Both tests assert their own negative first — nothing checked before the event, and no check from elapsed time alone under fake timers, before and after the listener is armed — so a wrong implementation that ran on mount or on an interval fails them too. The no-timer assertion is how the "MUST NOT introduce a … polling timer" half is proved rather than merely intended.
  - **The two dead aliases are routed, not deleted.** `attachForegroundConflict` now calls `CheckDocumentDisk`, which is the tab-activation occasion; `AppModelHandler.CheckExternalChanges` now calls `ForegroundCheck`, and after this task its only caller is the focus/resume listener, so each alias reads as the occasion its comment claims. `ForegroundCheck` joins `AppModelServiceAPI`; `handler_test.go`'s fake delegates it to its own `CheckExternalChanges` exactly as the real service does, so the panic-recovery table keeps arming and firing under the bound method's name. No new bound method and no binding regeneration: `CheckExternalChanges` was already on the wire and already reached the adapter.
  - **Deliberately not done.** No Go test asserts *which* alias each caller uses. The aliases are behaviourally identical by construction, so such a test would prove a name rather than a rule, and naming it `Proves: FR-FT-020` would be a false anchor — the requirement says nothing about function names. The occasion itself is proved in jsdom, where the event is.
  - **Fallout worth recording.** Two existing T142 cases dispatch a `window` `focus` event to prove FR-FT-037's deferred focus restoration, and with a real listener on that event they fell through to the production conflict adapter and called a Wails bridge that cannot exist under jsdom. They now pass an explicit `quietConflictAdapter()` rather than leaning on the sweep's error handling to hide the call. That handling exists for a real reason — the adapter throws while editor-state recovery holds the command surface — and abandoning the sweep is the specified remedy, since FR-FT-020 answers a check that could not run with another foreground check.
  - **Gates.** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites, 604 tests (+2), lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` exit 0. `just e2e-test` 267 passed, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T134 Drain accepted autosave and editor work before the close permit, per FR-FT-027 (partial). `AuthorizeQuit` (`internal/application/application_context_holder.go:193-196`) drains by calling `FlushBeforeClose`, which is only `service.FlushPendingUILayout()` (`:167-175`), and the permit is created immediately afterwards (`internal/application/close_coordinator.go:75-79`). FR-FT-027 requires cancelling in-flight long operations and draining "accepted layout, editor, and autosave work" before the one-use permit exists; no autosave flush or in-flight-write wait happens. The drain-failure path returns `apperr.IO("native close drain", err)`, which crosses as an untyped `WireError` in a `VoidResult` rather than the classified `io-failure` with `Retry` the requirement names.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16.** Both halves were real. Line numbers in the premise had drifted (`AuthorizeQuit` was at `:242`, `FlushBeforeClose` at `:208`, the permit at `close_coordinator.go:71-86`); the behaviour matched the description exactly.
  - **Root cause.** `FlushBeforeClose` is documented as the *Wails durability port* — one synchronous SQLite flush so the veto hook can refuse a close that would lose the layout. `AuthorizeQuit` reused it as if it were the whole shutdown drain. It is not: a debounced autosave the user had already earned stayed armed on a timer the process was about to destroy, and an explicit Save inside `AtomicReplace` was neither waited for nor cancelled. Both losses are silent — the permit is created, native Quit runs, and the accepted revision simply never reaches disk.
  - **The fix copies `PrepareClose`'s shape at process scope.** `AppModelService.DrainBeforeClose` (`internal/appmodel/close_drain.go`) walks the authoritative tab order and, per document, runs `flushAutosaveForClose` and then waits on the per-document `DocumentWriteCoordinator`; layout is flushed last. `ApplicationContextHolder.DrainBeforeClose` is the composition-root wrapper `AuthorizeQuit` now calls. `FlushBeforeClose` is unchanged and keeps its two original callers (`BeforeClose`'s fallback and `Close`).
  - **Three mechanisms, not one, and the difference is load-bearing.** Accepted autosave work is *run* — cancelling it strands the working copy. An in-flight write is *waited for*, never interrupted: `AtomicReplace` has no safe interruption point, so cutting one short produces the torn file the atomic path exists to prevent. Work whose document is no longer autosave-eligible is *cancelled*, and that is the requirement's "cancel in-flight long operations" clause — debounced work on a document that no longer qualifies is the only cancellable long-running work this backend owns. Nothing else in the backend takes a cancellation token, so no separate cancellation step was invented; see T175 for the clause this leaves unproven.
  - **The refusal changed shape too.** `apperr.IO` reached the frontend as an untyped `WireError` in a `VoidResult`, which `envelope.ts` turns into `notifyError` → `localizedErrorCopy` → generic catalogue copy, and a `WireError` names no remediation. So a window kept open by a failed drain showed two toasts (`unwrapPromise`'s and `reportNativeCloseError`'s) and offered nothing to press. `AuthorizeQuit` now returns the new `apperr.ClassifiedVoidResult`, carrying the classified `io-failure` with `Retry`; the adapter returns the `ClassifiedError` instead of throwing, `App.tsx` reports it through `reportClassifiedError`, and a new `quit` remediation intent re-asks the native frame to close. It re-issues the *request*, not the authorization — the coordinator cancelled the pending close, so `AuthorizeQuit` would refuse as stale.
  - **Red first, by triangulation.** The tests were written against an absent method (compile failure), then against a deliberately wrong `DrainBeforeClose` whose body was exactly today's production behaviour — `FlushPendingUILayout` alone. Verbatim:
    ```
    --- FAIL: TestDrainBeforeCloseRunsAcceptedAutosaveWork (0.00s)
        close_drain_test.go:58: bytes on disk after the drain = "base\n", want the last accepted revision
    --- FAIL: TestDrainBeforeCloseWaitsForAWriteInFlight (0.00s)
        close_drain_test.go:106: DrainBeforeClose returned <nil> while a write was still in flight
    --- FAIL: TestDrainBeforeCloseCancelsWorkThatCanNoLongerRun (0.00s)
        close_drain_test.go:158: pending debounces after the drain = 1, want the unrunnable one cancelled
    ```
    The frontend half was red separately — `expect(store.getState().notifications.items).toHaveLength(1)` received `0`, because the adapter change made the refusal reachable while `App.tsx` still discarded it.
  - **Every drain assertion runs under a real deadline.** `drainBeforeCloseWithin` fails in five seconds rather than at the package timeout, because `flushAutosaveMode` has spun forever once already on a scheduled document `runAutosave` declined and left in place. `TestDrainBeforeCloseCancelsWorkThatCanNoLongerRun` is the standing guard for that exact live-lock.
  - **Deliberately not done.** `FlushBeforeClose` was left narrow rather than widened into the full drain: `Close` runs after `AuthorizeQuit` has already drained, and `BeforeClose`'s fallback is the non-coordinator path that must stay a cheap synchronous veto. `CancelQuit` keeps its `VoidResult` — FR-FT-027 makes Cancel "a clean no-op that writes and closes nothing", so it has no classified failure to carry. The dev bridge mock's `AuthorizeQuit` still always succeeds; a mock that could refuse would invent a failure the browser bridge cannot produce.
  - **Gates.** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites, 612 tests (+1), lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` exit 0. `just e2e-test` 267 passed, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T135 Check the mixed-ending authorization before touching the disk in Save, per FR-FT-011 (contradicts). FR-FT-011 requires manual save, Save As, autosave and close-save to "refuse before disk access without matching authorization". `Save` does not: `internal/appmodel/save.go:68-73` runs `flushAutosave`, then `prepareWriteDisk` — which stats the file and can perform a full stable re-read through `inspectDocument` (`internal/appmodel/conflict.go:283-296`) — and only then reaches `snapshotForWrite`, where the authorization is actually checked (`save.go:250-257`). `SaveAs` gets this right, checking at `save.go:96-104` before any disk work; copy that ordering. Additionally `CancelNormalization` (`save.go:223`) has no non-test caller, so a cancelled normalization leaves its single-use token in `service.normalizations` for the process lifetime.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16.** Both halves were real, and the premise's own qualification held: the mixed-ending gate inside `snapshotForWrite` does already precede every *write*, so no unauthorized bytes ever reached a file. What preceded nothing was the *disk inspection*.
  - **Root cause (Save):** `Save` released the read lock after reading the path, then ran `flushAutosave` and `prepareWriteDisk`, and only reached the authorization inside `snapshotForWrite`. The gate now runs inside the same read-lock region that already validates revision and capability, exactly as `SaveAs` does. An untitled document still falls through to `SaveAs`, which runs the identical gate, so the check is not duplicated for it.
  - **Ordering is not cosmetic**, which is why there are two tests rather than one. The counter test proves no disk seam is reached; the behavioural test proves what the user saw. With the inspection running first, an unauthorized Save of a mixed-ending document whose file had *also* changed underneath returned `conflict` and never mentioned the line endings, so the normalization the requirement demands was never requested at all.
  - **Red-first**, both from `internal/appmodel/save_test.go`:

    ```
    --- FAIL: TestSaveRefusesMixedEndingsBeforeTouchingTheDisk (0.00s)
        save_test.go:370: unauthorized Save reached the disk: 1 disk-version reads and 0 stable reads, want 0 and 0
    --- FAIL: TestUnauthorizedSaveAsksForNormalizationEvenWhenTheFileAlsoChanged (0.00s)
        save_test.go:400: Save status = "conflict", want "needs-normalization" — the authorization gate must precede the disk inspection
    ```

  - **Root cause (autosave leak):** `runAutosave` calls `snapshotForWrite` with an empty decision token. On a mixed-ending document that never matches, so the unauthorized branch minted a fresh single-use authorization into `service.normalizations` and returned it with the refusal — which autosave discarded, having no prompt to show. One token leaked per debounce that fired, for the process lifetime. `runAutosave` now routes the refusal through `CancelNormalization`, which is what it was written for and which had no caller at all, not even a test.
  - **Red-first**, from `internal/appmodel/autosave_test.go`:

    ```
    --- FAIL: TestRefusedAutosaveLeavesNoNormalizationAuthorizationBehind (0.00s)
        autosave_test.go:317: normalization authorizations after 1 refused autosave(s) = 1, want 0
    ```

  - **Deliberately not done — close-save was checked and left alone.** `executeClosePlanWrite` (`close_plan.go:377`) also calls `snapshotForWrite` and can receive a minted token, but it *returns* the result to its caller rather than dropping it, so the token reaches a consumer. It is not the same defect and widening the change to it would have been speculative.
  - **Deliberately not done — the token minted by `RequestNormalization` on the new early-refusal path is not cancelled.** That is the designed prompt flow: the token is handed to the frontend, which either authorizes the save with it or abandons it. Reaping abandoned prompt tokens is a different question from this leak and is not in FR-FT-011.
  - **Gates:** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites / 601 frontend tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS; `go test -race ./internal/... .` clean. No `frontend/` or `e2e/` change, so `just e2e-test` was not required.

- [X] T136 Let an in-flight write finish before closing, and cancel the closed document's autosave timer, per FR-FT-024 (partial). FR-FT-024 requires that closing a clean document not prompt but that "any pending working-copy flush or in-flight autosave MUST complete successfully before the document is removed". `CloseDocument` (`internal/appmodel/tab_session.go:64-81`) instead **refuses** when `document.writeInFlight`, and never calls `flushAutosaveForClose` for a pending debounce — compare `PrepareClose` (`internal/appmodel/close_plan.go:81-83`), which does. Separately `closeDocuments` (`close_plan.go:526-583`) deletes the document without `cancelAutosaveLocked`, so its entry in `service.autosaveTimers` is never removed.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16**, with one addition the task did not name and could not have been done without — see the third bullet.
  - **Root cause (CloseDocument):** it evaluated `Dirty` before flushing anything. A tab whose autosave debounce was still pending is dirty *now* and clean a moment later, so the close was refused with "prepare a close plan first" — a prompt for work the application had already accepted and was about to write. The flush now runs between two validation passes, mirroring `PrepareClose`: validate, release the lock, flush, re-validate revision and existence, then decide.
  - **`writeInFlight` was narrowed, not deleted.** After the flush, an in-flight autosave is finished, so a surviving `writeInFlight` can only be a concurrent *explicit* save. FR-FT-024 names "in-flight autosave"; it does not ask for a document to be removed out from under a save that is mid-commit, and `executeWrite` writes back into `service.state.documents` after the replace. Refusing that narrow case with `Retry` is deliberate.
  - **A hang had to be fixed first, or this task would have shipped one.** `flushAutosaveMode` loops until the debounce is claimed, but `runAutosave` declines a document that is no longer autosave-eligible — detached, read-only, path cleared — and *leaves the entry in place* when it declines, so the loop re-read an unchanged entry forever. It is reachable today from `PrepareClose`: edit a document, have its file disappear so the document detaches, then close the window. T136 asks for that same call to be added to `CloseDocument`, which would have widened a window-close hang to ordinary tab close. The loop now cancels an entry that survived `runAutosave` and returns, so it always makes progress. Confirmed by probe before it was fixed: `flushAutosaveForClose` did not return in 3s on a detached document with a pending debounce.
  - **Red-first**, all three from `internal/appmodel/tab_session_test.go`:

    ```
    --- FAIL: TestClosingATabFlushesItsPendingAutosaveInsteadOfPrompting (0.00s)
        tab_session_test.go:160: CloseDocument on a tab with a pending autosave = &{Category:conflict SafeSubject:document Message:The document has unsaved changes; prepare a close plan first. Remediations:[Retry] DocumentID:doc-0000000000000003 DedupKey:doc-0000000000000003:conflict}, want a silent close
    --- FAIL: TestClosingDocumentsCancelsTheirAutosaveTimers (0.00s)
        tab_session_test.go:196: autosave timer entries for the removed document = 1, want 0
    --- FAIL: TestFlushingForCloseTerminatesWhenTheDocumentBecameIneligible (5.00s)
        tab_session_test.go:227: flushAutosaveForClose did not return within 5s: the debounce it cannot run is never cancelled, so the close spins forever
    ```

  - **Root cause (timer leak):** `closeDocuments` deleted the document from state without cancelling its debounce, leaving an entry in `service.autosaveTimers` keyed by an id that no longer resolves. `cancelAutosaveLocked` drops straight into the delete loop, which already holds `service.mu` — the `…Locked` suffix is the convention that says it must not re-acquire it, and `go test -race` over the package proves the caller really holds it.
  - **The timer test exercises `closeDocuments` directly**, and says so in its comment. Once the flush is in place, `CloseDocument` no longer reaches that function with a live timer, so driving it through the public command would have proved nothing. `closeDocuments` is the shared removal primitive behind both `CloseDocument` and `ExecuteClosePlan`, and the invariant — no autosave timer outlives the document it names — belongs to it rather than to either caller.
  - **Deliberately not done — the dirty-with-no-pending-autosave path is unchanged.** It still routes to a close plan, which is what FR-FT-024 requires; only the case where accepted work was already queued became a silent close.
  - **Gates:** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites / 601 frontend tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS; `go test -race ./internal/... .` clean. No `frontend/` or `e2e/` change, so `just e2e-test` was not required.

- [X] T137 Remove or route the Go symbols no production path calls, per the constitution's "Production paths MUST contain no placeholder, stub, or no-op" (unrequested). Fourteen exported symbols have no non-test importer anywhere, verified by repository-wide grep excluding `_test.go`: `appmodel.KeepMine` (`conflict.go:189`), `appmodel.ForegroundCheck` and `appmodel.CheckDocumentDisk` (`conflict.go:78,82`), `appmodel.CancelPreparedOpen` (`file_lifecycle.go:195`), `appmodel.CancelNormalization` (`save.go:223`), the entire `ProjectionRecovery` type (`resync_recovery.go`, see T124), `SqliteRecentFilesRepository.Remove` (`recent_files_repository_sqlite.go:78`), `file.DecodeDocument` and `file.NewDocumentSnapshot` (`codec.go:32,44`), `file.ReadClassifiedDocument` (`document_reader.go:249`), `file.CanonicalizeExisting` and `file.CanonicalizeCandidate` (`paths.go:52,91`), `file.CurrentVersion` (`disk_version.go:49`), and every method of `file.DocumentFileService` (`document_reader.go:112-131`). Each is exercised only by its own test, so those tests prove arithmetic rather than behaviour — the self-asserting shape already recorded against T099 and T110.
  - **Decide per symbol**: delete it, or route the production path through it. Several — `KeepMine`, `CancelPreparedOpen`, `CancelNormalization`, `CanonicalizeExisting` — name behaviour the specification requires, so their absence from a production path may be a coverage gap rather than dead code. Check before deleting.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16, per the owner's "decide per symbol" decision.** Sixteen declarations deleted here, one kept and filed, three excluded because they were never dead, and ten more already removed by T124.
  - **The task's count of fourteen is wrong in both directions, and the corrected figure is 27.** Three of the fourteen named symbols have production callers and are not dead at all. The other eleven entries include two collectives — "the entire `ProjectionRecovery` type" and "every method of `file.DocumentFileService`" — which expand to ten and six declarations. Counting declarations rather than list entries, and adding `DocumentSnapshot.Encode` and the `RecentFilesRepository.Remove` interface member that the list did not name: **27 exported declarations had no non-test caller**, of which 26 are now gone and one is kept deliberately.
  - **Per-symbol verdicts.**

    | Symbol | Verdict | Why |
    |---|---|---|
    | `appmodel.ForegroundCheck` | **not dead — excluded** | Called at `internal/appmodel/handler.go:303` from the bound `CheckExternalChanges`, and required by the `AppModelServiceAPI` interface at `:35`. The focus/resume work routed it. |
    | `appmodel.CheckDocumentDisk` | **not dead — excluded** | Called at `internal/appmodel/tab_session.go:49` on every `ActivateDocument`. The same work routed it. |
    | `appmodel.CancelNormalization` | **not dead — excluded** | Called at `internal/appmodel/autosave.go:223`. T135's autosave-token-leak fix routed it. |
    | `appmodel.ProjectionRecovery` + `NewProjectionRecovery` + `Start`/`Retry`/`Surface`/`RequestQuitAndDiscard`/`ConfirmQuitAndDiscard`/`QuitAndDiscardAuthorized` + `RecoverySurface` + `RecoveryQuitPrompt` (10) | **deleted by T124** | The owner's recorded decision. |
    | `appmodel.KeepMine` | **delete** | A one-line alias for `AuthorizeKeepMine`, which is already bridge-exposed (`handler.go:323` → `wailsjs` → `services.ts:216`) and is what the frontend calls. So the behaviour is routed under the other name, and this form was strictly worse: it discards the caller's context and substitutes `context.Background()`, which would have detached the state patch it publishes from the Wails lifecycle. |
    | `appmodel.CancelPreparedOpen` | **keep — filed as T174** | The one symbol here that names a clause of the specification. FR-FT-004 says a reservation is held "until activation, commit, **cancellation**, or failure", and this is the only implementation of the third. Deleting it deletes a requirement, not scaffolding. It is unreachable for a structural reason: the prepare/commit boundary never crosses the bridge, so nothing exists to cancel between the two — verified, not assumed, by reading every branch (`OpenPath` commits on the next line; every `PrepareOpen` refusal returns before a reservation is created; `CommitPreparedOpen` deletes the reservation on every path, so no reservation leaks either). A comment at the declaration now records this so a later pass does not delete it. |
    | `appmodel.SqliteRecentFilesRepository.Remove` **and** the `RecentFilesRepository.Remove` interface member (2) | **delete** | Checked against the requirement before deleting, because "a recent path no longer exists → the stale entry is removed" is a real acceptance scenario (`spec.md:590`). It is already satisfied elsewhere: `List` stats every stored path, drops the missing ones and writes the pruned list back — which is exactly the "lazily" the scenario asks for. `Remove` was a second route to the same outcome with no caller. Deleting the impl alone would have left an interface method nothing implements usefully, so the member and the two test doubles' implementations go with it. |
    | `file.DecodeDocument` | **delete** | Not an alias — a second, independent decoder, and one that **contradicts FR-FT-006**. It returns an error for invalid UTF-8, a NUL byte and a lone CR; the requirement says such input "MUST open tolerantly as clearly read-only", which is what the shipped `ReadClassified`/`classifyDocumentBytes` path does. Keeping it kept a trap: the next caller to reach for the obvious name would have got the forbidden behaviour. |
    | `file.NewDocumentSnapshot` | **delete** | Production's New path builds its snapshot in `internal/appmodel`; nothing has ever constructed one this way. |
    | `file.DocumentSnapshot.Encode` | **delete** | The method form. Production calls `file.EncodeDocument(...)` directly (`internal/appmodel/save.go:500`). The `DocumentSnapshot` type itself is live and stays. |
    | `file.ReadClassifiedDocument` | **delete** | A one-line alias for `ReadClassified(path, MaxClassifiedReadBytes)`, which production calls with the limit spelled out. |
    | `file.DocumentFileService` + `NewDocumentFileService` + `CanonicalizeExisting` + `CanonicalizeCandidate` + `ReadClassified` + `ReadClassifiedStable` (6) | **delete** | Its own comment says it "provides the filesystem capabilities used by later lifecycle slices". Those slices arrived and used the package functions instead; the type has **zero references of any kind** outside its own declaration — no construction, no test, no interface satisfaction. Every method was a one-line forward to the function the callers already use. |
    | `file.CanonicalizeExisting` (package function) | **delete** | Alias for `CanonicalizeDocumentPath`. Confirmed the task's claim of a double declaration: this and the `DocumentFileService` method were independent dead copies, not a chain — the method forwards to `CanonicalizeDocumentPath`, not to this alias. |
    | `file.CanonicalizeCandidate` (package function) | **delete** | Alias for `CanonicalizeCandidateDocumentPath`, which has seven production callers. Same double-declaration shape. |
    | `file.CurrentVersion` | **delete** | Alias for `CurrentDiskVersion`, which has sixteen production callers. |

  - **Deleting a symbol means deleting or repointing its test, and the two are not the same choice.** Where the behaviour under test is gone, the test goes; where only the *entry point* is gone and the behaviour is production-reachable, the test is repointed, because deleting it would lose real coverage.
    - **Repointed.** `internal/file/document_reader_test.go` drove `ReadClassifiedDocument`. Its subjects — FR-FT-005's size thresholds and FR-FT-007's line-ending classification — are proved against the shipped reader, so both cases now call a local `readClassifiedAtDefaultLimit` helper that is the deleted alias's body. No assertion changed.
    - **Rewritten.** `internal/file/codec_test.go` reached `EncodeDocument` through `DecodeDocument` and the `Encode` method. `EncodeDocument` is production code, so the encode-side assertions survive with snapshots written out literally — which is also how production builds them, from a `ClassifiedRead`. The decode-side cases are gone: they asserted the strict behaviour FR-FT-006 forbids, and classification is proved against the real reader next door. `TestEncodeRefusesMixedAndUnsafeContent` picks up the unsupported-encoding branch the old file never reached.
    - **Deleted outright.** `TestNewDocumentCodecUsesLFWithoutAddingTrailingNewline` — its only subjects were `NewDocumentSnapshot` and `Encode`, both gone. Its substance, "a `none` file gains no terminator until a break is inserted", is FR-FT-010 and is still proved by `TestNoneEndingPreservedUntilBreakInserted`.
  - **No red-first cycle, deliberately.** This task removes code rather than adding behaviour; the proof that it is safe is that the whole gate stays green with the symbols gone, plus the per-symbol reachability audit above. The three excluded symbols were verified by grep against the current tree, not taken from the task's text — which had them wrong.
  - **Deliberately not done.** `file.CanonicalizeDocumentPath` was left exported although its only callers are inside `internal/file`: unexporting it is a rename, not a dead-code removal, and it is genuinely called. `WriteCoordinator` (the type alias for `DocumentWriteCoordinator`) was left alone for the same reason — it is used. `internal/appmodel/open_lifecycle_test.go` still calls `CancelPreparedOpen`; those cases prove FR-FT-004's reservation and capacity rules and stay with the symbol.
  - **Gates.** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites, 613 tests (unchanged; no test was added or removed net), lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1-M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` exit 0. `just e2e-test` not re-run: this task touches no file under `frontend/` or `e2e/`.


- [X] T138 Stop branching the production DOM on the parity route, per FR-FT-054 and FR-FT-055 (contradicts). FR-FT-054 permits a populated fixture to be seeded only on the deterministic parity route; it does not permit a different component structure. `frontend/src/ui/primitives/ModalShell.tsx:48-52` reads `?parity-case` from `window.location.search` in production code and `:195-197` portals the surface to `document.body` only when that flag is present **and** `window.innerWidth <= 376`; `SettingsDialog.tsx:261,397` does the same. Production ships the inline structure while the harness measures the portalled one, so neither the pixel comparison nor any behavioural assertion taken on that route is measuring the shipped render path. **This is the mock-divergence class that hid T104 and T107, relocated into production code.**

  - **Line drift.** `ModalShell.tsx` held the two route constants at `:47-51` and the portal conditional at `:195-197`; `SettingsDialog.tsx` at `:260-262` and `:397-399`. Located by content.
  - **Worse than the task text, in two ways — one confirmed, one corrected.** (a) **Confirmed and it is the more serious half:** the parity route did not only relocate the surface, it *removed the accessibility contract*. `ModalShell` forced `aria-label`, `aria-modal` and `role` to `undefined` on the section and moved `role="dialog"` onto the backdrop, so every measurement taken on that route described a dialog that does not ship. (b) **Corrected:** `SettingsDialog`'s `narrowParityRoute` is indeed computed without the `?parity-case` guard, but it is *read* only inside `if (parityTab !== undefined)`, which is itself the parity branch — so production never portalled at ≤376px. The asymmetry is real as written and misleading to read; it was not a live production defect. Recorded here rather than repeated as fact.
  - **A third file, found by the fix and folded in.** `ClosePrompt.tsx:28-32,58,67` carried its own `?parity-case` branch: a document-specific heading and a raw untranslated English message. Because `aria-labelledby` points at that heading, the substitution changed the dialog's **accessible name** on the parity route — and `ModalShell`'s parity branch had been compensating by putting `aria-label={title}` on the backdrop-as-dialog. Removing one without the other is what turned three e2e cases red. It is the same rule in the same route (and the message also breaks FR-FT-047, T154's rule), so it was fixed rather than deferred; deferring it would have meant editing three test locators to accommodate a defect.
  - **Red-first evidence.** `● T138 renders one modal structure and one accessibility contract on every route` / `- Expected - 1 / + Received + 1` / `-   "shellParentIsBody": true, / +   "shellParentIsBody": false,` — the production route was the one that failed first, because it was the one that did not portal. The case compares a seven-field structural descriptor (role, `aria-modal`, `aria-label`, portal parent, backdrop role, backdrop `aria-hidden`, whether the backdrop wraps the shell) across `/`, `?parity-case` at 1280 and `?parity-case` at 375, and requires all three to be identical.
  - **What "portal unconditionally" resolved to.** One structure everywhere: an `aria-hidden` backdrop and a sibling `role="dialog" aria-modal` section, both portalled to `document.body`. The portal is the half of the parity branch that was *right* — `.overlay` and `.content` are `position: fixed`, and a transformed or filtered ancestor turns a fixed descendant into an absolute one against that ancestor's box, which at the 375px minimum window is the difference between a centred dialog and a clipped one. `SettingsDialog` now portals on both of its branches for the same reason.
  - **Which parity keys moved: none.** All **14 pixel-compared keys pass unchanged** — `[parity accounting] 150/150 attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted` — and no attributed residual grew, shrank or was added. All **36 behaviour-verified keys** pass, and `[parity states] 40/40`. That is the expected result once measured rather than assumed: no dialog is one of the 14 component keys, so the branch was never protecting a pixel comparison. What it was protecting was three *behavioural* cases, below.
  - **The five cases that did move, and the verdict on each. Every one is the harness finally measuring the shipped structure; none is production drift.**
    - `real-files-and-tabs.test.ts` **T051** and `real-files-parity.test.ts` **T057** — `locator.click … <div aria-hidden="true" class="_overlay_…" data-modal-backdrop="true"> intercepts pointer events`. Both locate the close prompt by `getByRole('dialog', { name: 'Save changes before closing?' })`, find nothing once the dialog is named by its heading, never click Discard, and then try to close a second tab through an open modal's backdrop. **Harness measuring the shipped name.** Fixed at source by removing `ClosePrompt`'s heading substitution; no locator was changed.
    - `narrow-width.test.ts` **T084 768px normalization prompt** — same locator, same cause, timing out on the prompt's Save button. **Same verdict, same fix.**
    - `ClosePrompt.test.tsx` `T045 keeps the parity close prompt locator name while showing the reviewed heading` — this case asserted the divergence by name. Rewritten as `T138 gives the close prompt one dialog element on the parity route and off it`, comparing a structural descriptor across both routes.
    - `AppearanceControls.test.tsx` `does not broadcast a reset into another mounted acknowledged Appearance projection` — `within(first.container).getByRole('button', { name: 'Reset appearance' })`. A genuine consequence of portalling: with two projections mounted, a portalled dialog is no longer inside its own projection's container. Only one dialog is open at that point, so the query is unscoped; the isolation the case exists for is still asserted container-scoped, on the *second* projection's menu.

  - **Gates:** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites / **611** frontend tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS; `just e2e-test` **267 passed**, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted`. The intermediate run at **3 failed / 263 passed** is recorded above rather than hidden — it is the measurement this task was asked to take.

Failures upload `frontend/test-results` and `frontend/playwright-report` as an artifact, because a red pixel gate with no difference image is not diagnosable. `release-skeleton` now needs **both** jobs.

(2) **`just archtest` was omitted** — CI inlined eight of `check`'s nine steps and dropped the one the justfile calls never-suppressed, so the Go archtest, `cgo-free-check`, `migration-immutability-check` and `frontend-archtest` never ran there. An `Architecture` step now sits between `Go vet` and `Go race tests`, matching `just check`'s order exactly (verified step-by-step against `just --show check`). (3) **`just check` called itself "a mirror of the CI gate set"** — it never was, and with the interface job it is now a strict subset. The comment says that, and names `just e2e-test` as the thing `check` cannot see.
  - **The fail-open hazard, and which way it was resolved: both.** `accounting-teardown.ts` treated zero recorded states — and zero recorded captures — as a silent pass, so any `--project=`- or `-g`-narrowed invocation reported success having measured nothing. CI runs the **full** `playwright test`, *and* the teardown now **fails closed whenever `CI` is set**, which GitHub Actions sets for us. Both, rather than either, because running the full suite today does not stop someone narrowing the command tomorrow; the guard is what makes the narrowing visible.        at e2e/parity/accounting-teardown.ts:39
      1 passed (1.4s)
