- [X] T140 Report a failed outgoing flush instead of discarding it, per FR-FT-031 (partial). The ordering is correct — `App.tsx:501-508` awaits `flushActiveSession` for the outgoing id before `activateDocument` — but the failure outcome is lost. `flushActiveSession` rejects on an activation-token mismatch (`logic/adapter/appModelAdapter.ts:596-601`), that rejection propagates out of `DocumentTabs.tsx:174-196`, and all three call sites discard it with `void activateDocument(...)` (`DocumentTabs.tsx:274`, `:455`, `:479`). FR-FT-031 requires the failure to be shown; the user instead sees an unresponsive tab click and an unhandled promise rejection. Separately `DocumentTabs.tsx:176-178` falls back to `adapter.activateDocument` with **no outgoing flush at all** when `onActivateDocument` is not supplied.
  - **Closure.** The root cause was a failure that had no shape to travel in. `flushActiveSession` rejects with a plain `Error` on an activation-token mismatch, a plain `Error` is not a `ClassifiedError`, and the reporting funnel in `DocumentTabs.activateDocument` only ever inspects `result.error` — so the one path a failure could take was the one nobody was watching, and all three call sites discarded it with `void`. The user got a tab click that did nothing and an unhandled promise rejection.
  - **Where the shaping goes, and why.** At the caller, not at the adapter. A new `frontend/src/ui/widgets/outgoingFlush.ts` exposes `flushOutgoingDocument`, which awaits the flush and returns either `undefined` or a `conflict` classified refusal carrying `Retry`; the caller turns that into an ordinary `{ error }` outcome the existing funnel already knows how to show. Shaping it in the adapter was rejected for three reasons: the rejection never came from Go, so classifying it there would invent a wire-shaped result for a client-side condition; `flushActiveSession` has other callers — the close paths and the entry commands — for which "the switch was not made" is the wrong sentence; and a `ClassifiedError` needs a `dedupKey`, a category and a remediation set that only the caller can choose honourably. `conflict` is the category because an activation-token mismatch is staleness rather than an IO failure, and because the contract's `conflict` row is the one that pairs with `Retry` — and the retry is a real command here: re-issuing the same switch against a settled session.
  - **Reported from both entry paths, shown once.** The strip's funnel does not see the `Retry` remediation's calls, which reach `App.tsx`'s `onActivateDocument` directly, so that handler reports the refusal as well as returning it. `enqueueToast` collapses reports sharing a `code` and a `subject` and merges their controls rather than replacing them (`logic/store/notificationsSlice.ts:141-150,193-200`), so the shared `activate:outgoing-flush` dedup key makes one failure one toast however many layers noticed it.
  - **The fallback's missing flush is fixed too.** With no `onActivateDocument` supplied, `DocumentTabs` went straight to `adapter.activateDocument`, dropping the outgoing document's newest caret, selection, scroll and view state. It now runs the same `flushOutgoingDocument` first, against the projection's `activeDocumentId`. A `catch` around the whole activation is the backstop for what is not the flush — the adapter throws rather than refuses while editor-state recovery holds the command surface — and reports the same refusal, because the user-visible outcome is identical.
  - **Red first.** Four cases, all failing before the change. `npx jest src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.test.tsx -t "T140"`: "reports a failed switch and leaves the outgoing tab active" — `Expected length: 1 / Received length: 0 / Received array: []`; "flushes the outgoing document before the adapter fallback activates" — `- "flush:one"` missing from `["activate"]`; "refuses the fallback switch when the outgoing flush rejects" — `Expected length: 1 / Received length: 0`. And `npx jest src/App.test.tsx -t "T140"`: `Expected length: 1 / Received length: 0` for a notification with subject `activate:outgoing-flush`.
  - **Both consequences asserted, not just the report.** Every case asserts the outgoing tab is still active (`documents.activeDocumentId` unchanged) and that the incoming activation was never issued. The App case asserts "installs no incoming content" literally: the acknowledgement its `activateDocument` double would have returned carries `incoming content that must not install`, and the active-buffer output still reads `outgoing content`.
  - **Deliberately not done.** `onRemediate`'s `activate-document` arm returns silently on `result.error !== undefined`, so a *backend* activation refusal met on the Retry path is still unreported — the original toast simply stays up. That is a pre-existing gap covering every activation refusal rather than the flush failure this task names, and it is filed as T170 rather than widened into here. The flush failure itself is reported on that path, because `onActivateDocument` reports it directly.
  - **Gates.** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites, 608 tests (+4), lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` exit 0. `just e2e-test` 267 passed, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T141 Close a tab on middle-click, per FR-FT-034 (missing). FR-FT-034 requires that "the close affordance, middle-click, and tab-specific Close action MUST close the targeted tab". No middle-click handler exists: a repository-wide grep over `frontend/src` for `onAuxClick`, `auxclick` and `button === 1` returns nothing, and the only `onMouseDown` handlers are in `Segmented.tsx:83` and `EditorChrome.tsx:112`. The tab button (`DocumentTabs.tsx:464-506`) carries `onClick` and `onContextMenu` only. Route it through the same `closeDocument` path as the close affordance so the dirty-close prompt still applies.

  - **Line drift.** The tab button was at `DocumentTabs.tsx:500-542`, not `464-506`. Everything else in the brief was accurate: `onAuxClick`, `auxclick` and `button === 1` were all absent from `frontend/src`.
  - **Root cause.** Not a broken handler — an absent one. The tab button carried `onClick` and `onContextMenu`, and nothing in the tree listened for an auxiliary button anywhere. The interesting part of the fix is therefore not the handler but where it points. `DocumentTabs` has two close routes: the local `closeDocument` callback, which prefers the shell's `onCloseDocument` and only falls back to `adapter.closeDocument`, and the adapter itself. The dirty-close prompt lives on the shell funnel. A middle-click wired to the adapter would have satisfied the letter of "closes the targeted tab" while discarding unsaved work without asking, and no tab-count assertion would have noticed.
  - **What changed.** The close affordance's inline handler was lifted into a single `closeTargetedTab(documentId)` callback — same call, same `'single'` plan, same one-element target list, same post-close focus follow-up — and both the close control and the new `onAuxClick` now call it. There is one close path for the two pointer affordances rather than two that happen to agree.
  - **Red-first evidence, unit.** `T141 closes the targeted tab on middle-click, through the shell close path` failed verbatim: `expect(jest.fn()).toHaveBeenCalledWith(...expected)` / `Expected: "one", 4, "single", ["one"]` / `Number of calls: 0`. A first attempt failed earlier still, on `TypeError: react_1.fireEvent.auxClick is not a function` — this version of `@testing-library/dom` has no `auxClick` shorthand, so the auxiliary click is constructed as a real `MouseEvent('auxclick', { button })` and dispatched through the generic `fireEvent`. That detail is recorded because the next person to test an auxiliary button will hit it.
  - **Red-first evidence, e2e.** `T141 middle-click closes the targeted tab through the same dirty-close prompt` drives the `?close-plan` route, dirties the document, middle-clicks the tab, and waits for the prompt. Against the unmodified component it failed with `Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded` / `Error: locator.evaluate: Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded.` / `Call log: - waiting for getByRole('dialog', { name: 'Save changes before closing?' })`. It asserts the *prompt*, not the tab count, precisely because the tab count cannot tell the two routes apart.
  - **Anchor corrected, not added.** `real-files-and-tabs.test.ts:56` carried `Proves: FR-FT-034 (partial — … middle-click close is unbuilt, see T141)`. Leaving that in place while the clause became true would have left a false record of a gap; it now names the test that covers it.
  - **What was deliberately not done.** No middle-click on the close control or the `+` control, and no `onMouseDown` autoscroll suppression beyond `preventDefault()` on the auxiliary click itself — FR-FT-034 names the tab, and nothing else was invented around it. A right-button auxiliary click is asserted *not* to close, so the handler cannot quietly widen to every non-primary button.
  - **Gates.** `just check` 79 suites / 579 tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test` 263 passed (262 + the new case), `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T142 Complete the Reveal focus and remediation contract, per FR-FT-037 (partial). Two gaps. **(a)** Focus is restored immediately rather than once the application regains foreground focus: `TabContextMenu.tsx:169-173` ends the dispatch with `.finally((): void => { onClose(); })` and `DocumentTabs.tsx:574-581` focuses the originating tab synchronously, with nothing listening for a window `focus` event — FR-FT-037 requires the restoration to wait, "since the file manager may briefly own it". **(b)** The required remediation **pairs** cannot be produced: `logic/store/classifiedNotification.ts:56-62` maps at most one remediation and never maps `Save to recreate`, so the detached `not-found` cannot offer `Save to recreate` plus `Copy path`, and a Reveal `system-command-failure` cannot offer `Retry` plus `Copy path`. Depends on T125's widened `ClassifiedError`.

  - **Root cause, gap (a).** The context menu ended its dispatch with `.finally(onClose)` and `onClose` focused the originating tab synchronously, so the outcome of the command never reached the focus decision — a Reveal the OS accepted and a Reveal it refused restored focus identically. FR-FT-037's closing clause requires the successful case to wait, "since the file manager may briefly own it". Grepping `frontend/src` for a `focus` listener confirmed the brief: there was none, anywhere, so there was nothing for the restoration to wait on and no seam to hang one from.
  - **Root cause, gap (b).** Two seams, not one. `Notification.remediation` was a single optional value, so a toast structurally could not carry a pair however faithfully Go sent one, and `Toast.tsx` rendered exactly one button. Underneath that, `remediationFor` could not have produced an honest pair even with somewhere to put it: `Retry` took its command from the caller's single `intent`, and the only intent the Reveal caller could name was `copy-path` — so a Retry built from the set would have carried the copy-path command, two buttons would have run the same thing under different labels, and that is precisely why the shipped mapping preferred `Copy path` and dropped `Retry` rather than render a lie. The missing piece was a `reveal` intent with a real command behind it.
  - **Red-first evidence, gap (a).** `DocumentTabs.test.tsx` gained a case driving a successful Reveal and asserting the tab is not focused until a window `focus` event arrives. It failed verbatim: `expect(element).not.toHaveFocus()` / `Received element is focused: <button aria-label="⁨one.md⁩" aria-selected="true" class="tab" data-document-id="one" role="tab" tabindex="0" title="/repo/one.md" type="button">…`. Its companion — a *refused* Reveal must restore focus at once — passed before the change and is a regression guard, not a proof of new behaviour; that is stated here rather than implied by a green tick.
  - **Red-first evidence, gap (b).** Five failures across two files, all `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'map')` on `state.items[0].remediations` — the field did not exist. Reported as `Tests: 5 failed, 5 passed, 10 total`. The shape of that red is the finding: the dedup-erasure case and the Reveal-pair case could not even be *expressed* against the old single-value type, which is why neither had ever been caught.
  - **The dedup hazard, fixed at the seam.** `refreshDuplicate` assigned `duplicate.remediation = incoming.remediation` wholesale, and the in-source warnings at `App.tsx:99-100` and `:289-291` describe the consequence: an intent-less second report erased the control the first had earned, behind a `×2` that read like the contract's dedup count working. It now merges by `action` — a repeat never withdraws a control, and a control the repeat brings is added once. Two dedicated tests pin both directions, and the e2e Reveal case re-asserts the surviving `Copy path` with `expectPainted` after a Retry that increments the count.
  - **What earned the new control.** `reveal` joins `NotificationRemediationIntent` and `App.tsx`'s exhaustive switch, running `appModelAdapter.revealInFileManager(documentId)` and dismissing the toast on OS acceptance per FR-FT-037. `Copy path` no longer takes its intent from the caller at all — it names its own command, so it needs only a document id, which also retired the `reveal?: boolean` option that used to gate it. The e2e proof clicks Retry under `?refuseReveal`, where every attempt is refused, so the count incrementing to `×2` is observable evidence that the button re-issued *Reveal*; a Retry still wired to copy-path would have copied the path and dismissed the toast instead.
  - **Deliberately NOT done, and filed as T160.** `Save to recreate` is not mapped, so the detached `not-found` pair still renders only `Copy path`. Nothing in the frontend can run it. `App.tsx`'s `beginWrite` refuses a detached document outright (`:1125`) with a `permission-denied` "read only" message — which contradicts **FR-FT-023**, "allow explicit Save to recreate the same path", and acceptance scenario 6 at `spec.md:546`, while Go already supports it (`internal/appmodel/save.go:340` clears `detached` after a successful write). Separately, `beginWrite` writes only the *active* document and ignores any id handed to it, so a control on a toast naming a background tab would save a different file. Rendering the member anyway would be the exact defect T116 exists to remove, so it is dropped, the drop is pinned by a test, and **T160** carries both blockers with the FR-FT-023 defect stated as live in its own right. This is the one clause of T142 that could not be honestly closed.
  - **Not done, and why:** the toast's own `reveal` remediation does not defer focus restoration. FR-FT-037 ties that wait to the context menu ("Invoking either action MUST close the open menu and return focus"), and a toast button closes no menu; extending it would be invented behaviour.
  - **Changed beyond the brief.** `DocumentTabs.test.tsx`'s Reveal-failure double sent `remediations: ['Copy path']` while Go has sent `['Retry', 'Copy path']` since T125 — a double narrower than the backend cannot see a mapping that drops a member, so the fixture was corrected and the case renamed. The bridge mock needed no tightening: T123 already gave it a per-category permitted table, and its inline Reveal refusal already sends the full pair. Three `Notification` fixtures in `Banner.test.tsx` and `Toast.test.tsx` gained `remediations: []` because the field is required, not optional — an always-present list, empty for the message-only categories.
  - **The grace period is a decision, not a guess.** A browser sees only its own `focus` event, so a host that accepts a Reveal without raising a window would never fire one and focus would be stranded for the session. `whenApplicationRegainsForegroundFocus` (`ui/widgets/foregroundFocus.ts`) therefore checks `document.hasFocus()` once after one second: still holding the foreground means it was never given away, so there is nothing to wait for. The timer decides *when to check*, not how long the handover takes. Four tests cover the focus path, the never-left path, the still-away path, and cancellation detaching the listener so nothing accumulates.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — `Test Suites: 79 passed, 79 total`, `Tests: 575 passed, 575 total` (562 before this task, +13); lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, the same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notices in `DocumentIdentity.tsx:40` and `Launcher.tsx:18`, neither file touched. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1–M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` ok across all eleven packages. `just e2e-test`: `262 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 planned verifications attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted`.

- [X] T143 Derive the last two hardcoded accelerators from the registry, per FR-FT-047 (contradicts). Two platform-blind literals survive T112's sweep. `frontend/src/i18n/locales/en.json:155` holds `"editor.tab.context.closeAccelerator": "Ctrl W"`, rendered verbatim at `TabContextMenu.tsx:221-222`, while the registry declares `close-tab` as `'Mod+W'` (`actionRegistry.ts:226-231`), which `formatShortcut` renders `⌘W` on darwin. `EditorChrome.tsx:64-73` defines `parityOverflowShortcuts` (`'Ctrl ⇧ 8'`, `'Ctrl K'`, `'⌥⇧C'`, …) rendered at `:428-430`. Both render only on the `?parity-case=` route, so this is a narrower repeat of T112's `SettingsMenu` defect rather than a shipped user-facing one — but T112 closed that class and these are the remainder. Derive both from `formatShortcut(getAction(id).shortcut, currentPlatform())`.

  - **Line drift.** `TabContextMenu.tsx`'s accelerator span was at `:266-270`, not `:221-222`; `EditorChrome.tsx`'s `parityOverflowShortcuts` table was at `:64-73` and its render at `:426-433`. `en.json:155` and `actionRegistry.ts:226-231` were exact.
  - **Root cause, and it is one cause with two spellings.** Both surfaces treated an accelerator as display copy. `TabContextMenu` read it from the translation catalogue (`editor.tab.context.closeAccelerator`, `"Ctrl W"`), and `EditorChrome` read it from a module-local `Partial<Record<ActionEntry['id'], string>>` transcribed from the mockup. Neither read `getAction(id).shortcut`, so neither could be platform-correct: on macOS the tab menu advertised `Ctrl W` for a binding of `Mod+W` that dispatches on `⌘W`, and the overflow advertised `Ctrl ⇧ 8` for `Mod+Shift+8`. The literals were also internally inconsistent — `⌥⇧C` for `compact` is macOS glyphs while `Ctrl K` beside it is not — which is what a hand-transcribed table decays into.
  - **Red-first evidence.** Both tests were written and run before any production edit, and both control the platform read rather than trusting the host, because a literal that happens to agree on one platform is indistinguishable from a derivation otherwise. `TabContextMenu.test.tsx`: `expect(received).toBe(expected)` / `Expected: "⌘W"` / `Received: "Ctrl W"` on darwin, and `Expected: "Ctrl+W"` / `Received: "Ctrl W"` on win32 and linux — `Tests: 3 failed, 4 passed, 7 total`. `EditorChrome.test.tsx`, on win32, the whole table as a diff: `- "bullet-list": "Ctrl+Shift+8"` / `+ "bullet-list": "Ctrl ⇧ 8"`, `- "compact": "Alt+Shift+C"` / `+ "compact": "⌥⇧C"`, and six more.
  - **The catalogue key was deleted, not left orphaned.** `editor.tab.context.closeAccelerator` had exactly one reader and no other locale. Leaving a platform-blind accelerator string in the catalogue is an invitation to bind the next surface to it, which is how this defect reached two components from one.
  - **The parity movement the task warned about was real, and it is a reference defect, not production drift.** `just e2e-test` failed on exactly one case — `T060 state-pairs the 375px Settings overflow in Minimal Light`, `zero-tolerance pixel drift: 1794 unattributed pixels`, deterministic across all three repeats. Production now draws `⌘⇧8 ⌘⇧7 ⌘⇧9 ⌘⇧. ⌘K ⌘⇧I ⌘⇧T ⌥⇧C` while the reference still drew the mockup's `Ctrl ⇧ 8 …`. That is the difference FR-FT-056 exists for: the mockup's literals were correct for a platform-blind reference and are wrong on macOS, so the reference must express Feature 003's own accelerators and the two must be *compared*, not forgiven.
  - **Fixed on the reference side by the mechanism already in place for this exact class.** `adaptOverflowMenuAccelerators` in `e2e/parity/reference-adapter.ts` rewrites the eight `.ovf-menu` accelerator spans per platform, in the same shape as `adaptDeferredSettingsRows` (which already does this for `Ctrl ,` → `⌘,`) and `adaptViewMenu` (`Ctrl \\` → `⌘\\`). It uses only the mockup's own `.mi` row and inline span, edits no byte of `docs/delivery/spec/surface/mockup.html`, leaves the raw source hash unchanged, and throws by name if the source ever stops writing one of the eight rows. Both tables joined `REFERENCE_ADAPTER_HASH`. **No forgiveness rectangle was added and no attributed residual grew** — `targeted:settings-overflow:375:minimal-light` still carries only its 62-pixel `popupBoundary` term.
  - **Deliberately not done.** The four File-menu accelerators the T059 decision preserves as literal `Ctrl` reference text (New File, Open File, Save, Save As) were left alone: they are a reviewed, bounded, per-row-measured exception, and folding them into this sweep would have silently retired a decision this task does not own. The `parityLabel` switch beside the accelerator was also left as it is — it is a label-source question, not a shortcut one, and `T154` owns the parity route's string sourcing.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — `Test Suites: 79 passed, 79 total`, `Tests: 591 passed, 591 total` (586 before, +5); lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, the same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notices in `DocumentIdentity.tsx:40` and `Launcher.tsx:18`, neither file touched. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1-M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test`: `266 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T144 Reconcile the two tab-label truncation budgets, per FR-FT-035 (partial). FR-FT-035 requires that "visual ellipsis MUST retain part of the distinguishing suffix". Two truncations apply and only the first honours it: `DocumentTabs.tsx:504` calls `truncateTabLabel(label, 42)`, whose suffix-preserving branch (`tabLabel.ts:105-120`) fires only above 42 **characters**, after which the raw label meets `.tabLabel` (`DocumentTabs.module.css:127-133`), which applies the binding's 190 **px** `max-width` with `overflow: hidden; text-overflow: ellipsis` — a trailing-edge clip that removes exactly the ` — suffix` segment. A ~30-character disambiguated label is under the character threshold and over the pixel budget. The two budgets are expressed in different units and reconciled nowhere.
  - **Measure the crossover before fixing** — it was not resolvable from source alone — and assert the rendered result with `expectPainted`, not `toContainText`, since the accessible name will pass either way.

  - **Line drift.** `truncateTabLabel(label, 42)` was called at `DocumentTabs.tsx:653`, not `:504`; the suffix-preserving branch was `tabLabel.ts:132-146`, not `:105-120`. `DocumentTabs.module.css:127-133` and `tokens.css:115` were exact.
  - **The crossover, measured.** Chromium, the real application, the tab's own computed font — `12.5px Roboto, "Segoe UI", Inter, system-ui, sans-serif`. `.tabLabel` computes `max-width: 190px`, but that is not the constraint: the label sits inside `.tabItem`, which is also capped at 190px and spends **60.67px** of it on chrome (12px + 12px item padding, an 8px item gap, an 8px tab gap, the modified dot and the close control). **The label has 129.33px.** Growing `⁨release-notes.md⁩ — ⁨p…⁩` one character at a time: 20 characters measures 128.44px and fits; **21 characters measures 136.03px and is clipped.** The character budget is 42. The pixel clip therefore fires at *half* the character threshold, and the segment it removes is the trailing ` — suffix` — precisely what FR-FT-035 protects.
  - **Why no character count can fix it, which is the finding the measurement produced.** In 129.33px the same font fits **42 `i` glyphs, 19 `x`, and 11 `m`**. The existing 42 is exactly the narrowest-glyph fit, so it is not a wrong number so much as a number that can only ever be right for text made of the narrowest glyph in the face. Lowering it to 11 would mutilate every ordinary label to protect the pathological one. A character budget cannot express a pixel budget in a proportional font, so the two were reconciled in the layout instead — and `--tab-max-width` was **not** touched, so no parity-locked pixel moved.
  - **The fix.** `truncatedTabLabelParts` (`tabLabel.ts`) returns the visual label split at the separator; `DocumentTabs.tsx` renders the two parts as `[data-tab-label-basename]` and `[data-tab-label-suffix]`; `.tabLabel:has(> [data-tab-label-suffix])` becomes an `inline-flex` box with `min-width: 0`, the basename is `flex: 0 1 auto` with the `text-overflow: ellipsis`, and the suffix is `flex: 0 0 auto`. The pixel ellipsis now lands on the non-distinguishing half. Measured after the change: basename `scrollWidth` 103 against `clientWidth` 73 — elided — with the full ` — ⁨archive⁩` drawn at 56.7px beside it.
  - **`:has()` scoping is deliberate and load-bearing for parity.** A label with no suffix — every tab in every parity fixture, and the ordinary case in use — keeps the single-text-node `inline-block` box it always had, including `min-width: auto`, which is one of the properties `METRIC_PROPERTIES` compares. `just e2e-test` moved nothing: `[parity accounting] 150/150`.
  - **Red first, in two stages, because one stage would have proved almost nothing.** Stage one, against unmodified production: `expect(locator).toHaveCount(expected) failed / Locator: locator('[role="tab"] [data-tab-label-suffix]') / Expected: 2 / Received: 0` — the suffix had no element of its own. That alone only proves an element is absent, so the plausible **wrong** version was built next: both spans rendered, the pixel clip left exactly as it was. Against that, `expectPainted` produced the defect verbatim — `Error: the first tab's distinguishing suffix is laid out at (501.922, 68.5) but the topmost paint there is div[role="presentation"]. It is clipped away or covered, and neither toBeVisible() nor toContainText() can see that (T113).` **The two accessible-name assertions in the same test passed in both red states**, which is the whole reason the task forbade `toContainText`: FR-FT-035's accessible-name clause was never broken, so a text assertion is green while the user sees `release-notes.md` twice with nothing to tell them apart.
  - **A test-double defect had to be fixed before the requirement could be reached at all, and it is the more alarming finding.** The dev bridge mock keyed a document on its **basename** (`selectedDocumentId`, `AppModelHandler.ts`), while Go keys on the canonical path (FR-FT-004). Opening `/Users/parity/Notes/archive/release-notes.md` and `/Users/parity/Notes/projects/release-notes.md` therefore returned `focused` on the second and produced **one** tab. FR-FT-035 exists entirely for the case of two tabs sharing a basename, and in the mock that case could not occur — so every defect behind it was unreachable from a browser run, and the suite stayed green. The mock now falls back to the canonical path when a basename is already claimed by a different file, keeping the basename id while it is free so no existing fixture moves; a `?duplicate-basenames=1` recents fixture supplies the pair, following the `?refuseReveal` / `?refuseSave=N` precedent.
  - **Deliberately not done.** `--tab-max-width` was left at 190px and `tokens.test.ts:171` untouched — the task named it as parity-locked and the layout route makes it unnecessary. `truncateTabLabel` was kept as the exported string form rather than replaced: it still bounds the joined label at 42 characters and `truncatedTabLabelParts` is built on it, so the two cannot disagree. And one residual is recorded rather than hidden: if a *suffix alone* is wider than 129.33px, the basename shrinks to nothing and the suffix's own trailing edge is clipped — the user still sees the leading part of the distinguishing suffix, which is what FR-FT-035 requires ("part of"), so this is within the requirement rather than a remaining gap.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — `Test Suites: 79 passed, 79 total`, `Tests: 593 passed, 593 total` (591 after T143, +2); lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, the same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notices in `DocumentIdentity.tsx:40` and `Launcher.tsx:18`, neither file touched. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1-M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test`: `267 passed (5.2m)` (266 before, +1), `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T145 Repair the tab strip's ARIA ownership, per FR-FT-047 (contradicts). The structure is invalid, so the roles it exposes are not the roles assistive technology sees. `DocumentTabs.tsx:416-421` sets `role="tablist"`, but each `role="tab"` button (`:464-506`) is wrapped in a plain `<div className={styles.tabItem}>` (`:463`), so **no `role="tab"` is an owned child of the tablist**; the per-tab close button (`:507-532`) and the `+` control (`:536-565`) are additional non-`tab` children. There is no `aria-controls`, no `role="tabpanel"`, and no `aria-owns` compensating. Give the wrapper `role="presentation"` (or add `aria-owns`), and associate each tab with the editor region.

  - **Line drift.** `role="tablist"` was at `DocumentTabs.tsx:452-457`, the tab button at `500-542`, the wrapper at `499`, the close control at `543-568` and the `+` control at `572-601`. The description of each was exact.
  - **Root cause.** Nothing was mis-declared; the declarations were unreachable. `role="tab"` requires an owning `tablist`, and `role="tablist"` requires owned `tab` children, and a plain `<div className={styles.tabItem}>` sat between them. Both halves of the pair therefore failed at once. Chromium's accessibility tree confirmed it before the change: the tablist node's children were `generic, generic` and no `tab` node was its child.
  - **Why the markup could not prove it.** The DOM shape is identical before and after — the wrapper stays, because it is the flex item that pairs a tab with its close control and removing it moves the strip's pixels. Only the accessibility tree changes. A markup assertion would have passed in both states, so the e2e case reads Chromium's real tree over a CDP `Accessibility.getFullAXTree` session and counts the tablist's `tab` children. That is the tree assistive technology consumes, and it is the only artefact where this defect was ever visible.
  - **Red-first evidence, e2e.** With `role="presentation"` removed and everything else in place: `expect(received).toHaveLength(expected)` / `Expected length: 2` / `Received length: 0` / `Received array:  []` on `expect(childRoles.filter((role) => role === 'tab')).toHaveLength(2)`. With the whole change reverted it failed one assertion earlier, on `expect(locator).toHaveCount(expected) failed` / `Locator:  getByRole('tabpanel')` / `Expected: 1` / `Received: 0`.
  - **Red-first evidence, unit.** `T145 owns every tab from the tablist and points it at the editor panel` failed verbatim with `- "controls": "editor-tabpanel", - "id": "tab-one", - "owned": true` against `+ "controls": null, + "id": null, + "owned": false`. jsdom does not compute an accessibility tree, so ownership is asserted structurally there: every element between a tab and its tablist must carry `presentation` or `none`.
  - **The association.** Each tab now carries `id={tabElementId(documentId)}` and `aria-controls`, and the editor panes container carries the matching `id`, `role="tabpanel"` and an `aria-labelledby` naming the active tab. The identity is shared through a new `frontend/src/ui/widgets/editorTabPanel.ts` rather than exported from either component — a non-component export from a component module costs a third `react-refresh/only-export-components` warning, and the warning count is part of the baseline. One panel serves every tab because the strip switches documents inside a single stage rather than mounting a pane per tab; the panel therefore takes its name from whichever tab is active.
  - **Roving focus, covered as asked.** `T145 keeps the roving tabIndex and Home/End/Arrow model after the ownership repair` re-asserts one-tab-at-`tabindex=0`, Home, End, and the Arrow clamp at both edges. It passed before the change and passes after: it is a regression guard on the wrapper that gained the role, not a proof of new behaviour, and it is recorded that way rather than counted as coverage. The `T084` cases that own this behaviour remain untouched.
  - **What was deliberately not done.** The per-tab close control and the `+` control remain siblings of the tabs inside the tablist — the AX tree now reports `tab, button, tab, button, button`. Every alternative costs more than it buys: `aria-owns` over elements that are already DOM descendants is explicitly discouraged, `role="presentation"` on a focusable control is ignored by conflict resolution, and relocating either control out of the strip moves parity-locked pixels. ARIA does not forbid additional children of a `tablist`; what it requires is that the tablist own its tabs, and it now does. This is a judgement recorded rather than an omission hidden.
  - **Gates.** `just check` 79 suites / 581 tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1-M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test` 264 passed, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T146 Stop rendering a full canonical path in the external-change prompt, per FR-FT-048 and the classified error contract (partial). FR-FT-048 forbids exposing private full paths, and the error contract restricts user-facing copy to the safe basename or disambiguated tab label. `ExternalChangePrompt.tsx:64` interpolates `preview.displayName ?? preview.path ?? t('editor.untitled')`, so whenever the backend omits `displayName` the prompt renders the **full canonical absolute path**. Everything else about this surface is correct and should not be disturbed: title, button labels, order and initial focus all match FR-FT-021 and FR-FT-022 (`ExternalChangePrompt.tsx:56-58,111-148`), as does `NormalizationPrompt.tsx:63-83` for FR-FT-011. Reduce the fallback to a basename.

  - **Root cause.** The prompt's subject expression treated `preview.path` as a display value. It is not one: `ConflictPreview.path` is the canonical absolute path, and `displayName` is optional on both the Go and TypeScript sides, so every conflict the backend reported without a `displayName` rendered the user's full directory chain into the dialog body. The safe-basename logic FR-FT-035 requires already existed in `ui/widgets/tabLabel.ts`, but only as a module-local step inside `tabLabelFor`, reachable solely by callers that can supply a `DocumentMetadata` and the whole document set — which the conflict prompt cannot. There was no seam for "make an arbitrary path safe to show", so the prompt had nothing to call and fell back to the raw string.

  - **Red-first evidence.** Two tests were added to `frontend/src/ui/widgets/ExternalChangePrompt.test.tsx` and run before any production edit. Both failed, verbatim: `expect(received).not.toContain(expected) // indexOf / Expected substring: not "/Users/someone/Private/Journal/notes.md" / Received string: "/Users/someone/Private/Journal/notes.md changed outside GoMarkEdit."`, and `expect(received).not.toContain(expected) // indexOf / Expected substring: not "‮" / Received string: "/tmp/reports/re‮gnp.md changed outside GoMarkEdit."` — `Tests: 2 failed, 5 passed, 7 total`. The second case matters independently of path exposure: a right-to-left override embedded in a filename reached the dialog unescaped and unisolated, so a hostile name could reorder the sentence around it.

  - **The fix is the seam, not the call site.** `tabLabel.ts` gained an exported `safeBasenameOf(source: string | undefined): string | undefined` built on the same `isolateUserText` the tab strip uses, with the path splitting factored out of `pathParts` into a shared `splitPath` so the two cannot drift. It returns `undefined` for an absent or separator-only source, which is what lets the prompt keep its own `editor.untitled` fallback rather than render an empty subject. `ExternalChangePrompt.tsx` now reads `preview.displayName ?? safeBasenameOf(preview.path) ?? t('editor.untitled')`.

  - **Deliberately not done.** `displayName` is passed through unchanged: it is the backend's already-safe display name, and reducing it too would have added directional isolation marks to a value that never carried a path, for no requirement. The module-local `safeFilename` in `App.tsx:112-124` was left in place rather than collapsed into the new helper — the two differ in a way that is not incidental (`safeFilename` accepts a `targetPath` override, strips control characters without isolating, and substitutes the literal `Untitled.md`), so unifying them is a behaviour change to the save-status copy and belongs to its own task, not to a path-disclosure fix. Nothing at `ExternalChangePrompt.tsx:56-58` or `:111-148` was touched, so the FR-FT-021/FR-FT-022 title, button set, order and initial focus are byte-identical.

  - **Gates.** `just check` green — frontend `Test Suites: 77 passed, 77 total`, `Tests: 562 passed, 562 total` (560 before, +2 from this task); lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, both the pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notices in `DocumentIdentity.tsx:40` and `Launcher.tsx:18`, neither file touched here. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1–M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` ok across all eleven packages. `just e2e-test`: `262 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 planned verifications attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted`.

- [X] T147 Bring the branch topology back to the AGENTS.md git protocol (contradicts). The protocol is one task = one branch = one commit, squash-merged into the parent and the branch deleted. **20 commits sit on the current task branch** (`feature/v1-implementation--003-t113-details-visibility`), and 31+ task branches survive unmerged. This is the mechanism that produced T114's divergence and will reproduce it; fixing T114 without this leaves the cause in place. Sequence it after T114, since the resolution there determines what is merged where.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16, and the task's own figures were wrong — which is the finding.** It records "**20 commits sit on the current task branch**" and "31+ task branches survive unmerged". Measured at `621fc3c4`: the chain tip carried **158** commits, not 20, and **0** merge commits; of **44** task branches, **42 were already merged** and only **2** were not. The protocol had in fact been followed — 158 linear commits *is* 158 squash-merges, one per task. What had drifted was not the merging but the **deleting**, plus the parent branch the merges never reached (T114).
  - **Recording the corrected measurement is the point.** Closing this against "20 commits / 31+ unmerged" would have left a false record of what the repository looked like, which is the evidence dishonesty this phase exists to remove.
  - **The two genuinely unmerged branches were superseded, and that was checked rather than assumed.** `003-t009-preview-slice` adds four `preview.*` catalogue keys and a paused-refresh bar — all four keys are present on the chain tip and `PreviewPane.tsx:115-139` renders the bar. `003-t009-mock-bridge` adds New/Open coverage to a 481-line `AppModelHandler.ts`; the chain tip's is **1859** lines and covers both. Each was tagged (`archive/t009-mock-bridge`, `archive/t009-preview-slice`) before deletion, so the claim stays reversible.
  - **Two stale worktree registrations blocked the cleanup and are the mechanism worth naming.** Both t009 branches were checked out in linked worktrees under `/private/tmp/` whose directories the OS had already removed. `git branch -D` refuses a branch held by a worktree, so the branches were undeletable and invisible as such — `git branch --list` marks them only with a `+`. `git worktree prune` cleared both registrations. **A task branch created in a `/tmp` worktree outlives its directory and cannot be deleted afterwards**, which is one way 44 branches accumulated.
  - **Final topology:** three branches — `master` (untouched at `85205ba7`), `feature/v1-implementation`, and the chain tip, with the parent now equal to the tip — and three `archive/*` tags holding everything discarded.
  - **Gates:** none run — no file in the build changed.

- [X] T148 Make the Open picker filter case-insensitively, per FR-FT-002 (partial). `main.go:58-61` passes a single lowercase glob `"*.md;*.markdown;*.mdown;*.txt"` to `runtime.OpenFileDialog`, while FR-FT-002 requires the picker to be "filtered case-insensitively". The backend suffix check is already correct (`file.IsSupportedDocumentSuffix`, `internal/file/paths.go:95`, lowercases first), so this is the dialog layer only. **Host-dependent and not resolvable from source**: GTK's pattern filter is case-sensitive, macOS `NSOpenPanel` is not. Either add the case variants to the pattern or verify per platform and record the evidence — do not assume from the macOS result.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16**, taking the direction `decisions-phase-21.md` records: add the case variants to the pattern rather than block on a Linux host.
  - **Root cause.** `documentFileFilters()` returned one hardcoded lowercase glob string for all three hosts. The wails sources make the consequence exact: `internal/frontend/desktop/linux/window.go:402-404` splits the pattern on `;` and hands each token to `gtk_file_filter_add_pattern`, which compiles a **GPatternSpec** — case-sensitive, and understanding only `*` and `?`, so there is no `*.[mM][dD]` character class available. On GTK, `NOTES.MD` was therefore hidden by the filter even though `file.IsSupportedDocumentSuffix` would have accepted it after selection. `darwin/dialog.go:67` and the Windows common item dialog both match case-insensitively already.
  - **Where the fix went.** `main.go` now holds one `supportedDocumentSuffixes` list; `documentFileFiltersFor(goos)` emits the four lowercase globs on macOS and Windows and, on Linux, `suffixCaseGlobs` enumerates **all 300** case forms (4 + 256 + 32 + 8). Enumeration is scoped to Linux deliberately: it is the only host that needs it, and the two that do not are the two this repository cannot exercise — a ~3 KB, 300-entry filter spec is not worth pushing through an untested Windows `IFileDialog::SetFileTypes` path for zero behavioural gain. `*.md` stays the first glob on every host because `windows/dialog.go:125` derives `DefaultExtension` from it.
  - **Red-first**, after the `goos` parameter existed but before the enumeration:

    ```
    --- FAIL: TestNativePickersFilterExactlyTheSupportedSuffixes (0.00s)
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".MD", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".Md", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".MARKDOWN", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".Markdown", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".MDOWN", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".Mdown", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".TXT", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:770: linux: the picker does not offer ".Txt", so GTK hides files named that way
        main_test.go:775: linux: picker offers 4 globs, want every case form of the four suffixes (300)
        main_test.go:785: the suffix list appears 0 times in main.go, want one shared definition
    ```

  - **The test drives the host branch explicitly** rather than reading `goruntime.GOOS`, so the Linux enumeration is proved on this macOS host. `TestNativePickersFilterExactlyTheSupportedSuffixes` loses its `(partial)` qualifier, and the matching caveat in `internal/appmodel/handler_test.go:230` goes with it.
  - **Deliberately not done.** No per-platform manual verification and no Linux VM — the owner decision rules that out. No change to `file.IsSupportedDocumentSuffix`, which was already correct. The macOS and Windows case-insensitivity claims rest on those platforms' documented behaviour and on the task text, not on a measurement taken here; they are stated as such in `main.go`'s comment.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — 80 suites / 633 tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS. `go test -race ./internal/... .` green. No `frontend/` or `e2e/` file touched, so `just e2e-test` was not re-run.

- [X] T149 Count only lines that actually rendered in the conflict hunk, per FR-FT-021 (partial). `internal/appmodel/conflict.go:515-531` returns `LineCount: lineCount + 1` when the 4,096-byte budget is exhausted mid-line, even where the inner rune loop wrote zero bytes of that line — `remaining` can be 0, in which case `runeBytes > remaining` breaks immediately. The reported displayed-line count can therefore exceed the lines present in `Text`, and FR-FT-021 requires the prompt to "visibly identify which side is truncated" against an accurate count. The 12-line and 4,096-byte caps and the no-split-code-point rule are correctly enforced and should not change.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16.** Root cause exactly as described: the budget-exhaustion branch returned `lineCount + 1` unconditionally. The branch now counts the line only when the inner rune loop actually wrote bytes of it.
  - **Reaching it needs the byte budget to land on a line boundary *inside* the 12-line cap**, which is why it had gone unseen — the obvious oversized fixture trips the line cap first and returns through the early return that was already correct. Four 1,024-byte lines exhaust the 4,096-byte budget exactly at line five, with `lineCount` at 4, so `remaining` is 0 and the rune guard breaks before the first byte.
  - **Red-first**, from `internal/appmodel/conflict_test.go`:

    ```
    --- FAIL: TestConflictPreviewCountsOnlyTheLinesItActuallyRendered (0.00s)
        conflict_test.go:389: side.LineCount = 5 but Text contains 4 lines: the prompt would name a line the reader cannot see
    ```

  - **A second test pins the fix against over-correcting.** `TestConflictPreviewStillCountsAPartiallyRenderedLine` asserts that a line the budget cuts *through* is still counted, because it is visible. It passed before the change and after it — recorded here as a regression guard, not as a red-first proof, so the pass is not mistaken for one.
  - **Deliberately not done:** the 12-line cap, the 4,096-byte cap and the no-split-code-point rule are untouched, and the line-cap early return already reported `LineCount: lineCount` correctly.
  - **Gates:** `just check` exit 0 — 79 suites / 601 frontend tests, lint 0 errors and the 2 pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS; `go test -race ./internal/... .` clean. No `frontend/` or `e2e/` change, so `just e2e-test` was not required.

- [X] T150 Remove `comparisonAttempted` outright, per FR-FT-056 (partial). The 2026-08-14 clarification (`spec.md:333`) strengthened the rule: a production-only `comparisonAttempted: false` artifact "is now forbidden outright rather than merely uncountable". One writer survives at `targeted-parity.test.ts:1332`, in the semantic-pairing-mismatch branch. It rethrows immediately (`:1336-1337`), so the case is red and the artifact can never stand in for a pass — which is why this is LOW rather than a live defect — but the field persists in the writer's type (`:1069`) and its serialisers (`:1089,1175,1189`). Replace it with an explicit `status: 'pairing-mismatch'` discriminant.

  - **Line drift.** The writer's input type had moved to `targeted-parity.test.ts:1069`, its serialisers to `:1089` (`semantic.json`), `:1175` (`status.json`) and `:1189` (`raw-status.log`), the pairing-mismatch writer to `:1338` and the normal compared path to `:1489`. Six occurrences, not the four the task text lists — the field appeared on **both** call sites, not only the mismatch one. Located by content.
  - **Root cause.** The field was a second, redundant encoding of something `status: TargetedStatus` already carried exactly. `'pairing-mismatch'` is the one of that union's four values which means the comparison did not run, so `comparisonAttempted` could only ever restate it — and a restatement is precisely what the spec forbids, because a boolean named "was a comparison attempted" can sit alone in an artifact and be read as a verification outcome, while a `status` discriminant cannot. Confirmed **zero production occurrences**: every one of the six was in `frontend/e2e/`.
  - **Red-first evidence.** The proving test reads the harness source, exactly as `accounting.test.ts:24-31` reads `playwright.config.ts` for the repetition count — Jest cannot import a Playwright file that calls `test()` at module scope. Verbatim: `● T150 leaves no comparisonAttempted field for any parity artifact to carry` / `expect(received).not.toContain(expected) // indexOf` / `Expected substring: not "comparisonAttempted"`, `Tests: 1 failed, 1 passed, 2 total`. Green after the change.
  - **What the fix did.** Deleted the field from the input type and from all three serialisers, and added `status: input.status` to `semantic.json` so the artifact that lost the boolean gains the discriminant rather than losing the information. The `default-open-mode` and `markdown-standard` lists carried the opposite failure: a literal `aria-disabled="true"` that agrees with the registry today by coincidence and would keep disabling the rows after the registry stopped deferring them.

It is not. `getAction('default-open-mode').availability.kind` is `deferred` (`laterDeferred`), and **nothing in the frontend can write the setting**: `AppearanceControls.persist` accepts only `mode` and `theme` patches and forwards `next.defaultOpenMode` unchanged, and `SettingsMenu` is handed `defaultOpenMode` with no `onDefaultOpenModeChange` beside it. FR-FT-003 requires the mode to be *applied*, which T119 delivered; it does not require this popup to change it. The registry and the backend therefore agree, and the row stays a state report. `markdown-standard` is the same shape with no writer anywhere.
  - **Both terms are now stated at every row, and that is deliberate.** `rowUnavailable(id, writer?)` requires the registry to allow the action **and** a writer to exist. The registry alone is not sufficient: if `default-open-mode` were flipped to available tomorrow, deriving from the registry alone would draw an operable row calling nothing — the defect T116 exists to remove — so the omitted `writer` argument at those two call sites is the second reason, written down rather than implied. Each row also carries `data-availability={getAction(id).availability.kind}`, the idiom `ViewMenu.tsx:193` and `ShortcutsDialog.tsx:106` already use, which is what makes the registry the *observable* authority instead of an invisible one.

Autosave, with the handler wired and the registry deferring: `expect(element).toBeDisabled()` / `Received element is not disabled: <input aria-label="Autosave" checked="" class="toggleInput" type="checkbox" />`. The two state-row cases: `no settings row for Reading (Viewer); found Autosave | Format on save | Lint on save` — the five state rows carried neither `data-settings-row` nor `data-availability`, so nothing about them was addressable at all. The third case makes the registry call both settings *available* and asserts the rows report `available` while staying `aria-disabled="true"`, which is what pins the second term.
  - **Deliberately not done.** No writer was added for either setting. Making `default-open-mode` or `markdown-standard` operable is a behaviour change against a registry that defers both and a backend that exposes no frontend setter for the standard at all; it is not what "derive availability from the registry" asks for, and adding a control ahead of its command is the defect this task's own precedent removed. `.row.stateRow[aria-disabled='true']`'s full-strength treatment in `MenuSurface.module.css` was left untouched — those rows are still state reports and the binding draws them undimmed.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — `Test Suites: 79 passed, 79 total`, `Tests: 596 passed, 596 total` (593 after T144, +3); lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, the same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notices in `DocumentIdentity.tsx:40` and `Launcher.tsx:18`, neither file touched. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1-M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test`: `267 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150` — the Settings popup is a compared region and `data-availability` paints nothing, so no parity key moved.

## Phase 22: Coverage gaps opened by the Phase 21 CRITICAL block

Filed rather than papered over, per T115's rule: where a fix reaches part of a contract and leaves the
rest genuinely unbuilt, the remainder is its own task with its own evidence.

- [X] T156 Honour `Retry` on the entry and close-plan paths, per the classified error and remediation contract and FR-FT-015 (partial). T116 made a classified remediation reachable, but only where a caller could name the command behind it. Three `reportClassifiedError` callers still declare no intent and therefore render no control, even though Go sends `RemediationRetry`: `reportEntryError` (`frontend/src/App.tsx:441-446`, backing New/Open/Open Recent/Reopen), `reportClosePlanError` (`:568-573`), and the tab activate/close/reorder arms in `frontend/src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.tsx:184-188,302-311,328-336`. Every one of those refusals is `conflict` with the message "The tab set changed; … must be retried." (`internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle.go:136,167`, `close_plan.go:44,88`, `tab_session.go`), so a retry is well defined — re-read `snapshot.tabSetRevision` and re-issue the same command — but it is not built, and rendering a control that does nothing would be the defect T116 exists to remove. **Do not add an intent without the command behind it.**
  - **Tests/evidence**: per path, a test that refuses once on a stale revision, clicks Retry, and asserts the command is re-issued against the fresh revision and succeeds — failing first.

  - **Closed 2026-08-16 for two of the three paths named, with the third filed rather than faked.** Wired: the four entry commands (`reportEntryError`, backing New / Open / Open Recent / Reopen) and the tab **activation** arm. Filed: **T164** (the close-plan path and the tab *close* arm) and **T165** (the tab *reorder* arm). The rule the task set — never add an intent without the command behind it — is what decided the split, and each refusal is reasoned below rather than asserted.
  - **Line drift.** `reportEntryError` was at `App.tsx:477-482`, `reportClosePlanError` at `:615-620`, and the `DocumentTabs.tsx` arms at `:236-240`, `:289-293` and `:320-324`. The described content was exact.
  - **Root cause.** `reportClassifiedError`'s `intent` is how a caller earns a control, and these callers passed none, so `remediationsFor` dropped the `Retry` Go had sent. The refusal still reached the user with the backend's own message — "The tab set changed; Open must be retried." — instructing them to do a thing the interface offered no way to do.
  - **What the retry actually is, and why re-sending is not it.** Every one of these refusals is a *stale revision*, so the revision that failed is by definition the wrong one and re-sending it would refuse identically — the same reasoning that already makes the write arm use `beginWrite` rather than `finishWrite`. `retryEntryCommand` therefore reads `snapshot.tabSetRevision` from the backend, **not** from the store: the store is a projection and the patch carrying the change may not have arrived. Each arm then calls the *same handler the original invocation used*, so the flush, the active-buffer update and the re-report of a second refusal stay in one place.
  - **The construction-side guarantee, so "no intent without a command" cannot rot.** `retryIsExecutable` (`classifiedNotification.ts`) names each intent's required arguments once and is exhaustive over the union, so a new intent that names none fails the build. `open-recent` without a path and `activate-document` without a document produce **no control at all** rather than an inert one, and that is asserted directly. `activate-document` takes its document from the caller (`retry.documentId`) rather than from `error.documentId`, because a stale-tab-set refusal is about the *set* and carries no document — reading the error would have silently dropped the control for want of a target the strip has.
  - **Red first, four ways, all captured before any production edit was kept.** Entry path, driving the real File menu: `Unable to find role="button" and name "Retry"` — Go sent `Retry`, the toast rendered nothing. Tab activation: `expect(received).toEqual(expected)` / `- Array [ - Object { - "action": "retry", - "documentId": "two", - "intent": "activate-document", - "labelKey": "action.retry.label", - }, - ]` against `+ Array []`. The freshness half needed its own red: with the first assertion written as "re-issued with the revision it already had", `expect(received).not.toBe(expected) / Expected: not 41` failed — which is why the tests now move the backend's revision *while the toast is up* (41 → 99, 77 → 78) and assert the second call carries the new one.
  - **T164, filed — the close-plan path cannot be re-issued from where it reports.** `reportClosePlanError` fires inside `processClosePlanResult` and `completeClosePlan`, and the retry is not "run this again": a stale-tab-set close refusal (`close_plan.go:44,88`) must re-run **`prepareClose(kind, targets, freshRevision)`**, and neither site holds `kind` or `targets` — `App.tsx`'s `onCloseDocument` does, three frames up. Worse, the three arms have three different original requests: a tab close, a native quit (where `cancelNativeClose()` has already run and re-issuing would re-trigger a quit the OS already asked about), and a recovery `discard-all`. The plan id the site *does* hold is precisely the one the backend refused as stale. Threading a retry descriptor through `resolvePreparedClosePlan` → `processClosePlanResult` → `completeClosePlan` is a real change to three signatures with a per-arm decision at each, and doing it badly would produce exactly the control-that-calls-nothing this task forbids. **The `DocumentTabs.tsx` close arm goes with it**, and its own finding is recorded there: in the shipped application that arm is unreachable, because `onCloseDocument` swallows the refusal into `{status: 'noop'}` and the error is reported by `reportClosePlanError` instead.
  - **T165, filed — the reorder command is re-issuable but its announcement is not.** Re-running `reorderDocument(documentId, targetIndex, freshRevision)` is well defined, but FR-FT-034 requires a successful move to be announced, and that announcement is built in `DocumentTabs` from the tab label and the strip length. `onRemediate` lives in `App.tsx` and has neither. A Retry that moved the tab silently would satisfy the pixels and break the requirement, so the intent was not added.
  - **Deliberately not done.** No `close-document`, `close-plan` or `reorder-document` intent exists — the union carries only commands that run. `NotificationRemediation.path` was added rather than a general payload bag, because exactly one intent needs an argument the error does not already carry, and a bag invites the next caller to smuggle in something unchecked.
  - **Gates.** `just check` green — `Test Suites: 79 passed, 79 total`, `Tests: 601 passed, 601 total` (596 after T155, +5); lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, the same pre-existing `react-refresh/only-export-components` notices in `DocumentIdentity.tsx:40` and `Launcher.tsx:18`, neither file touched. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1-M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test`: `267 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`.

- [X] T159 Resolve the `conflict` remediation-row disagreement, per Constitution I (contradicts). T123 made the classified error and remediation contract enforceable and found one pairing it could not honour without a decision. The contract's `conflict` row (`spec.md:892`) lists `Reload from disk`, `Keep mine` and `Skip` for an editable external change, `Reload from disk` and `Cancel` for a read-only one, and message-only for a Save As collision — it does **not** list `Retry`. But this codebase also classifies **stale-tab-set and stale-revision refusals** as `conflict`: "The tab set changed; Open must be retried." (`internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle.go:136`), the close-plan equivalents (`close_plan.go:44,88`) and the reorder/close arms. For those, re-issuing the command against the fresh revision is the only sensible action, and T156 is filed to honour exactly that.
  - `remediationsByCategory` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go`) therefore allows `Retry` for `conflict` with the disagreement stated at the definition. **That comment is a record, not a resolution.**
  - **Two directions, and the owner picks:** amend the contract's `conflict` row to cover revision conflicts and their `Retry`, **or** introduce a distinct category (e.g. `stale-revision`) and reclassify those refusals, leaving `conflict` to mean an external-change collision only. The second is cleaner but touches ~45 call sites and the eight-category vocabulary the whole contract is built on.
  - Sequence before **T156**, which will otherwise honour `Retry` on a category the specification does not permit it for.
  - **Closed 2026-08-16. The owner picked the first direction: amend the row.** `spec.md:892`'s `conflict` row now covers both collisions it was always being used for — an external change (`Reload from disk`, `Keep mine`, `Skip`; `Reload from disk` and `Cancel` when read-only) **and** a stale tab-set or stale revision, where `Retry` is "the only action that can succeed". The Meaning column gained the second clause too, since a row that describes only external changes cannot justify a revision remediation. The ninth-category direction was rejected on cost: it would have touched ~45 call sites and the eight-category vocabulary the whole contract is built on, to express a distinction the row can carry.
  - **Root cause was that nothing could compare the two tables.** The contract exists twice — as prose in `spec.md` and as `remediationsByCategory` in `internal/apperr/classified_error.go` — and no gate read both. The divergence survived a whole phase as a comment because a comment was the only place it *could* be recorded.
  - **The seam fix, not just the row.** `TestRemediationsByCategoryMatchesTheContractTable` (`internal/apperr/contract_table_test.go`) parses the eight-row table out of `spec.md` and compares each row's actionable remediations against what this package enforces. Editing either side alone now fails, naming the category and the difference. Without it, T159 would have resolved one instance of a class that recurs by construction.
  - **Red first, verified:** against the unamended row the test failed with `category "conflict": enforced [Cancel Keep mine Reload from disk Retry Skip], contract names [Cancel Keep mine Reload from disk Skip]` — the divergence stated as a diff. It passes against the amended row, and `conflict` was the only category that differed.
  - **The anchor is partial and says which clause it drops.** `RemediationNone` is excluded from the comparison: the contract's preamble puts message-only in every category's vocabulary and this package allows it everywhere, so a row that does not spell out "message-only" is not forbidding it. The test therefore does not prove which rows *require* message-only, and the comment says so rather than letting the anchor overstate.
  - **No behaviour changed.** The table already allowed the pairing and 47 call sites already emitted it; what changed is that the specification now says so. That is why there is no behavioural red-first here — the honest red was the two tables disagreeing.

        host_ports_wiring_test.go:42: host port reveal (file.RevealPort) is nil: the composition root never injected it, so the command that uses it cannot work in a shipped binary
    FAIL

It fails rather than passes when the scan matches fewer than the two known ports, so a rename cannot silently empty it. `reflect.Value.IsNil` is legal on an unexported field; only `Interface()` is not.

Windows goes through `Set-Clipboard` with an explicit UTF-8 stdin encoding rather than `clip.exe`, which decodes stdin with the console code page and would corrupt every non-ASCII character in a path — and a path is the only thing `CopyPath` ever writes.

That is precisely the optional injection that caused this defect: it would have made the port droppable again, by the one caller the test cannot force. Keeping the implementation in `internal/file` lets the composition root wire it unconditionally, and keeps `internal/appmodel` free of Wails as Constitution III requires. The cost is a runtime dependency on a host clipboard tool on linux — the posture `platformRevealPort` already takes with `xdg-open`.

It is a latency-evidence harness that never invokes either command, and changing it would have widened this task past the shipped binary. Filed as **T167**.
