- [x] T160 Make `Save to recreate` a real command so the detached `not-found` remediation pair can be offered, per FR-FT-023 and FR-FT-037 (partial, opened by T142). The contract's `not-found` row remediates a detached document with "`Save to recreate` plus `Copy path`" (`spec.md:889`), FR-FT-037 requires the disappearance race to "report one deduplicated `not-found` error offering `Save to recreate` and `Copy path`", and Go has sent exactly that pair since T125 (`internal/appmodel/copy_path.go`, `revealDetachedFailure`). T142 widened the toast to carry and render a set, and deliberately did **not** map `Save to recreate`, because nothing in the frontend can run it — a control that renders and calls nothing is the defect T116 exists to remove. Two blockers, both in `App.tsx`:
  - **`beginWrite` refuses a detached document outright** (`App.tsx:1125`), reporting a `permission-denied` "read only" error. That directly contradicts **FR-FT-023**: "If the backing file is missing, the application MUST keep the buffer, mark the document detached and modified, and **allow explicit Save to recreate the same path**", and acceptance scenario 6 at `spec.md:546`. The Go side already supports it — `internal/appmodel/save.go:340` clears `detached` on a successful write — so this is a frontend guard blocking a backend capability. **This is a live FR-FT-023 defect independent of the remediation**, and it means explicit Save is refused for every detached document today, not only via a toast.
  - **`beginWrite` writes only the *active* document**, resolving its target from `activeDocument`/`activeBuffer` and ignoring any document id it is handed. A `Save to recreate` control belongs to the document the toast names, which need not be the active one; wiring the intent without addressing this would save a different file than the message identifies.
  - Where the code goes: `frontend/src/App.tsx` (`beginWrite`, `onRemediate`), `frontend/src/logic/store/classifiedNotification.ts` (`remediationsFor` — add the member and its intent), `frontend/src/logic/store/notificationsSlice.ts` (`NotificationRemediationAction`/`Intent`), plus the `action.save-to-recreate.label` catalogue entry. Red-first must include a test that an explicit Save on a detached document recreates the file rather than being refused (FR-FT-023), separately from the remediation rendering.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17. There were three blockers, not two** — the task named the two in `beginWrite` and missed the one that actually fired first.
  - **The third blocker, found by the red test, is an *availability* gate.** `applicationMenuState.writable` (`App.tsx`) ended `&& activeDocument.detached !== true`, and `writable` reaches `ShellMenuRow.tsx:403`, which draws Save and Save As unavailable. So a detached document could not even *dispatch* the write that `beginWrite` would then have refused: the first red run showed `save` never called **and an empty notification list**, because the click landed on a disabled menu item and `beginWrite` was never entered. Removing only the documented `beginWrite` clause left the test just as red. Both layers encode the same wrong idea — that detachment is a write incapacity — and both are gone. Recorded because the two gates hide each other: a test that called `onSave` directly rather than driving the File menu would have gone green with the menu item still dead for every real user.
  - **Red first, and isolated.** The failure was `Unable to find … save called with 'document-1'`-shaped rather than obviously about `detached`, so the cause was pinned by flipping the single variable: the identical test with `detached: false` passes. For the remediation half the red was exact — `Expected: "document-2", 0, ""` / `Received: "document-1", 0, ""`, which is precisely the "saves a different file than the message identifies" hazard the task predicted.
  - **`beginWrite` now takes an optional `targetDocumentId`.** Omitted, it writes the active document exactly as before, which is every menu- and shortcut-driven Save. Given one, it takes a **separate path**: no `flushActiveSession` and no active-buffer check, because only the active document has an editor working copy, and `state.activeBuffer.documentId` is by definition some other document for a background target — running that check would have refused every `Save to recreate` on a non-active tab by construction. Its revision comes from the projection's own `contentRevision` for that document. Go needed no change: `Save` resolves the document by id from `service.state.documents` (`save.go:48-54`) and never required it to be active.
  - **`finishWrite` no longer reads `activeDocument` for the confirmation.** It resolves the written document from `documentsById[documentId]`, because a background save was otherwise labelled with the *foreground* document's encoding. `safeFilename` was already correct — it prefers `result.data.targetPath`.
  - **`save-to-recreate` is its own `NotificationRemediationAction`, carrying `intent: 'save'`.** It needs no `intent` from the caller, for the same reason `copy-path` does not: it names its own command and needs only the document the toast is about. It is pushed before `Copy path`, which is the contract's order.
  - **The T142 assertion that pinned the drop was superseded, not deleted.** `classifiedNotification.test.ts`'s *T142 drops Save to recreate rather than rendering a control with no command* asserted behaviour whose own comment named T160 as its owner. It is rewritten as *T160 offers both Save to recreate and Copy path for a detached not-found*, **and the rule it protected is re-proved separately** by a new case on a member that genuinely has no command behind it (`Retry` with no document), so the drop path did not become unproved when this one started rendering.
  - **Gates:** baseline captured at `4f391957` before the first edit, every gate exit 0 with findings parsed (`lint=0=clean=2`, the two pre-existing `react-refresh` warnings) — no `UNRELIABLE`. After: `just check` green, `Test Suites: 80 passed`, `Tests: 640 passed`, 0 recipe failures. `just check` first failed on `frontend-format-check` for `App.tsx` alone and was corrected with `prettier --write`. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1–M6 all PASS. **`just e2e-test` was run and diffed rather than skipped**, because this adds a rendered control and `just check` never runs Playwright: `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted`.
  - **Not proved: the real binary, and the disappearance race end to end.** No host walkthrough was performed — deleting a backing file in the packaged `.app`, seeing the detached `not-found` toast, and clicking `Save to recreate` to watch the file come back — so FR-FT-037's *race* clause is not claimed by this task. What is proved is at the jsdom level: the pair is offered, and the recreate runs against the named document.

## Phase 23: Convergence — the contract the call sites still misstate

Assessed 2026-08-16 after Phase 21's Batch A (T159, T125, T146, T142, T126). Checked the
eight-category error contract, FR-FT-005/015/021/037/045/046/048, the bridge-shape plan decision,
and Constitution I–III. **One finding.** The `conflict` row's Reload/Keep mine/Skip were verified as
delivered by `ExternalChangePrompt.tsx:107-148` rather than the toast, so their absent Go call sites
are not a gap; the dedup-overwrite hazard is closed at `notificationsSlice.ts:162`; and the dev
mock's per-category table is a strict subset of Go's permitted table, which cannot hide a defect.
`Save to recreate` having no command and the nil host ports are already tracked as T160 and T161.

- [x] T162 Correct the 31 call sites that request a remediation their category forbids, per the classified error and remediation contract (contradicts). `permittedRemediations` (`internal/apperr/classified_error.go`) drops a forbidden member, so **nothing reaches a user wrongly and this is not a live defect** — it is the source stating an intent the contract refuses. Three pairings, 31 production lines, all in `internal/appmodel/`: `ClassifiedNotFound` with `RemediationCancel` (**25 sites**), `ClassifiedSystemCommandFailure` with `RemediationCancel` (**3** — `service.go:272`, `save.go:122`, `close_plan.go:425`), and `ClassifiedNotFound` with `RemediationRetry` (**3** — `close_plan.go:130,292,681`).
  - **Why it matters despite being invisible.** The coercion is a net, not the mechanism — T123 said so when it added it. A reader cannot tell which of these 31 sites meant "message-only" and which is a genuine mistake that the net happens to be catching, and the next call site copied from one of them inherits the error. The contract permits `not-found` only message-only removal or `Save to recreate` plus `Copy path`, and `system-command-failure` only `Retry` and `Copy path`.
  - **Decide per site, do not bulk-replace with `RemediationNone`.** Some of the 25 `not-found` + `Cancel` sites are refusing a command against a document that is gone, where message-only is right; others may want the `Save to recreate` plus `Copy path` pair now that T125 makes a pair expressible. Read each.
  - **Fix the seam too.** Nothing stops site 32. Consider making `Validate` failure a build- or test-time error for the constructor's own callers, or add a test that walks `internal/` for `NewClassifiedError` call sites and asserts each literal pairing is permitted — the coercion would then be unreachable rather than load-bearing.
  - **Tests/evidence**: per corrected site, an assertion that the emitted error carries the row the contract names, failing first against the current literal.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17. The count was 32, not 31, and two were not in `internal/appmodel/`.** `internal/file/document_reader.go:168,180` state the same forbidden `not-found` + `Cancel` pairing, so the task's "all of them in `internal/appmodel/`" was wrong. A hand-counted inventory drifts; the walker cannot, which is the argument for building the seam first.
  - **The seam was built first and drove the work.** `TestArchitectureClassifiedRemediationsMatchTheirCategory` (root `architecture_test.go`) walks `internal/` and fails on any call whose literal category and literal remediation the contract forbids. Red at 32 findings, green at 0, and `permittedRemediations` is now a net nothing reaches — unreachable rather than load-bearing.
  - **Matched by shape, not by function name.** The literals are almost never at `NewClassifiedError`; they sit at the wrappers — `refusedWrite`, `classifiedOpenError`, `closePlanRefused`, `conflictRefused`, `tabTransitionFailure`, `pathCommandFailure`, `classifiedReadError`. The walker flags any call passing exactly one `apperr.Classified*` and one or more `apperr.Remediation*`, so a wrapper added later is covered without extending the test. `RemediationNone` is exempt: it requests nothing and can contradict no row.
  - **The contract's table is restated in the test rather than imported.** Importing `remediationsByCategory` would make the test agree with whatever that map says, including a mistake in the map — it would prove consistency, not conformance to the spec.
  - **Three groups, decided per site rather than bulk-replaced.** (1) **29 → message-only.** Every `not-found` site says the referenced thing is gone — "The document is no longer open", "could not be found", "The file no longer exists", "The close plan is no longer active". None is the *detached* case the contract's second `not-found` option is for: a detached document is still **open** with its file missing, and that path is `copy_path.go`'s `revealDetachedFailure`, which already sends the `Save to recreate` plus `Copy path` pair. The three `not-found` + `Retry` close-plan sites join them, because re-issuing a dead plan id refuses identically — T164's reasoning. (2) **2 → `Retry`.** `service.go:290` (Open dialog unavailable) and `save.go:137` (Save dialog unavailable) are `system-command-failure`, whose row names Retry, and both commands are genuinely re-issuable with frontend intents already wired by T156. These were being served message-only where the contract names an action. (3) **1 → message-only despite its row naming Retry.** `close_plan.go:425` is the same Save-dialog refusal inside a close plan, and T164 records that a close-plan failure cannot be re-issued from where it is reported — Retry there would be the button-that-calls-nothing this contract exists to prevent.
  - **Only the two `Retry` sites changed what a user receives**, so only they got emitted-error assertions (`TestUnavailableHostDialogsOfferRetryRatherThanNothing`). The other 30 became message-only, which is byte-for-byte what the coercion already produced — an emitted assertion there would prove the coercion, not the fix. Red-first verified by reverting `service.go:290` to `Cancel`: `Open dialog refusal remediations = [], want Retry`.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `0a97ccdf` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 652 passed`, lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `go test ./...` green; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.1m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`. The new test is selected by `just go-archtest` (`go test -run TestArchitecture`), which is the gate that is never baseline-diffed and must be green outright — so the seam is enforced on every run rather than advisory.

- [ ] T163 Decide what the tablist may own besides its tabs, per FR-FT-047 (partial, opened by T145). T145 repaired the ownership defect that mattered — `role="tab"` had no owning `tablist`, because a plain `<div className={styles.tabItem}>` sat between them, and Chromium reported the tablist's children as `generic, generic`. With the wrapper marked `role="presentation"` the tree now reads `tab, button, tab, button, button`: the tabs are owned, and the per-tab close controls (`DocumentTabs.tsx`, the `styles.tabClose` button) and the `+` control (`data-tab-new="true"`) are owned alongside them. ARIA does not forbid a `tablist` from having other children, and axe's `aria-required-parent`/`aria-required-children` pair is satisfied, so **this is not a live defect** — it is an unmade decision recorded rather than left implicit.
  - **Why T145 did not settle it.** Every available remedy costs something the task had no mandate to spend. `aria-owns` over elements that are already DOM descendants is explicitly discouraged by the ARIA specification. `role="presentation"` on the close control is ignored, because presentational-role conflict resolution exempts focusable elements. Nesting the close control inside the tab button, which is what VS Code does, is invalid HTML for a `<button>` and changes the strip's box structure. Moving the `+` control out of the strip moves parity-locked pixels, and FR-FT-050 makes the binding's computed values the authority.
  - **What a decision looks like.** Either accept the current tree and say so in the accessibility notes, or restructure the strip so the tablist owns only tabs — and if the latter, the parity comparator must be re-measured first, not after. `frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts` already reads the real tree over a CDP `Accessibility.getFullAXTree` session in `T145 exposes every tab as an owned child of the tablist controlling the editor panel`; extend that case rather than writing a second one.
  - **Tests/evidence**: whichever way it goes, the AX-tree assertion must name the accepted child roles explicitly, so that a later change to the strip's contents fails rather than passes silently.


- [ ] T164 Honour `Retry` on the close-plan path, per the classified error and remediation contract and FR-FT-015 (partial, opened by T156). T156 wired the entry commands and tab activation and refused this one, because the retry cannot be issued from where the failure is reported. `reportClosePlanError` (`frontend/src/App.tsx`) fires inside `processClosePlanResult` and `completeClosePlan`. A stale-tab-set close refusal — "The tab set changed; close must be retried." (`internal/appmodel/close_plan.go:44`) and "The tab set changed while autosave work drained." (`:88`) — is not re-issued by re-running what failed: it needs `prepareClose(kind, targets, freshRevision)`, and neither reporting site holds `kind` or `targets`. `App.tsx`'s `onCloseDocument` does, three frames up. The plan id the sites do hold is exactly the one the backend refused as stale, so re-executing it would refuse identically.
  - **Three arms, three different original requests, and one of them must not be re-issued at all.** A tab close can be re-prepared. A **native quit** cannot: `cancelNativeClose()` has already run by the time the failure is reported, and re-issuing would re-trigger a quit the operating system already asked about and the user already saw resolved. The recovery `discard-all` arm is a third shape again. Any fix must decide each arm explicitly rather than thread one descriptor through all three.
  - **`DocumentTabs.tsx`'s close arm belongs to this task, and it carries a finding of its own.** In the shipped application that `reportClassifiedError` is **unreachable**: `App.onCloseDocument` returns `{status: 'noop', orderedDocumentIds}` when the plan refuses, having already reported through `reportClosePlanError`, so the strip sees no error. It fires only when a caller supplies no `onCloseDocument` and the raw adapter is used — that is, in tests. Decide whether the arm is dead code to remove or a real path to reconnect; do not leave it as a third reporting site nobody reaches.
  - **Tests/evidence**: refuse a close once on a stale revision, click Retry, and assert `prepareClose` is re-issued with the *original* `kind` and `targets` against the fresh revision and succeeds — failing first. Separately assert the native-quit arm offers no Retry, whichever way that decision goes.
  - **Do not add an intent without the command behind it.** That rule is why T156 filed this rather than shipping a button.

- [ ] T165 Honour `Retry` on the tab reorder arm, per FR-FT-034 and the classified error and remediation contract (partial, opened by T156). The command is re-issuable — `adapter.reorderDocument(documentId, targetIndex, freshRevision)` is well defined after a tab-set change — but the success feedback is not reachable from where a retry runs. FR-FT-034 requires a completed move to be announced, and `DocumentTabs.tsx` builds that announcement (`t('editor.tab.moved', { filename, position, count })`) from the tab's disambiguated label and the strip length. `onRemediate` lives in `App.tsx` and has neither the labels nor the ordered strip. A Retry that moved the tab and said nothing would satisfy the contract's remediation row and break FR-FT-034, so T156 declined to add the intent.
  - **Two shapes to choose between**: give `App.tsx` enough of the strip's state to announce (it already owns `announceRemediation` and a live region), or give the tab strip its own remediation execution seam so the command and its announcement stay together. The second is cleaner and larger.
  - **Tests/evidence**: refuse a move once on a stale revision, click Retry, and assert the reorder is re-issued against the fresh revision **and** that the move announcement is emitted — failing first on both.

- [ ] T166 Walk the real binary for `Copy path` and `Reveal in file manager`, per FR-FT-037 (evidence gap, opened by T161). T161 fixed the composition root so both host ports are non-nil in a production build, and proved it two ways: `TestHostPortsAreNonNilInTheProductionCompositionRoot` (`internal/application/host_ports_wiring_test.go`) reflects over the real `AppModelService` built by `NewApplicationContextHolder`, and `TestPlatformClipboardWriterPipesTheExactPathToTheHostTool` (`internal/file/clipboard_unix_test.go`) puts a stub on `PATH` and asserts the exact bytes piped to it, spaces and non-ASCII included. Neither is the walkthrough T161 asked for, and T161 did not perform it.
  - **What is still unproven** is the last hop: that `pbcopy` receiving the path actually leaves it on the system pasteboard for a paste into another application, and that `open -R` selects the file in Finder rather than only opening its folder. Both are host behaviours no Go test in this repo can observe, and the Playwright suites run the dev bridge mock, which reimplements both commands in TypeScript — the mock is precisely what T161 bypassed.
  - **How to run it**: `just build`, then check `ps -o lstart` against the binary mtime before trusting the window (`open` raises a stale process — the trap recorded against earlier host evidence). Open a path-backed document, choose `Copy path` from the tab context menu, paste into a text field, and confirm the exact canonical absolute path. Then choose `Reveal in file manager` and confirm the file is selected, not merely its folder. Repeat with a filename containing a space and a non-ASCII character.
  - **Also confirm the announcement** FR-FT-037 requires alongside the copy: `Copied path for {safe filename}` in a transient polite live region naming only the safe basename or the disambiguated tab label.
  - Sequence before **T158**, for the same reason T161 was: SC-FT-013 evidence should not be written against a binary whose FR-FT-037 path commands have never been observed working end to end.

- [ ] T167 Wire the host ports in `cmd/native-evidence`, or state that it must not have them, per FR-FT-037 and Constitution III (gap, opened by T161). T161 made `NewApplicationContextHolder` build the document model through `appmodel.NewAppModelServiceForHost`, so every host reached through the holder now gets a clipboard writer and a reveal port. `cmd/native-evidence` does not go through the holder for its model: `main_native_evidence.go:120` calls `NewAppModelServiceWithLayoutRepositoryAndTimer` directly, so both host ports are still nil there.
  - **Not currently a defect** — the evidence driver measures autosave and explicit-save latency and never invokes `CopyPath` or `RevealInFileManager`, so nothing takes the nil branch. It is recorded because it is the one remaining construction site that can silently omit a host port, and because T161's own analysis named it as one of the three blind layers.

The first is cleaner and removes the divergence rather than annotating it.

- [x] T168 Give a dismissed normalization prompt a way to release its authorization, per FR-FT-011 (gap, opened by T135). T135 closed the autosave arm of this leak — `runAutosave` discarded a minted single-use token on every refused attempt and now routes it through `CancelNormalization`. The manual arm is still open, and it is the one a user drives.
  - **The shape of it**: `Save` on a mixed-ending document mints an authorization into `service.normalizations` and returns it as `WriteResult.DecisionToken`, which is how the prompt is raised. If the user then dismisses the prompt instead of authorizing, nothing consumes the token and nothing cancels it — it stays for the process lifetime, and the next Save mints another. `CancelNormalization` (`internal/appmodel/save.go`) is the right primitive and, after T135, its only caller is autosave; it is **not** on the Wails-bound surface — `internal/appmodel/handler.go` contains no `Normalization` method at all — so the frontend has no way to call it. `frontend/src/ui/widgets/NormalizationPrompt.tsx` and its owner in `App.tsx` simply close the prompt.
  - **Not the same severity as the autosave leak** — it takes a deliberate user action per token rather than one per debounce — but it is unbounded in the same way, and `service.normalizations` is never swept.
  - **Two shapes to choose between**: bind a cancel command so dismissing the prompt releases the token it was raised with (matches the single-use model already in the code, and gives the primitive the caller it was written for), or make authorizations self-expiring against the document's content revision, so a token is dead once the document moves on. The first is smaller and does not introduce a time or revision policy the spec does not describe.
  - **Tests/evidence**: red-first, refuse a mixed-ending Save to mint a token, dismiss the prompt, and assert `service.normalizations` is empty — then assert a subsequent Save with the dismissed token is refused rather than honoured, so the release cannot be implemented as a no-op that merely stops counting.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17**, on the first of the two shapes: `CancelNormalization` was bound rather than a revision-expiry policy invented. It is the smaller change, it gives the primitive the caller it was written for, and it introduces no time or revision rule the spec does not describe.
  - **This is the first new Wails-bound command in the feature**, so it crossed every layer. Go: `CancelNormalization` now returns `apperr.ClassifiedVoidResult` instead of nothing (the no-payload envelope whose failures belong to the classified contract), gained a `CancelNormalization` entry on `AppModelServiceAPI`, and a bound handler in `internal/appmodel/handler.go`. Bindings: `wails generate module` regenerated `AppModelHandler.{js,d.ts}`, both still mode `100755`. Frontend: `DocumentWriteBindings`/`DocumentWriteAdapter` gained the method with its arity guard, `adapter/index.ts` wired it to the generated binding, and `App.tsx`'s manual prompt `onCancel` now hands the token back before closing.
  - **The dev bridge mock got it too** (`src/dev/bridge-mock/go/appmodel/AppModelHandler.ts`). Omitting it would not have failed `just check` — that gate never runs Playwright — but every e2e run would have hit a command the mock does not expose, the moment a dismissal occurred. The mock mints no authorizations, so it accepts and reports success, which is what Go does for an unknown token.
  - **The release is deliberately idempotent and never an error.** An unknown token, or one naming another document, returns success: a dismissal can legitimately arrive after the authorization was already consumed or invalidated, and in each case the caller's intent is satisfied. Reporting a classified failure would put an error in front of a user who did nothing wrong, and the security property is identical either way — the token buys nothing.
  - **Red first at both ends, and the Go red was a compile error**, which is the honest shape for a signature change: `service.CancelNormalization(...) (no value) used as value`. The frontend red was `Expected: "document-1", "authorization-token"` against a `cancelNormalization` that was never called.
  - **The Go test proves release, not bookkeeping.** After the dismissal it asserts `service.normalizations` is empty **and** that a replayed Save with the dismissed token is answered with a *fresh* `needs-normalization` carrying a **different** token. Without that second half, a no-op that merely stopped counting would pass.
  - **Both hand-listed handler tables were extended**, or the new bound method would have been the only one with no shape and no panic-recovery coverage: the signature table gained `{"CancelNormalization", 3, ClassifiedVoidResult}` — three inputs, no context, because releasing an in-memory authorization touches neither disk nor the operating system — and the recovery list gained a case asserting the classified refusal. `just archtest` checks the four bound-handler rules mechanically; the tables are what prove this handler in particular.
  - **`just gen-check` needs the regenerated bindings staged.** It runs `wails generate module` then `git diff --exit-code -- frontend/wailsjs/`, which compares the working tree with the index — so a *legitimate* change to the bound surface fails the gate until `git add`. AGENTS.md records the mode-bit version of this trap, not this one.
  - **Out of scope, deliberately:** the close-plan prompt's own `onCancel` routes through `onCloseNormalizationDecision(false)`, a different mechanism that resolves the plan. This task is the manual arm, which is what it says.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `39835ceb` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0 — including `gen-check`, which re-ran `wails generate module` and found no drift — `Tests: 652 passed` (+1 frontend), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `go test ./...` green across every package; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`.
  - **Not proved: the host.** The release is proved at the service, the handler, the adapter seam and the prompt. Nobody has dismissed a real normalization prompt in `just build` and confirmed the authorization is gone — that needs the running binary, and joins **T166**, **T181**, **T186**.

- [x] T169 Bind the external-change reload's active-buffer install to a revision, per FR-FT-030 (partial, opened by T140's predecessor T128). FR-FT-030 covers "activating a tab **or reloading the active document**", and requires the resulting acknowledgement to be applied "only while both values still match the confirmed active projection". T128 routed all five *activation* installs through `useGuardedActivation` (`frontend/src/ui/widgets/editorSession.ts`), which checks generation, requested identity, projection revision, active identity and `contentRevision`. The reload arm was left where it was: `onExternalConflictDecision` in `frontend/src/App.tsx` handles `decision === 'reload'` by re-reading `getState()` and installing on an **identity check alone** — `refreshedState.activeBuffer?.documentId === current.documentId`, falling back to `result.activeBuffer` with no check at all — so a reload whose acknowledgement is overtaken by a newer one, or which lands after the user has switched documents, installs its text on the strength of a matching id.
  - **Why T128 stopped short**: its five named sites were the entry and activation handlers, and the same statement also serves the close-plan and editor-state-recovery handoffs (`replaceActiveBuffer` at the close-plan, recovery and reload sites). Folding those under a guard none of them has ever been tested against, in the same commit that first wired the guard up at all, would have put three unrelated flows behind one untried check.
  - **Watch for the `omitempty` asymmetry T128 found**: Go tags `DocumentMetadata.ContentRevision` `contentRevision,omitempty` (`internal/apperr/results.go:132`) but tags `ActiveBuffer.DocumentRevision` plainly (`:160`), so an unedited document projects no `contentRevision` and acknowledges `documentRevision: 0`. `acceptsActivationAcknowledgement` now defaults both sides; anything new comparing them must do the same or it will reject every document at revision zero.
  - **Tests/evidence**: red-first, resolve a reload acknowledgement after the active document has changed and assert its text is never installed — and check it fails against the current code because the *stale text was installed*, not because a double was missing.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17. The task understated it: the `else` arm had no check at all, and it was the arm that fired.** `if (refreshedState.activeBuffer?.documentId === current.documentId)` installed on identity alone, and its `else if (result.activeBuffer !== undefined)` installed `result.activeBuffer` **unconditionally** — reachable exactly when the refreshed state showed a *different* active document. So the unguarded path was not an edge case of the race; it *was* the race. A reload overtaken by a tab switch wrote the reloaded document's text over whatever the user had switched to.
  - **Routed through the existing guard rather than given a new check.** `useGuardedActivation`'s own contract says a handler claims a generation with `begin()` before issuing its command and hands the answer to `acknowledge()`, "so a sixth handler cannot reintroduce the defect by forgetting a check it never had to write" — reload *is* that sixth handler. `activation.begin()` now runs before `reloadFromDisk`, and the install is `activation.acknowledge(reloadGeneration, acknowledgement, current.documentId)`. Passing `current.documentId` as the requested identity is what makes an acknowledgement for another document fail `request.documentId === acknowledgement.documentId`. The fresher-answer preference is kept — `refreshedState.activeBuffer` when it is the right document, else `result.activeBuffer` — but neither is installed directly any more.
  - **Red first, and for the stated reason:** `expect(element).not.toHaveTextContent('stale reload text')` failed because the stale text *was* present, not because a double was missing. The test holds the reload promise open, switches the projection to `document-2` while it is in flight, then resolves it with `document-1`'s buffer — the same shape T128 uses for a superseded activation.
  - **The guard discriminates.** The pre-existing *Reload replaces the active same-document buffer from authoritative state* still passes, so the legitimate same-document reload is accepted and only the overtaken one is refused. A guard that rejected both would have looked identical on the new test alone.
  - **A type error in the new test surfaced as an unrelated failure.** `renderer.test.ts`'s `STORY-014-AC-6 keeps renderer dependencies and assets offline` shells out to a real `npm run build`, so `App.test.tsx(2908,9): error TS2322` — a `resolve` contravariance slip in the test's own promise plumbing — was reported as an offline-assets failure. `App.test.tsx` passed in isolation and the whole-suite run did not. Worth knowing for the next reader: when that offline test fails, read the build error first. `eslint` was clean throughout; only `tsc` saw it.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `96bfbbcd` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 650 passed` (+1), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings` — the same two pre-existing findings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`.
  - **Not proved: the host.** The race is proved in jsdom against the mock bridge. Nobody has edited a file externally on the real `just build` binary, chosen Reload, and switched tabs mid-flight. That belongs with the outstanding host walks (**T166**, **T181**, **T186**).

- [x] T170 Report an activation refusal met on the `Retry` path, per FR-FT-031 and the classified error and remediation contract (gap, opened by T140). `onRemediate`'s `activate-document` arm in `frontend/src/App.tsx` ends `if (result === undefined || result.error !== undefined) return;` — a refused retry dismisses nothing and reports nothing. On the tab strip that is fine, because `DocumentTabs.activateDocument` owns the reporting funnel and every click goes through it; the `Retry` control does not, calling `onActivateDocument` directly. So a stale-tab-set refusal met *on the retry* leaves the original toast standing with the original message and no sign that the second attempt also failed.
  - **Not the outgoing-flush case**: T140 made `onActivateDocument` report that one itself, so the flush failure is surfaced on both paths. What is unreported is every other refusal the backend can answer an activation with — the `conflict` stale-tab-set refusal above all, which is precisely the one a `Retry` is most likely to meet twice.
  - **The same silence covers the other four intents by a different route**: `onNewDocument`, `onOpenDocument`, `onOpenRecentFile` and `onReopenLastFile` all call `reportEntryError` internally, so their retries do report. Only `activate-document` delegates its reporting to a component the retry never reaches, which is why this is one arm and not five.
  - **Tests/evidence**: red-first, click `Retry` on an `activate-document` refusal whose re-issue is refused again, and assert a notification describing the second refusal — then assert the first notification is still not dismissed, so the fix cannot be a dismissal that merely hides the problem.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17.** The refusal is now reported from `onRemediate`'s arm, scoped to `activate-document`, and the standing notification is left in place: the contract dedups the repeat onto it and raises `count` to 2, which is what `Toast.tsx:64-66` renders as the repeat indicator. Red first on exactly that — `Expected: 2, Received: 1`.
  - **It must not move into `onActivateDocument`, and the symmetry is a trap.** Making the handler self-report like its four siblings looks obviously right and is wrong: in production `DocumentTabs.runActivation` calls `onActivateDocument` (`DocumentTabs.tsx:294`) and `DocumentTabs.activateDocument` then reports the same error (`:330`), so a self-reporting handler reports **twice per tab click**. That is the defect already recorded at `notificationsSlice.ts:180-183`, where a Save reported by two paths put the Retry control behind a `×2` that read like the dedup count working correctly. The `Retry` control is the one path the strip's funnel never sees, so the arm is where the report belongs — which is what this task said, and the reason is now written down.
  - **Scoped to one intent.** The `case` covers five; the other four call `reportEntryError` inside their own handlers, so reporting them here would duplicate them too.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `3736ff27` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 651 passed` (+1), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings` — the same two pre-existing findings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`. The first `just check` failed on `frontend-format-check` for `App.test.tsx` alone — the new case was appended after the last `prettier` pass — and all three were re-run on the formatted tree rather than reasoning that whitespace in a Jest file cannot reach Playwright.
  - **Filed: T188.** The "one activation failure, one report" invariant this task depends on has no test, and a partial one would be worse than none — see T188 for why neither existing suite can cover it alone.

- [x] T171 Remove the parity-only launcher height variant, per FR-FT-054 (contradicts). `frontend/src/ui/widgets/Launcher.tsx` still reads `?parity-case` from `window.location.search` in production code and adds `styles.parityLauncher`, which `frontend/src/ui/widgets/Launcher.module.css:143-167` defines as three fixed heights — `313px`, `316px` at ≤376px, `301px` between 377px and 768px — plus `flex: 1 1 0%`, `min-height: auto` and `overflow: visible`. FR-FT-054 permits the parity route to seed a fixture, not to restyle a component, and the whole-screen contract those heights were built for was withdrawn on 2026-08-14: the launcher is not one of the 14 pixel-compared component keys, so nothing measures them. T154 removed the string half of the same branch and left this deliberately, because it is a style question rather than a string one. `Launcher.test.tsx` `T045 keeps the parity launcher inside the reference content bands` asserts the CSS source text and must go with it — a test asserting a rule no selector can reach is a false record of coverage, not coverage. Confirm first that no reference-side assertion depends on the launcher's height, then delete the class, the rules and the case together.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17. The precondition was established with evidence before anything was deleted**, and it was the whole risk in the task.
    - The pixel-compared regions are the `targeted:` keys in `frontend/e2e/targeted-manifest.ts` — file menu, settings menu, settings overflow, view menu, about menu, tab strip, toolbar and the rest. **None is the launcher, and no selector in that manifest reaches it.**
    - A grep across all of `frontend/e2e` for anything reading a launcher height — `boundingBox`, `offsetHeight`, `clientHeight`, `toHaveCSS` — returns nothing. `launcher-binding.test.ts` draws the frame, block, type scale, actions and recents list from the binding and asserts no height at all.
    - **The manifest does carry `launcher-first-run` and `launcher-six-file`** (`e2e/parity/manifest.test.ts:48-49`), which looks alarming on a class-name grep. They are **state** entries feeding `[parity states] 40/40`, not pixel comparisons — recorded here because the next reader will hit the same false alarm.
  - **Removed together, because none of the three can stand without the others**: the `?parity-case` branch and the `parityRoute` const in `Launcher.tsx`, the `.parityLauncher` rule plus its two media overrides in `Launcher.module.css` (the `@media (max-width: 376px)` block keeps its `.launcher { align-items: start }` rule, which is production styling and shares the block; the `@media (min-width: 377px) and (max-width: 768px)` block held nothing else and went whole), and `Launcher.test.tsx`'s `T045 keeps the parity launcher inside the reference content bands`.
  - **Deleting that test is not the forbidden kind.** It asserted the *CSS source text* of the rule by regex — `readFileSync` on the stylesheet, then `toMatch(/height:\s*313px/)`. Once the rule is gone it could only assert an absence, and keeping it would mean keeping dead CSS to satisfy a test that measures nothing a user can reach. Its now-dead `readSource` helper and the two `node:` imports went with it.
  - **The proving evidence is the parity suite, not a new unit test.** The `?parity-case` launcher states are exercised by `launcher-first-run` and `launcher-six-file`; if removing the heights had moved anything the harness measures, `just e2e-test` would have caught it. Writing a unit test asserting the *absence* of a CSS rule would have recreated exactly the false-coverage shape T045 had.

- [x] T172 Give `ModalShell` one labelling mechanism, per FR-FT-047 (partial). `frontend/src/ui/primitives/ModalShell.tsx` sets **both** `aria-label={title}` and `aria-labelledby={labelledBy}` on the dialog section. `aria-labelledby` wins, so `aria-label` is inert on every dialog in the application and `title` never reaches a user as the accessible name — it is dead weight that reads like a contract. FR-FT-047 requires correct accessible names; the name is correct today, but two mechanisms where one is silently ignored is how the T138 divergence stayed invisible (the parity branch exploited exactly this by moving `aria-label` onto the backdrop and stripping the section's role, so the same prompt answered to two different names by route, and three e2e cases were written against the wrong one). Decide which mechanism is authoritative — `aria-labelledby` at the heading is the recommended default, because every caller already renders that heading — and remove the other. This is pre-existing and was not introduced by T138.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17 on the recommended default**: `aria-labelledby` survives, `aria-label` is gone.
  - **One correction: the callers do not render the heading — `ModalShell` does.** `<h1 className={styles.title} id={labelledBy}>` is inside the component, so no caller can omit it and there was no per-caller audit to run. That inverts the risk the task implies: removing `aria-label` cannot strip a dialog's name, because the element `aria-labelledby` points at is guaranteed by construction. The three production callers (`App.tsx`'s recovery-quit prompt, `ClosePrompt`, `ExternalChangePrompt`) were still checked individually.

The invariance assertion now covers the mechanism that actually names the dialog, which is the one the parity branch T138 removed had tampered with by moving `aria-label` onto the backdrop. `shellAriaLabel: null` is kept in the snapshot as an explicit absence, so a second mechanism reappearing fails this case.

After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 653 passed` (+1), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`. The interface gate is the load-bearing evidence here: the e2e suites carry **29** `getByRole('dialog', …)` queries, several matching on the accessible name, and every one still resolves — which is what confirms that removing the losing mechanism changed no computed name.
