The others are `CodeEditor.tsx:126,184,333,361,377-381` (a parity-only font family, 13px size and 23.4px line height, plus a document-id special case), `EditorChrome.tsx:159`, `PreviewPane.tsx:46`, `TabContextMenu.tsx:90-93,138`, `AppShell.tsx:56-61`, and `Launcher.module.css` via T171. FR-FT-054 permits the route to seed a fixture and hold capture conditions fixed; it does not permit a different component structure, and a harness that measures a substituted component is measuring nothing that ships. **Decide the direction before writing code** — each branch is either (a) a fixture seed that should move into the mock backend, (b) a capture condition that should be expressed on the reference side as an FR-FT-056 variant, or (c) a substitution that should simply be deleted — and classify all seven before touching any. Expect the `SettingsDialog` case to need the owner.

- [x] T174 Decide whether FR-FT-004's reservation *cancellation* arm is reachable, or record it as vacuous (gap, opened by T137). FR-FT-004 requires Open, recent-file, reopen and Save As to "hold a process-local reservation for the prepared canonical identity until activation, commit, **cancellation**, or failure". `appmodel.CancelPreparedOpen` (`internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle.go`) is the only implementation of the cancellation arm and it has no production caller — T137 kept it for exactly that reason rather than deleting a clause of the requirement. It is unreachable structurally, not accidentally: `OpenPath` calls `PrepareOpen` and `CommitPreparedOpen` in adjacent statements, every `PrepareOpen` refusal returns before a reservation is created, and `CommitPreparedOpen` deletes the reservation on every branch, so no reservation can outlive a prepare and there is nothing to cancel between the two. But FR-FT-004 also describes the sequence that *would* make it reachable — "Before any prepared New/Open/focus transition, the frontend MUST flush and await the outgoing identity-bound content and view state. The backend MUST then revalidate the expected tab-set revision and reservation" — which places a frontend round trip between prepare and commit. Today that flush happens *before* the single `OpenPath` bridge call, so the two-phase boundary never crosses the bridge at all.
  - **Owner decision required, and it is the whole task.** Either (a) expose the prepare/commit boundary across the bridge so the frontend flush sits between them and a failed flush cancels the reservation — which makes the clause real and gives `CancelPreparedOpen` its caller, at the cost of a two-call Open protocol and a new way for a reservation to leak if the frontend dies mid-sequence; or (b) record that the clause is satisfied vacuously by the single-call design, amend the requirement to say so, and delete `CancelPreparedOpen` with its test cases.
  - Do not resolve this by adding a caller that exists only to have one.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17 on the owner's decision: option (b), recorded vacuous.** FR-FT-004 carries a 2026-08-17 amendment stating that the single-call Open design satisfies the cancellation arm vacuously, with the structural reason spelled out, and `CancelPreparedOpen` is deleted along with the `fmt` import it was the last user of.
  - **Option (a) was declined on its cost, not its difficulty.** Splitting prepare and commit across the bridge would give the clause a caller, and would simultaneously introduce a two-call Open protocol and a fresh way for a reservation to leak if the frontend dies mid-sequence — a new failure mode bought to satisfy a sentence rather than a user-observable behaviour.
  - **The deletion did not cost coverage, and one test got stronger.** Three test sites called it. `TestIdentityReservationLifecycle` existed only to drive it, and survives as `TestPreparedOpenReservesExactlyOneIdentity` keeping its real assertion — that a prepared Open reserves exactly one identity. The other two called it only as trailing cleanup on a discarded service, which proved nothing; in `TestPendingReservationCountsTowardLimit` the now-unread `preparations` slice was replaced with `len(service.reservations) != maxOpenDocuments`, so the test now asserts the pending reservations are actually *held* rather than only that the 41st is refused.
  - **A comment block was left where the function was**, naming the amendment, so the next reader finds a resolved decision rather than an apparent omission and re-adds one.

- [x] T175 Prove or retire FR-FT-027's "cancel in-flight long operations" clause (gap, opened by T134). FR-FT-027 requires shutdown to "cancel in-flight long operations and drain accepted layout, editor, and autosave work". T134 built the drain and can prove all three of its halves. It could not prove a *cancellation* of a long operation, because no long-running operation in this backend accepts one: `file.AtomicReplace` has no cancellation point by design, `file.ReadClassifiedStable` takes no context, and the write coordinator serializes rather than interrupts. T134 read the clause as covering debounced work whose document has become ineligible — which `flushAutosaveForClose` does cancel, and which `TestDrainBeforeCloseCancelsWorkThatCanNoLongerRun` proves — and said so in its closure note rather than claiming the clause outright.
  - **Establish which reading the requirement intends.** If it means "a large read or write in progress must be abandoned", nothing implements it and the drain would need context-aware I/O throughout `internal/file` — a substantial change with a real correctness question attached (an abandoned `AtomicReplace` must still leave the target intact). If it means the debounce reading, the clause is built and the requirement text should say "cancel scheduled work that can no longer run" so the next audit does not re-open this.
  - Whichever way it goes, the anchor in `internal/appmodel/close_drain_test.go` must end up naming what the test actually proves.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17 on the owner's decision: the debounce reading governs.** FR-FT-027's first clause now reads "cancel scheduled work that can no longer run" under a 2026-08-17 amendment, which is what `flushAutosaveForClose` does and what `TestDrainBeforeCloseCancelsWorkThatCanNoLongerRun` proves.
  - **The anchor was the actual defect here, and it is fixed.** It read `Proves: FR-FT-027 (partial — the "cancel in-flight long operations" half…)` over a body that drives a debounce. That is an anchor asserting a claim its body does not make — the thing AGENTS.md calls worse than no anchor, because it converts a coverage gap into a false record of coverage. The comment now names the amended clause and records why the stronger reading was never proved: `AtomicReplace` has no cancellation point *by design*, `ReadClassifiedStable` takes no context, and the write coordinator serializes rather than interrupts.
  - **The strong reading was declined as a separate feature, not as too hard.** It needs context-aware I/O throughout `internal/file` plus a fresh correctness argument that an abandoned `AtomicReplace` leaves the target intact — which is the guarantee the whole write path exists to provide. That is not a clause of this requirement.
  - **No behaviour changed**; this task is a requirement amendment and a comment correction. `TestDrainBeforeCloseCancelsWorkThatCanNoLongerRun` passes unmodified in body.

- [x] T176 Make `App.test.tsx`'s translator double read the real catalogue (gap, opened by T124). `frontend/src/App.test.tsx` mocks `./i18n` with a hand-written `copy` map of about a dozen keys and falls back to returning the key itself for anything absent. That fallback is silent, so a component rendering a key that does not exist in `en.json` — or a typo'd key — produces a string, renders, and passes: the double cannot distinguish "translated" from "missing". T124 hit this directly. The two new `recovery.quit.documents*` keys were added to `en.json` and the prompt still rendered `recovery.quit.documents` as its accessible name until the map was edited too, which means the map is a second catalogue that has to be kept in step by hand and nothing checks that it is.
  - `frontend/src/test/i18nShim.ts` already exists for exactly this and is wired for the `^\.\./\.\./i18n$` depth in `jest.config.mjs`; it builds a real translator from `en.json`. The fix is to point `App.test.tsx` at it — `jest.mock('./i18n', () => jest.requireActual('./test/i18nShim'))` — or to widen the `moduleNameMapper` pattern so `./i18n` resolves to the shim at every depth.
  - **Expect fallout and budget for it.** Several assertions in that file match the hand-written strings, and any that differ from `en.json` will fail — each one is either a stale expectation to correct or a genuine catalogue gap to fill. Do not reconcile a difference by editing `en.json` to match the test without checking which of the two the user actually sees.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17, and there was no fallout at all** — all 47 cases passed on the swap. The two catalogues happened to agree wherever they overlapped, so nothing had to be reconciled and `en.json` was not touched.
  - **Which is exactly why the change needed a guard.** With no failing assertion, the swap is indistinguishable from a no-op. `T176 translates from en.json rather than a hand-maintained copy` pins the property directly, over three keys the superseded map never carried and this suite's production code does render — `editor.tabs`, `notification.count`, `editor.tab.moved`. Red-first verified by restoring a hand map: `Expected: "Document tabs", Received: "editor.tabs"`.
  - **The task's premise needs one correction.** Pointing at the shim does **not** make a missing key detectable: `createTranslator` also falls back to returning the key (`src/i18n/catalog.ts:75`), so a key absent from `en.json` still renders as itself in production and in tests. What the swap removes is the *second catalogue* — the hand map that had to be kept in step with `en.json` and that nothing checked, which is the defect T124 actually hit when two `recovery.quit.documents*` keys were added and the prompt kept rendering the bare key. Detecting a key missing from `en.json` is a different problem and is not claimed here.
  - **Scoped fix, deliberately.** `jest.mock('./i18n', () => jest.requireActual('./test/i18nShim'))` in the one file, rather than widening `moduleNameMapper` to catch `./i18n` at every depth. An audit for siblings found none: `App.test.tsx` was the only suite in `frontend/src` with an inline translator map, because every other one imports `../../i18n` and the existing mapper pattern already routed it to the shim. Widening the pattern would have changed resolution for suites that need no change.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `503f8721` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 653 passed` (+1), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`.
  - **The first e2e run failed and was not waved through.** `T026 retains at least 20 measured resize and divider samples` reported `0.65` against `assertShellTiming`'s 0.95 target, on a change that touches one Jest file and no production source. Diagnosed rather than re-run blind: the assertion is wall-clock, the machine was at load average 29 with 25 Playwright processes still draining from the previous chain, and the case passed alone on the same commit. The suite was then re-run after waiting for the machine to drain and came back 268/268. The underlying host-sensitivity is recorded against **T180**, which had named only the font and rasteriser risk.


- [x] T177 Build the two FR-FT-036 clauses T130 deferred: the strip's edge auto-scroll and the reduced-opacity dragged tab (gap, opened by T130). FR-FT-036 names five clauses — "show the insertion position **and reduced-opacity dragged tab**, allow Escape to cancel, **auto-scroll the strip near its edges**, and issue no reorder for a same-position drop". T130 built three of them plus the two guards, on the owner decision recorded in `decisions-phase-21.md` ("Build the core only: grab, insertion indicator, drop. Defer edge auto-scroll and the reduced-opacity ghost"). These two are what remains, and **nothing asserts either of them today** — every `// Proves: FR-FT-036` anchor in `frontend/src/ui/widgets/DocumentTabs.test.tsx` and `frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts` is partial and says so in as many words.
  - **The reduced-opacity dragged tab.** `DocumentTabs.tsx` already knows which tab is being dragged — `activeDrag.current.documentId`, set the moment the grab passes `TAB_DRAG_THRESHOLD_PX`. It is held in a ref rather than in state precisely because nothing renders from it yet; making the tab translucent means promoting it (or mirroring it) into state and adding a rule to `DocumentTabs.module.css`. Use the existing `--disabled-opacity` token or add a drag-specific one — **do not write a literal opacity-bearing colour**, `just archtest` catches stray literals. Prove it with a computed-style assertion in Playwright, not a class-name assertion in jsdom: the point of the clause is that the user can see which tab is in flight.
  - **The edge auto-scroll.** The strip is a scroll container only when its tabs overflow — `.tabStrip[data-tabs-overflowing='true']` gets `overflow-x: auto`, and `tabsOverflowing` is measured by a `ResizeObserver`, never counted. So the clause is reachable only in the overflowing case, and a test must **make the strip overflow by opening enough documents at the harness's width** rather than assuming a tab count. Auto-scroll while the pointer sits within some margin of the strip's leading or trailing edge, and stop when it leaves. Respect `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, which the stylesheet already honours for the row's other transitions.
  - **Two hazards the T130 work already paid for.** (1) `.application-frame` is the application window; `window.innerHeight` is not — measure the frame, not the browser viewport. (2) Making an element a scroll container costs ~332 deterministic antialiasing pixels against the parity reference, so do not widen `overflow-x: auto` beyond the `data-tabs-overflowing` case to make auto-scroll easier to implement.
  - When this lands, revisit the partial anchors T130 left behind and widen them — and only then may any anchor name FR-FT-036 without a parenthetical.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17.** Both clauses built, both proved in the browser, both anchors widened.
  - **Honouring the deferral rather than reversing it.** `decisions-phase-21.md` records the owner's T130 call — "Build the core only … **Defer** edge auto-scroll and the reduced-opacity ghost". That deferred them to a later task and T130 filed this one to carry them, so building them here is the decision being executed, not overturned.
  - **The five clauses split by what each level can observe, which is why they sit in two files.** Grab, insertion position, drop, Escape-cancel and the same-position no-op all turn on the presence or absence of a *command* — observable in `DocumentTabs.test.tsx` and invisible in a browser, where an unchanged order is also what a reorder-to-its-own-index produces. Opacity and scroll geometry are the exact inverse: jsdom has no layout engine, so both are only real in Playwright. The preamble in `DocumentTabs.test.tsx` and the anchor on T130's e2e case now say this explicitly instead of describing the two clauses as unproved.
  - **Reduced opacity.** `activeDrag` stays a ref — the pointer handlers need the synchronous read — and the id is *mirrored* into `draggingDocumentId` state, which is what lets the strip re-render on grab and release. The item carries `data-tab-dragging`, and the rule uses `var(--disabled-opacity)`. A drag-specific token was considered and rejected: `tokens.test.ts` checks palette completeness, so a second token would have to be declared in all six theme/mode combinations to carry one palette-independent number. Asserted as a **computed** style on the real strip, and asserted three ways — translucent while in flight, `1` on the tab *not* being dragged, and back to `1` after Escape — so it cannot pass on a strip-wide fade or a ghost left behind.
  - **Edge auto-scroll**, driven from pointer position rather than from move events, so the strip keeps travelling while the pointer is held still inside the margin — which is the behaviour, not an optimisation. `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` arms no interval and steps once per move instead: the capability is kept (a drag can still reach an off-screen tab) and only the self-driving animation is dropped.
  - **`overflow-x: auto` was deliberately not widened**, though it would have made the fixture trivial. It stays confined to `[data-tabs-overflowing='true']` because making an element a scroll container costs ~332 deterministic antialiasing pixels against the immutable reference. The test instead opens documents until the strip *reports* overflow, bounded by FR-FT-004's 40-document cap, rather than assuming a tab count that depends on filename width, theme and viewport.
  - **A CSS insertion bug worth recording.** The first attempt appended the rule after the first `}` following `.tabItem {` — which belongs to a CSS snippet **quoted inside a comment** (`…max-width:190px}`), splitting the comment and breaking the stylesheet. Brace depth still balanced to 0, so a balance check said nothing, and the symptom was not a style failure but the whole application failing to render: `waiting for getByRole('button', { name: 'New tab' })`. `DocumentTabs.module.css` quotes binding excerpts throughout; append at the file end.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `e52dadf4` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 653 passed`, lint `0 errors, 2 warnings` — `archtest`'s colour-literal rule accepts the token usage; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, **`271 passed (5.2m)`** (+2), `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`.
  - **The parity result is the load-bearing half.** The tab strip *is* a pixel-compared region (`targeted:tab-strip:1280:minimal-light`), and this is the only change in the closing sequence that alters production rendering of one. The new rule is scoped to `[data-tab-dragging='true']`, which is never set during a capture because no drag is in flight, so the compared pixels are unchanged by construction — and `150/150 … 0 failed` is what confirms it rather than the argument.
  - **Not proved: the host.** Both clauses are proved in Chromium against the dev bridge. Nobody has dragged a tab in the packaged `just build` binary, so the native webview's own compositing of a translucent, auto-scrolling strip is unobserved — it belongs with the outstanding host walks (**T166**, **T181**, **T186**).

- [x] T178 Make editing genuinely unavailable for an `unsafe-read-only` document, per FR-FT-006 (gap, opened by T157). FR-FT-006 lists five things that "MUST be unavailable" when invalid-UTF-8 or NUL-bearing input opens tolerantly: "Editing, document formatting or lint commands, Save, Save As, and autosave". T157 covered four of them and could not cover the first, because nothing implements it. **Two independent holes, both verified directly.** (a) The editor is never made read-only: a repository-wide grep for `readOnly` across `frontend/src/ui/components/CodeEditor.tsx` and `frontend/src/ui/widgets/EditorView.tsx` returns nothing, no capability reaches Monaco's options, and `CodeEditor` has no `readOnly` prop to pass one through. A user can type into a document the backend will refuse to write, and the first refusal arrives at Save. (b) `getActionAvailability` (`frontend/src/logic/actions/actionRegistry.ts:474`) applies its capability rule only to `save` and `save-as` (`:517-530`); every `editor`-scope action — `bold`, `italic`, `heading-1..3`, the list and table actions, `paste`, and the rest at `:307-379` — is gated on `editorFocused` alone, in the registry and again in `actionDispatcher.ts:90-95`. So the formatting toolbar stays live on an unwritable document.
  - **The two must be fixed together.** Making Monaco read-only without the registry leaves the toolbar buttons enabled and silently inert, which is the defect T116 exists to remove; correcting the registry without Monaco leaves the keyboard able to edit.
  - **`unsafe-read-only` is not the only capability that must block editing.** `large-read-only` (a file above 10 MiB, FR-FT-005) is equally unwritable, and `readOnlyReason.ts:23-34` already distinguishes the two for the status surface. Read the capability, not one string — Go's own predicate is `capability != writable` (`internal/appmodel/save.go`), and `App.tsx:1265-1267` already mirrors it for the write path.
  - **Where the covering tests go.** Registry and dispatcher unavailability beside the two `T157` cases in `actionRegistry.test.ts` and `actionDispatcher.test.ts`; the Monaco option in `EditorView.integration.test.tsx`, whose `mockRuntime` already records the options `CodeEditor` passes. When it lands, widen the `// Proves: FR-FT-006 (partial …)` anchors both tests carry — they name this task by number.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17. There were three holes, not two** — the task named (a) Monaco and (b) the registry, and both are closed, but closing them did **not** disable the formatting toolbar, which is the symptom the task leads with.
  - **The third hole: the toolbar never asked the registry.** `EditorChrome`'s `ActionButton` computed `disabled` from `entry.availability.kind === 'deferred'` — the **static** registry entry — and `EditorChrome` passed no `projectedState` to `dispatchAction`. `projectedStateFor` reads `context.projectedState ?? context.projection` with **no store fallback** (`actionRegistry.ts`), so with neither supplied the capability rule could not fire on this path at all: after (a) and (b) the user still could not type, but every formatting button still rendered enabled **and still worked**. FR-FT-006 was not satisfied. This is the defect class AGENTS.md already records against `SettingsMenu` — a surface deciding availability for itself instead of reading the registry — and it is why the registry fix alone measured as done while the requirement was not met.
  - **And it was two surfaces, not one.** `EditorContextMenu` has the identical shape — static `item.availability.kind`, no projection to `dispatchAction` — so right-click → Bold, Cut and Paste stayed live on an unwritable document too. A grep for `availability.kind === 'deferred'` across `frontend/src/ui` finds nine production sites; the two that offer *editing* commands are these, and both are fixed. The remaining seven offer window, application, tab and shortcut-list actions, which FR-FT-006 does not speak to, plus `EditorChrome.tsx`'s `parityOverflowItem`, which is the `?parity-case` route T173 owns.
  - **Fixed by asking, not by re-deriving.** Each surface keeps its static check first and **ORs** in `getActionAvailability(id, { projectedState })`. Because no `modalOpen`, tab or target context is passed, that call can only fire the capability rule — the disabled set widens by exactly FR-FT-006 and nothing else, which is what let this land without touching any other action's enabled state. The same projection is passed to `dispatchAction`, so a bypassed control is refused as well as dimmed.
  - **One projection, not one per surface.** `frontend/src/logic/hooks/useEditingProjection.ts` is new and both surfaces use it. Two hand-maintained copies of the same projection is precisely the `SettingsMenu` defect — right until the registry changes and nobody remembers the duplicate exists. `EditorChrome` reaches its buttons through a module-local context rather than a fourth parameter threaded through ten `actionButtons` call sites.
  - **`copy` is deliberately exempt, and asserted.** It is `editor`-scope and mutates nothing; FR-FT-006 makes *editing* unavailable, not the clipboard, and lifting text out of a file you cannot write must keep working. `NON_MUTATING_EDITOR_ACTIONS` states the **exceptions** rather than the 16 mutations, so an `editor`-scope action added later is gated by default — a new mutation silently enabled on an unwritable document is a data defect; a new reader wrongly dimmed is visible immediately.
  - **The predicate is `capability !== 'writable'`, in all three places**, mirroring Go (`internal/appmodel/save.go`), so `large-read-only` (FR-FT-005) is covered alongside `unsafe-read-only`. Both are asserted at the registry.
  - **Red first, six tests, each watched fail for its own reason.** Registry: `bold` on an `unsafe-read-only` document returned `{kind:'available'}` where `unavailable` was required. Monaco: `options.readOnly` was `undefined` on both the read-only and writable cases. Toolbar: `Received element is not disabled` for `Bold`. Two failures were **test** defects, corrected rather than coded around — the dispatcher returns `status: 'mutated'` for an invoked editor action, not `'ran'`; and the second toolbar case passed alone but failed in sequence, because `hydrateProjection` at a non-increasing revision is a silent no-op against the shared store, so the fixture under test was still the previous one. `resetProjection()` now precedes both.
  - **Files outside the task's named list**, declared per AGENTS.md: `EditorChrome.tsx`, `EditorChrome.test.tsx`, `EditorContextMenu.tsx`, `EditorContextMenu.test.tsx`, and the new `logic/hooks/useEditingProjection.ts`. The task named only `actionRegistry`, `actionDispatcher`, `CodeEditor`/`EditorView` and their tests; neither surface hole is reachable from any of those, and closing T178 without them would have recorded FR-FT-006 as met while both a toolbar button and a context-menu item still edited an unwritable document.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `03c2275e` before the first edit, every gate exit 0 with findings parsed (`lint=0=clean=2`) — no `UNRELIABLE`. After: `just check` exit 0, `Test Suites: 80 passed`, `Tests: 647 passed` (+7), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings` — the same two pre-existing `react-refresh` notices, so no new static-analysis finding; `archtest (frontend): ok`, which is what clears the new `logic/hooks/` production file against the boundary rules. `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs`: M1–M6 all PASS. `just e2e-test` exit 0: `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150 attempted, 150 passed, 0 failed, 0 unaccounted` — run because both fixes change rendered `disabled` state on real surfaces and `just check` never runs Playwright. Exit codes were captured directly rather than read off a piped `tail`, which reports the pipe's status and not `just`'s.
  - **`useEditingProjection` has no test of its own, deliberately.** Its two production importers exercise it through the four surface tests. A dedicated test would assert the shape a test itself constructed — the self-asserting test-only symbol pattern — rather than any behaviour.
  - **Not proved: the real binary.** FR-FT-006's editing clause is proved in jsdom at three seams (registry, dispatcher, Monaco options) and at two rendered surfaces. Nobody has opened a NUL-bearing file in `just build` and tried to type into it. That belongs with the other outstanding host walks (**T166**, **T181**, **T186**), and one session can carry it.

- [x] T179 Give a document its own Reading state and restore its preview scroll, per FR-FT-032 (gap, opened by T157). FR-FT-032 requires each document to preserve "its own dirty state, Editor/Split/Preview arrangement, **Reading state**, editor and **preview scroll**, caret, selection, and editor view state across switches". T157 covered dirty state, arrangement and the scroll *base*; the two named clauses could not be covered because neither exists.
  - **Reading state has no per-document field.** `DocumentView` (`frontend/src/logic/store/appModelTypes.ts:27-34`) carries `arrangement`, `editorVisible`, `previewVisible`, `cursor`, `selection` and `scroll`, and `apperr.DocView` is the same shape on the Go side. The only "Reading" concepts in the product are the `distraction-free-reading` **window** action (`actionRegistry.ts:64,289`, rendered by `ViewMenu.tsx:296-299`) and the `defaultOpenMode: 'reading' | 'editor'` **application setting** that T119 wired. Neither is per document, so today switching tabs cannot preserve a per-document Reading state and cannot fail to. **Decide first whether the requirement means distraction-free reading or means `arrangement === 'preview'`** — if the latter, the clause is already satisfied by the arrangement clause and the requirement text should say so rather than naming a second thing; if the former, it needs a field on `DocView`, a migration-free addition to the Go projection, and a `setDocView` round trip.
  - **Preview scroll is recorded and never restored.** `useSyncedBuffer.onPreviewScrollChange` (`frontend/src/logic/hooks/useSyncedBuffer.ts:221-227`) writes `scroll.preview` through `updateDocView`, and the value survives in the projection — T157's `T157 keeps each document its own dirty state and arrangement across a switch` proves the recorded base is per document. Nothing reads it back: a grep for `scrollTop =` across `frontend/src/ui` returns nothing, so the preview element always mounts at zero. The editor half is not affected — Monaco's own `restoreViewState` carries it, which is why only the preview is named here.
  - **Restore it on activation, not on every render.** The preview remounts on every accepted revision (`EditorView.tsx:427` keys `LivePreview` on `documentId:content`), so an unconditional assignment would fight the user's scrolling on each keystroke. Apply the saved offset once per activation, the way the Monaco view state already is.
  - **Prove it with a real scroll.** The harness for this already exists: T157's case in `EditorView.integration.test.tsx` drives `fireEvent.scroll` on the `previewContent` element inside the `Preview pane` region and reads the resulting view from the `updateLocalDocView` mock, not `setDocView`. When this lands, widen that test's `// Proves: FR-FT-032 (partial …)` anchor, which names this task by number.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17. Preview scroll built; Reading state resolved as an owner-approved amendment, not in code.**
  - **Preview scroll — restored once per activation.** `LivePreview` now holds a ref on its scroll container and, in a **layout** effect (so the offset lands before paint rather than showing zero for a frame and jumping), *claims* the one restore the document is owed. The claim is `EditorView`'s: `claimPreviewScrollRestore(documentId)` returns `true` exactly once per activation and is called from inside the child's effect, so the record is never read while rendering.
  - **Three `react-hooks` rejections shaped that design, and the first was a real defect.** The hooks were first written after `EditorView`'s `if (activeBuffer === null) return null;` — conditional hooks. **Jest stayed green at 649 tests** because every one of them renders with an active buffer, so none took that branch; `just e2e-test` failed **10 cases**, every one of them `getByTestId('document-launcher')` not found. The rules-of-hooks violation broke the entire empty state — the launcher — and only the interface gate saw it. Then `Cannot access refs during render` rejected reading the ref to compute a prop (unsafe under concurrent rendering), and `set-state-in-effect` rejected the obvious `useState`-from-`useEffect` replacement. The claim-from-effect shape is what survives all three.
  - **The "once per activation" assertion was checked for discrimination, twice.** Making the restore unconditional (`restoreScrollTop={view.scroll.preview}`, and later dropping the `claimScrollRestore` guard) fails *T179 does not re-apply the saved preview scroll when the content changes* with `Expected: not 240` while the sibling still passes. Without that check the pair would prove only "restored at all", and the naive fix — which fights the user's scrolling on every keystroke, because the pane is keyed on `documentId:content` — would have passed.
  - **jsdom honours a `scrollTop` assignment.** Verified with a throwaway probe (`READBACK=120`) rather than inferred from T157's `Object.defineProperty` workaround, which exists to pin a value *before* a synthetic scroll event and not because assignment fails. That saved a prototype patch and its cleanup hazard.
  - **Reading state — amended, on the owner's decision of 2026-08-17, to mean the preview arrangement.** It is **not** built, and it must not be built inside Feature 003. Two findings settle it:
    - **The spec requires distraction-free reading to stay unavailable.** `actionRegistry.ts:289` marks `distraction-free-reading` `laterDeferred` and `ViewMenu.tsx:294` renders the row permanently `disabled` — and that is not an omission: `spec.md:1289` *requires* the View menu to mark its "deferred `Toggle Assistant` and `Distraction-free reading` rows visibly unavailable", and `spec.md:229-233` records it as an answered clarification. The row's state is inside the parity contract. Building the feature would contradict the spec in two places and move a compared row.
    - **The product's Reading concept already *is* the arrangement.** `openArrangement` (`internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle.go:421-429`) maps `OpenModeViewer` directly to `ArrangementPreview`, so `defaultOpenMode: 'reading'` produces no state separate from `arrangement`. FR-FT-032's arrangement clause therefore already carries it, and T157 proved arrangement survives a switch.
    - **Owed to the owner, not done here:** the `spec.md` FR-FT-032 text still names "Reading state" alongside arrangement as if they were two things. Per Constitution I this is an approved amendment rather than a silent reinterpretation, and the spec edit belongs to the owner — recorded here so the next audit does not re-open it as a coverage gap.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `dcfefdcc` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). The first attempt failed all three — `just check` exit 1 with 3 lint errors, `just verify` M3 FAIL, `just e2e-test` exit 1 at 258/268 — and the fix is above. After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 649 passed` (+2), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings` — the same two pre-existing findings; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `268 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`, with all ten launcher regressions gone. **The first attempt is the whole argument for running the interface gate**: `just check` was green at 649 tests over a change that had removed the application's entire empty state.

- [ ] T180 Run the new CI interface gate once and record whether the zero-tolerance parity contract holds on `ubuntu-24.04` (gap, opened by T139). T139 added an `interface gate` job to `.github/workflows/main.yml` that runs `just e2e-test` on a hosted `ubuntu-24.04` runner. It has never executed. The job was validated statically only — the YAML parses, three jobs are defined with `release-skeleton` needing both `test` and `interface`, every `just` recipe it names resolves under `just --show`, and the suite is green on the macOS development host (268 passed, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150`, 5.1 minutes). **One risk is real and unmeasured.** The parity contract compares the production page against the immutable reference at **zero tolerance**. Nothing compares a committed screenshot across hosts — both pages render in the same Chromium in the same run, so the host cancels out of most of the comparison — but a hosted Linux runner has different installed fonts and a different rasteriser from macOS, and the two pages are *not* identical inputs: they are two different DOMs whose glyph rasterisation only agrees if both resolve the same font. If `Inter` or `JetBrains Mono` resolve differently on the two sides under Linux's fallback chain, residuals appear that are host artefacts rather than drift. **Do not chase such a residual as production drift** — `docs/delivery/plan/KNOWN_ISSUES.md` and `AGENTS.md` already record that a pixel difference with no style or bounds difference is usually layerisation or rasterisation. Trigger the workflow with `workflow_dispatch`, read the `interface gate` log, and then either record that it is green (closing this task) or classify the residual with the same four-way split `evidence/ft-vs-08/phase-18/residual-attribution.md` uses, and decide whether the runner needs the fonts installed or the contract needs a host-scoped exclusion — the latter needs the owner, because it narrows a contract.
  - **A second unmeasured risk, and it is not fonts: wall-clock thresholds.** `assertShellTiming` (`frontend/e2e/helpers/shell-observation.ts:39-47`) asserts three of them per FR-FT-048's shell budget — 95% of resize samples repainting within **100 ms**, every freeze under **250 ms**, every acknowledgement under **500 ms**. These are host- and load-sensitive in a way the parity comparison is not, because both parity pages render in the same browser on the same machine and the host largely cancels out, while a millisecond budget does not cancel against anything. **Observed locally on 2026-08-17**: `T026 retains at least 20 measured resize and divider samples` failed at `0.65` against the 0.95 target during a run started while a previous Playwright suite was still draining (load average 29), and the same case passed alone on the same commit moments later. A hosted runner is shared, throttled and noisier than a development machine, so expect this before expecting a font residual — and do not classify a timing failure with the four-way *rasterisation* taxonomy, which does not describe it. If it recurs on CI, the question is whether the budget is a product requirement that a shared runner cannot fairly measure, which needs the owner for the same reason a host-scoped parity exclusion does.
  - **Also unmeasured, and cheap to read off the same run**: the wall-clock cost on a hosted runner. The job allows 30 minutes against 5.1 locally, dominated by one case (FR-FT-048 watches a live application for five continuous minutes). If the runner is slower than about 5x, that allowance needs revisiting, and the Playwright browser download is not cached.

- [ ] T181 Spot-check SC-FT-007's autosave latency on the real `just build` binary by hand, per SC-FT-007 and plan.md Decision 19 (evidence gap, opened by T121). T121 re-ran the full 100-trial protocol at HEAD, found it agrees with the 2026-08-15 distribution, and proved mechanically that the measured write path is the **same object code** the release binary links — identical Go package build IDs for `internal/appmodel` and `internal/file` under `desktop,wv2runtime.download,production` and under `native_evidence production`, no `native_evidence` build constraint in either package, no cgo file in either. What it could not do is run the protocol against the `just build` artifact, and the reason is structural rather than incidental.
  - **Two missing seams, both verified.** The stop stamp comes from the write-commit observer: `SetWriteCommitObserver` (`internal/appmodel/service.go:261`) has three call sites in the whole repository, all in `cmd/native-evidence/main_native_evidence.go:128,133`, and `internal/application/application_context_holder.go` never calls it — a release binary publishes no commit instant to anything. The start stamp is the `autosaveInputEvent` that `frontend/evidence/main.tsx` emits after its final input handler returns; the release binary embeds `frontend/dist`, which has no emitter and no trial loop.
  - **Do not close this by adding either seam.** Instrumenting the release build changes the artifact under measurement, which is precisely the defect T121 existed to remove. The only honest route is a person driving the real `.app`.
  - **What is actually owed**: open a path-backed writable fixture in `build/bin/GoMarkEdit.app` with autosave on, type one character, and observe the atomic replacement — enough trials at 1 KiB and 2 MiB to say whether the observed interval sits in the same range as the harness distribution (p50 ≈ 1.25 s, p95 ≈ 1.33 s, max ≈ 1.34 s). This is a *spot-check*, which is what `plan.md:693` asked for; it is not a second 100-trial run and must not be recorded as one. **A disagreement reopens SC-FT-007**, and the reopening is the point of doing it.
  - **The residual this measures** is the production editor's own keystroke path — Monaco's input event, the React render, the call into `appModelAdapter.updateBuffer` — plus the packaged `.app` launch context (LaunchServices, self-signing, bundle). The 200 ms working-copy synchronisation is already inside the harness interval and is not what is missing.
  - **Check the stale-instance trap first.** `open`-ing the `.app` raises an already-running process; compare `ps -eo pid,lstart` against the binary mtime before trusting one observation, and record that you did.
  - Sequence with **T166**, the other outstanding interactive host walkthrough (`Copy path` and `Reveal in file manager`) — one session of the real binary can carry both.

- [x] T182 Make the native-evidence bundle directory invisible to the source gates, per Constitution VII (tooling gap, opened by T121). `frontend/evidence/vite.config.ts` sets `outDir` to `frontend/dist-native-evidence/<scenario>/`. Every other build output in this repository — `frontend/dist/`, `playwright-report/`, `test-results/` — is named by `.gitignore`, `frontend/.prettierignore` **and** the eslint ignore list. This one is named by none of them, so the first `just check` after running any native-evidence scenario fails twice over: `frontend-format-check` reports eight unformatted minified artifacts, then `frontend-lint` reports **9,182 problems** in the Monaco worker bundle (`'self' is not defined`, `'define' is not defined`, and 9,000 more of the same shape). The findings are entirely artefacts of linting generated output; no source file is involved.
  - **Why T121 did not simply fix it.** It added the `.gitignore` entry, which is pure protection against the `git add -A` hazard AGENTS.md already warns about and demonstrably makes no gate pass. It stopped there because the remaining fix requires editing an eslint config, and "never edit an eslint config to make a gate pass" is a non-negotiable. The distinction that has to be argued — that adding a *generated output directory* to a source linter's ignore list removes no coverage, because prettier and eslint have never been intended to read build artefacts — is exactly the kind of reinterpretation the rule exists to stop being made casually, mid-task, by whoever hit the failure.
  - **What is owed**: add `dist-native-evidence/` to `frontend/.prettierignore` and to the eslint ignore list (alongside the existing `dist` entry), verify with a scenario run followed by `just check` that both gates are clean and that **no source file left either tool's scope** — the check that makes this a fix rather than a suppression is diffing the file list each tool visits before and after. **Do not** solve it by deleting the directory after every run: that is what T121 did as a stopgap and it leaves the next person to rediscover the same two failures.
  - Confirm at the same time whether `frontend/dist-native-evidence` should live under `frontend/` at all. An output directory outside the frontend package would need no ignore entry in any of the three tools, which is the smaller surface.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17 by taking the second option, which makes the non-negotiable irrelevant rather than argued around.** `.prettierignore` and the eslint config are **untouched**. `format:check` and `lint` both run as `npm --prefix frontend`, so prettier and eslint are rooted at `frontend/` and visit everything beneath it; an output directory outside that root is structurally invisible to both. The bundle now lands at repository-root `dist-native-evidence/<scenario>/`.
  - **Three lines, and only two live consumers** — smaller than the task assumed: `frontend/evidence/vite.config.ts` resolves `outDir` through `'..'`, `cmd/native-evidence/main_native_evidence.go:74` drops the `frontend` path segment, and `.gitignore`'s entry loses its prefix. A repository-wide grep for the old path finds nothing live; the remaining hits are historical copies under `.specify/bridge-snapshots/`.
  - **The defect was reproduced before it was fixed.** A stand-in worker bundle (`self.define = …; define(…)` — the shape that produced the 9,182 `'self' is not defined` findings) was written into `frontend/dist-native-evidence/autosave-1kib/assets/`. eslint's visited file list grew from **220 to 221** and prettier flagged both artifacts, exactly as described.
  - **The check the task names as the difference between a fix and a suppression came out stronger than an ignore entry could produce.** With the bundle present at the new location, `eslint . --format json` visits a list **byte-identical** to the one before any change — `diff` is empty, 220 files both times — and `prettier --check .` reports *All matched files use Prettier code style*. An ignore entry would have **shrunk** that list, and the argument would then have had to be that nothing removed was source; here nothing was removed at all.
  - **Not closed by deleting the directory after each run**, which is what T121 did as a stopgap and which leaves the next person to rediscover both failures.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `ca905478` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 655 passed`, lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `go build ./...` clean; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `271 passed (5.2m)`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed` — run because `frontend/evidence/vite.config.ts` is under `frontend/`, though it is not a rendered surface.
  - **One stated limit.** The defect was reproduced with a stand-in worker bundle rather than by running a real native-evidence scenario. The stand-in carries the same mechanism — generated JavaScript using `self` and `define` inside `frontend/`, entering both tools' scope — and the fix is structural rather than rule-based, so a real 3 MB Monaco build would exercise the identical path at much greater cost. The next person running a genuine scenario should see a clean `just check`; if they do not, this note is the place to start.

- [x] T183 Prove the CRLF, BOM, permission and mixed-ending fixture families reach disk, per SC-FT-001 (coverage gap, opened by T158). `evidence/sc-ft-001/fixture-families.md` maps all seven families. Four are proved end to end; three stop at one end, and the criterion says "100% of the … fixtures".
  - **CRLF and BOM are proved only at `EncodeDocument`** (`internal/file/codec_test.go:30`), a pure function whose output is compared in memory, and at the reader's classification (`document_reader_test.go:90,203`). Nothing joins the ends. Do **not** cite `internal/file/atomic_replace_test.go:44` for this: it moves `"before\r\n"` → `"after\r\n"` as opaque payload bytes and exercises no line-ending logic. What is owed is one test per convention that opens a uniformly-CRLF (then BOM) fixture, edits one line through `appmodel.Save`, and asserts the bytes read back off disk — including that the BOM is still the first three bytes and was not duplicated.
  - **Permission mode is proved at `file.AtomicReplace` only** (`atomic_replace_test.go:43`, the `existing target preserves bytes and mode` subtest asserting `0o640`). No `appmodel.Save`-level test asserts the mode survives. **Ownership is a different matter and must not be folded in**: `internal/file/atomic_replace.go` has no `Chown` at all, only a `chmod` to `expected.Mode` at `:174`, so ownership preservation is unimplemented. Decide whether SC-FT-001's "permission-mode" clause includes ownership before writing a test that would fail by design.
  - **`TestMixedEndingAuthorization` (`internal/appmodel/save_test.go:143`) never reads the disk** after the authorised commit — it reads the projected `LineEnding` field. Add the byte assertion; the fixture is already real.
  - **There is no single-document explicit-`Save` write-failure test.** The only end-to-end failure-plus-intact-bytes-plus-dirty assertion rides on the close-plan Save-all path (`close_plan_test.go:55`), which is a different requirement's test doing this one's work by accident.
  - When each lands, widen the corresponding row of `evidence/sc-ft-001/fixture-families.md` — the artifact is written so a row moving from "Gap" to "Proved" is a one-line edit, and leaving it stale would recreate the defect T158 removed.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17.** Five tests added in `internal/appmodel/save_test.go`; rows 2, 3, 4 and 7 of `evidence/sc-ft-001/fixture-families.md` moved from Gap to Proved, and no `Gap — encoder end only` row remains.
  - **Every one passed on arrival, so the discrimination check *is* the work.** T183 is a coverage gap, not a defect, which means five green tests are indistinguishable from five tautologies until each has been shown to fail. Each was re-run against a fixture stripped of the property it asserts, and each failed with a precise message: CRLF fixture as LF → `bytes on disk = "one\nedited\n"`; BOM fixture without the mark → `bytes on disk = 6f 6e 65 …`; `0640` fixture as `0644` → `mode after save = -rw-r--r--`; the write-failure test with no injected failure → `failed Save = {Status:committed …}`.
  - **The CRLF test is a transformation, not a round trip.** The working copy is **LF** — as the editor always holds it — and the disk must be **CRLF**. That is what makes it immune to the failure mode the ledger warns about in `atomic_replace_test.go:44`, which moves `"before\r\n"` → `"after\r\n"` as opaque payload and would pass a naive round-trip assertion while exercising no line-ending logic at all.
  - **The BOM test's real assertion is the *doubled* mark.** The reader strips the BOM and the encoder re-adds it, so the plausible defect is two marks rather than none — which a prefix check or a byte count alone would not catch. It asserts `EF BB BF` first, that the next three bytes are *not* another BOM, and that the content following is the edited text.
  - **Ownership is out of scope, decided from the criterion's own words rather than referred to the owner.** SC-FT-001 names a "**permission-mode**" fixture and Constitution V says "preserve permissions"; neither states ownership, and `internal/file/atomic_replace.go` has no `Chown` at all. An ownership assertion would therefore fail by design against a requirement nothing makes, so none was written and no task was filed for one — recording the reasoning here so the next audit does not re-open it as a gap.
  - **`TestMixedEndingAuthorization` now reads the disk.** Every assertion it made was against the *projection*, so a normalization that updated the model and wrote the old bytes would have satisfied all of them. It now also asserts no `\r` survives and the exact bytes are the edited LF text.
  - **The write-failure family is no longer proved by accident.** Both halves of the criterion's "or" — original bytes intact **and** the document visibly modified — were asserted together in exactly one place, `close_plan_test.go:55`, a Save-all across a close plan. A single explicit Save is the ordinary path and now has its own test. `file.AtomicReplace`'s six pre-commit subtests cannot substitute: they have no concept of a dirty document, so they cannot assert the second half at all.
  - **`just e2e-test` is not owed and was not run.** The change is Go tests and one markdown ledger; `git status` shows no file under `frontend/` or `e2e/`, so there is no rendered surface for the interface gate to observe.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `7c15a481` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, frontend `Tests: 653 passed` unchanged as expected — the five new tests are Go-side; `go test ./...` green across every package; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS.

- [x] T184 Close the four uncovered recent-file clauses, per SC-FT-008 (coverage gap, opened by T158). `evidence/sc-ft-008/recent-file-clauses.md` maps eleven clauses; eight have a covering test. The four that do not:
  - **The focus arm of promotion.** `file_lifecycle.go:284-301` promotes on the focus branch as well as the open branch, but `TestOpenFocusesCanonicalDuplicate` (`internal/appmodel/open_lifecycle_test.go:53`) asserts `OpenStatusFocused` and no extra tab and makes **no recents assertion at all**. Re-open an already-open file and assert it moved to the front.
  - **The display boundary.** `TestPromotionIsLatestValueTransaction` proves an explicit `List()` observes the other instance's commits at the *repository*. The seam the criterion means is `GetState` → `refreshRecentFiles` (`service.go:312`, `recent_files.go:11`). Mutate the repository out of band and assert the next `GetState` snapshot carries it.
  - **"Prune … without background polling", for recents.** `TestNoWatcherOrPollingTimerIsRegistered` (`conflict_test.go:147`) counts disk-version conflict reads through `SetConflictReadersForTesting`; it says nothing about `RecentFilesRepository.List`. Count `List` calls and assert only display and explicit choice drive them. Today the rule lives in a comment at `recent_files.go:9-10`, which is the same shape as the guard T128 found unwired.
  - **Clause K's non-empty prior order.** `failingRecentFilesRepository` fails `List` *and* `Promote`, so the "last committed order" at failure is the empty list and the assertion reduces to "no phantom entry appeared". Force a real SQLite busy failure against a populated list — `tasks.md:428,545` ask for exactly this and `grep -i busy` across `*_test.go` finds only the fake error string at `recent_files_test.go:189`.
  - Also worth deciding, not owed: clause J is proved in React only (`App.test.tsx:2627`). Nothing asserts the production composition root (`application_context_holder.go:142`) opens no document from a populated recents database.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17.** Four tests in `internal/appmodel/recent_files_test.go`; rows B, H, I and K of `evidence/sc-ft-008/recent-file-clauses.md` updated, and no `no covering test` row remains.
  - **Every test passed on arrival, so the discrimination check is the deliverable.** Three were verified by removing the property each asserts: focusing the file already at the front (focus arm), skipping the out-of-band commit (display boundary), and removing the refusing trigger (clause K) each produced a precise failure. **The first clause-K attempt was wrong and nearly accepted** — it *renamed* the trigger instead of removing it, so the trigger still fired and the test still passed. A mutation that does not actually remove the property proves nothing; the redo gave `open warning = <nil>, want a persistence warning`.
  - **Clause K uses a trigger, not lock contention, and that is a deliberate substitution.** A genuine `SQLITE_BUSY` is reachable — `isRecentSQLiteBusy` and the three-attempt retry loop in `withEntries` exist for exactly it — but it costs **about fifteen seconds** in a unit test: the busy timeout is `busyTimeoutMilliseconds = 5000` and `withEntries` retries three times. A `BEFORE UPDATE … RAISE(ABORT)` trigger on the `recent.files` row is the same thing SC-FT-008 asks about — "a failed metadata transaction" — against real SQLite, at no cost. It is also better than the obvious alternative of dropping the table, which would make `List` fail too and put the prior order back to empty, recreating the very flaw this clause was filed for.
  - **The no-polling clause is counted, not timed.** A sleep proves only that nothing has fired *yet*. `TestRecentsAreListedOnlyAtDisplayAndChoice` takes the `List` count after a display refresh and an explicit choice, asserts it is unchanged across a quiet interval, and then asserts it rises again on the next `GetState` — so the seam is shown quiet rather than dead. Its "something polls" direction is **not** mutation-verified, and that is stated here rather than implied: injecting a poller means adding production code.
  - **Clause J is still React-only and was left alone**, as the task says it is worth deciding but not owed. Nothing asserts that the production composition root (`application_context_holder.go:142`, which wires the SQLite recents repository) opens no document from a populated recents database.
  - **`just e2e-test` is not owed and was not run**: the change is Go tests and two markdown ledgers, with no file under `frontend/` or `e2e/`.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `213db113` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`, frontend `Tests: 653 passed` unchanged as expected — the four new tests are Go-side; `go test ./internal/appmodel/` green; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 all PASS.

- [x] T185 Exercise SC-FT-003's forty-document switch scale, per SC-FT-003 (coverage gap, opened by T158). The criterion reads "Across **repeated switches among 40 distinct documents**, 100% of named cases restore the correct content identity, caret, selection, scroll, and arrangement". The restoration tests use **two** documents and one round trip (`EditorView.integration.test.tsx:1343,1715`), and the guard tests use two documents and one supersession (`App.test.tsx`, T128 and T140).
  - **Every other 40 in the tree is the cap, not a switch population** — `internal/appmodel/file_lifecycle_test.go:110` `TestNewDocumentRefusesStaleOrFortyFirst`, the e2e forty-first refusal, `DocumentTabs.test.tsx:895`. The `[parity states] 40/40` line printed by `just e2e-test` is a count of visual-parity states and is an **unrelated** 40; a reader who takes it for this clause is wrong.
  - **What is owed**: open 40 distinct documents and switch repeatedly among them, asserting per document that content identity, caret, selection, editor scroll and arrangement come back as its own. Decide first whether this belongs in jsdom (fast, but the editor is mocked) or e2e (real Monaco, but 40 documents against the bridge mock) — and say which clause the chosen level cannot prove, in the anchor.
  - **Do not include preview scroll.** It is captured and never re-applied; that is **T179**, and asserting it here would fail by design.
  - Nothing currently exercises multiple concurrently pending acknowledgements or a supersession chain deeper than one; a 40-document switch storm is the natural place to add it.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17**, in two tests because the criterion spans two harnesses.
  - **The scale clause, in jsdom** (`EditorView.integration.test.tsx`, `T185 restores each of forty documents its own view across repeated switches`). Forty documents, each with a distinct arrangement, cursor, selection and editor scroll, driven through a full pass and then a revisiting order — the criterion says *repeated* switches, so each document must survive being left and returned to. **What the level cannot prove is stated in the anchor**: the editor is mocked, so Monaco's own painting of a caret and selection is not claimed; what is proved is that the application resolves each document's own view and hands it to the editor, and that no document inherits a neighbour's. Preview scroll is deliberately excluded — T179 owns it. Verified to discriminate: feeding every switch document-0's content fails with `Expected: "# document-1", Received: "# document-0"`.
  - **The zero-cross-document-installation clause at depth** (`App.test.tsx`, `T185 installs only the newest of several concurrently pending switches`). Three activations issued back to back with all three answers still in flight, resolved newest-first and then in reverse — the shape a guard comparing against "the previous request" rather than the newest generation would get wrong.
  - **That second test passed vacuously when first written, and the fix is the interesting part.** It asserted only that the superseded text was *absent*, which is trivially true when nothing is installed at all — and nothing was: `projectionRevision: 13` did not satisfy `projection.revision >= …` against `tabSetSnapshot()`'s `revision: 12`, and `documentRevision: 1` did not match its `contentRevision: 5`, so **every** acknowledgement was rejected on the projection check and the generation logic was never reached. Adding the positive `toHaveTextContent('winning source')` is what exposed it. **A negative-only assertion about installation can always be satisfied by installing nothing.**
  - **Correcting it sharpened the test.** All three switches now target `document-1`, which the projection confirms, at matching revisions — so identity, projection revision and content revision agree for all three and **generation is the only thing left to tell them apart**. A guard that dropped losers because their document did not match would otherwise pass a weaker test while ignoring generation entirely.
  - **The 40 in this task is a switch population, not the cap**, and nothing else in the tree is: `file_lifecycle_test.go:110`, the e2e forty-first refusal and `DocumentTabs.test.tsx:895` are all the 40-document *limit*, and `[parity states] 40/40` counts visual-parity states. None of them evidences this clause.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `93a253e9` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 655 passed` (+2), lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, `271 passed (5.1m)`, `[parity states] 40/40`, `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed` — run because both changed files live under `frontend/`, even though neither is a rendered surface.

It is a genuine host resolution; it is also the least-guarded observation in the tree.

- [x] T187 Decide which reading of SC-FT-013 governs, per Constitution I (contradicts, opened by T158). **Owner decision required — this is a specification question, not a coverage gap, and must not be resolved in code.** SC-FT-013 says the criterion is "proven by every behavioural assertion in the end-to-end suites passing at all three widths and all six palettes". Two readings: **(A)** every behavioural assertion executes at each of three widths and each of six palettes; **(B)** every behavioural assertion passes, and the suites between them cover three widths and six palettes. The suite satisfies B and does not satisfy A, and the shortfall is structural — see `evidence/sc-ft-013/e2e-behaviour-coverage.md` for the file-by-file map.
  - **Under A the gap is large, not incidental.** The full cross-product runs in `window-shell.test.ts`'s T026 matrix (18 tests) and `editor-stage.test.ts` (108, with `Auto` as a third mode), plus one `appearance.test.ts` test. Everything else runs once, at 1280, in material/light — including **all five `interactive-states.test.ts` tests, which are the file the criterion's own word "focus" points at**, all four `launcher-binding` tests, and all 20 `real-files-and-tabs` tests. `narrow-width.test.ts` never runs at 1280. No pixel-compared key runs at 768; `TargetedEntry.width` is typed `1280 | 375`.
  - **Recommended default: amend the criterion to B**, and have it name the subset that carries the cross-product rather than implying the whole suite does. Expanding every behavioural suite to 18 cells would multiply a 5.1-minute gate by roughly six for coverage that is largely redundant — the palettes are token swaps and the assertions that actually vary by width already loop.
  - **Two things to fix whichever way it goes.** The criterion names a "token gate across all six palettes" as end-to-end evidence; that gate is **Jest** (`frontend/src/ui/styles/tokens.test.ts`), run by `just check`, and `just e2e-test` cannot evidence it. And the negative clause is about a quarter proved: `offline-and-controls.test.ts:194` genuinely asserts Assistant deferral (registry availability plus a 0 px grid track) at one width and palette, workspace is pinned only as sizing/absence, and **custom native frame and deferred rich-rendering have no assertion anywhere** — the wording survives as a prose comment at `real-files-parity.test.ts:90-95`.
  - **Do not close this by editing the counters into the criterion.** `[parity states] 40/40` is one assertion per state at one palette and one width; `[parity accounting] 150/150` is 50 keys × 3 repetitions. Neither measures width or palette breadth, and citing them as if they did is the overstatement this whole strand of work exists to remove.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17 on the owner's decision: Reading B governs**, recorded as an approved amendment to SC-FT-013 in `spec.md` rather than resolved in code. The criterion now says every behavioural assertion passes and the suites *between them* cover three widths and six palettes, and it names the subset that actually carries the cross-product — `window-shell.test.ts`'s T026 matrix, `editor-stage.test.ts`, and one `appearance.test.ts` case — so a later reader cannot mistake the whole suite for the matrix.
  - **The counters were kept out of the criterion**, per the instruction above. The amendment cites the structural shortfall (the five `interactive-states.test.ts` cases, the four `launcher-binding` cases, the 20 `real-files-and-tabs` cases, `narrow-width.test.ts`, and `TargetedEntry.width` being typed `1280 | 375`) as the *reason* B governs, not as evidence that A is met.
  - **Both side errors were corrected in the same amendment.** The "token gate across all six palettes" is named as Jest (`src/ui/styles/tokens.test.ts`, run by `just check`), so nobody cites `just e2e-test` as its evidence again; and the negative clause's real coverage is stated — Assistant deferral asserted, workspace pinned only as sizing and absence, custom native frame and deferred rich-rendering asserted **nowhere**.
  - **Filed: T189** for that last gap. Naming it in the criterion as though it were covered is exactly the false record Constitution II forbids, so it leaves as a task rather than as prose.

- [x] T188 Make "one activation failure, one report" enforceable, per FR-FT-031 and the classified error and remediation contract (coverage gap, opened by T170). Two paths report a refused activation and they must never both fire for the same click: `DocumentTabs.activateDocument` (`:330`) owns the tab-strip funnel, and `onRemediate`'s `activate-document` arm (`App.tsx`) owns the `Retry` control, which the funnel never sees. `App.onActivateDocument` deliberately reports **only** the outgoing-flush refusal and returns a backend refusal unreported, precisely so the funnel is not duplicated.
  - **Nothing enforces it.** T170 verified the invariant by reading `DocumentTabs.runActivation:294` and `activateDocument:330` and by the incident recorded at `notificationsSlice.ts:180-183`; no test fails if someone later "simplifies" by making `onActivateDocument` self-report. The failure mode is quiet and familiar: one click, `count: 2`, and the Retry control disappearing behind a `×2` that reads like the dedup contract working.
  - **A partial test would be worse than none**, which is why T170 filed this rather than writing one. Asserting single-reporting inside `DocumentTabs.test.tsx` proves nothing about the App side, because that suite supplies its own `onActivateDocument` stub; asserting it in `App.test.tsx` proves nothing about the strip, because that suite stubs `AppShell`. Either alone is an anchor over a claim its body does not make.
  - **What is owed**: one case where App's real `onActivateDocument` is wired to the real `DocumentTabs`, a backend activation refusal is answered once, and `store.getState().notifications.items` is asserted to be length 1 with `count === 1`. Decide first whether that belongs in `EditorView.integration.test.tsx` (which already renders the strip through `EditorChrome`) or in a Playwright case against the mock bridge — and say in the anchor which half the chosen level cannot see.
  - **Closed 2026-08-17 at the Playwright level**, as `T188 reports a refused activation exactly once` in `frontend/e2e/real-files-and-tabs.test.ts`.
  - **Every jsdom route was rejected for the same reason, and it is worth recording.** `EditorView.integration.test.tsx` renders the real strip but mocks `logic/adapter` with a narrow object — no `closePlanAdapter`, `documentWriteAdapter`, `documentConflictAdapter` or `nativeLifecycleAdapter` — so rendering `<App />` there means extending it into a second copy of `App.test.tsx`'s ~100-line adapter mock. `App.test.tsx` stubs **both** `AppShell` and `EditorView`, so unstubbing one is not enough. A new file duplicates both mock sets. Each option makes a third hand-maintained double of the same seam, which is the defect class T176 had just removed. Playwright needs no double at all: the shipped App, the shipped `AppShell`, the shipped strip and the shipped handler.
  - **The refusal is seeded, not raced.** `?refuseActivate` makes the dev bridge's `ActivateDocument` answer the `conflict` stale-tab-set row unconditionally, following the existing `refuseReveal` and `refuseSave` seeds. A revision race would have made the refusal probable rather than deterministic, and the property under test is "exactly one report", which needs the refusal to be certain. The seed **adds** fidelity — the real backend refuses activations and the mock previously could not be made to — so it is a fixture seed under FR-FT-054, not a component substitution.
  - **The assertion is the absence of `×2`.** A second report deduplicates onto the first and the contract renders the count, so after a single click `×2` is exactly the signature of the defect. **Verified by injecting it**: adding `reportClassifiedError` to `onActivateDocument`'s backend-result path made the case fail with `"The document could not be activated. ×2The tab set changed before this operation completed.RetryDismiss"` — the Retry control stranded behind the count, which is what `notificationsSlice.ts:180-183` describes. `App.tsx` was then restored and diffed byte-for-byte against its backup.
  - **What this level cannot see**, per the task's own instruction: the Go side. The seed forces the row, so this proves the frontend reports it once — not that Go classifies a stale tab set correctly, which `internal/appmodel`'s tests own. It also does not cover the `Retry` control's own path, which is T170's case in `App.test.tsx`.
  - **Gates:** baseline re-captured at `a2dee00a` before the first edit, reliable (`lint=0=clean=2`). After: `just check` exit 0, `Tests: 653 passed`, lint `0 errors, 2 warnings`; `just verify 003-real-files-and-tabs` M1–M6 PASS; `just e2e-test` exit 0, **`269 passed (5.2m)`** — the count moving from 268 is itself part of the evidence, since a new browser case is the whole deliverable — with `[parity states] 40/40` and `[parity accounting] 150/150, 0 failed`.

- [ ] T189 Assert SC-FT-013's negative clause for the custom native frame and deferred rich rendering, per SC-FT-013 and Constitution II (coverage gap, opened by T187). SC-FT-013 requires that "zero populated workspace, Assistant/provider, custom native-frame, or deferred rich-rendering surface appears in order to manufacture parity". Four surfaces, and the clause is about a quarter proved.
  - **What is covered.** Assistant/provider is genuinely asserted at `offline-and-controls.test.ts:194` — registry availability plus a 0 px grid track — though at one width and one palette only. Populated workspace is pinned as sizing and absence, which is weaker than the clause but is an assertion.
  - **What is not covered at all.** Custom native frame and deferred rich rendering have **no assertion anywhere in the repository**. The wording survives only as a prose comment at `real-files-parity.test.ts:90-95`, and a comment is not a test. T187's amendment states this in `spec.md` rather than concealing it, but stating a gap does not close it.
  - **Decide the level first.** Both surfaces are absences, and an absence is cheap to assert falsely — `expect(locator).toHaveCount(0)` passes just as well when the selector is wrong as when the surface is genuinely gone. Whatever is written must fail when the surface is *present*: seed or inject the surface, watch the assertion go red, then remove it. Without that step this task produces exactly the false record it exists to remove.
  - **Do not fold this into the parity accounting counters.** `[parity states]` and `[parity accounting]` measure key coverage, not surface absence, and citing them here would repeat the overstatement T187 was filed to correct.
